===Original===
To my 

 unnamed 

 little collaborator

===Chinese===
献给我的

无名的

年幼的合作者

===Original===
> Wisdom is the principal thing; therefore get wisdom: and with all thy getting get understanding.
> — Proverbs 4:7

===Chinese===
> 智慧为首，所以要得智慧，在你一切所得之内必得聪明。
> ——《箴言》4:7

## I / 一

===Original===
The four children were lying on the grass.

“… and the Prince went further and further into the forest,” said the elder girl, “till he came to a beautiful glade﻿—a glade, you know, is a place in the forest that is open and green and lovely. And there he saw a lady, a beautiful lady, in a long white dress that hung down to her ankles, with a golden belt and a golden crown. She was lying on the sward﻿—a sward, you know, is grass as smooth as velvet, just like green velvet﻿—and the Prince saw the marks of travel on her garments. The bottom of the lovely silk dress was all dirty﻿—”

“Wondrous Fair, if you don’t mind you’ll make that sheet dirty, too,” said Pin.

“Shut up, will you!” answered her sister who, carried away by her narrative, had approached her boots to some linen that was bleaching.

“Yes, but you know Sarah’ll be awfly cross if she has to wash it again,” said Pin, who was practical.
===Chinese===
四个孩子躺在草地上。

「……王子往森林深处越走越远，」大女孩说道，「最后来到一片美丽的林间空地——空地嘛，你知道，就是森林里一块又开阔、又翠绿、又可爱的地方。在那里他看见一位女子，一位美丽的女子，穿着一条垂到脚踝的长白裙，束着金腰带，戴着金王冠。她正躺在草茵上——草茵嘛，你知道，就是平整得像天鹅绒一样的草，就像绿色的天鹅绒——王子看见她衣裙上留着旅途风尘的痕迹。那条可爱的绸裙，裙摆全弄脏了——」

「奇妙佳人（Wondrous Fair），你要是不当心，也会把那条床单弄脏的。」萍（Pin）说。

「闭嘴，行不行！」姐姐回嘴道，她讲故事讲得入了迷，两只靴子已经蹭到一旁漂晒着的亚麻布上了。

「是呀，可你知道的，要是再洗一遍，萨拉（Sarah）会气得不得了的。」讲求实际的萍说。

===Original===
“You’ll put me out altogether,” cried Laura angrily. “Well, as I said, the edge of her robe was all muddy﻿—no, I don’t think I will say that; it sounds prettier if it’s clean. So it hung in long, straight beautiful folds to her ankles, and the Prince saw two little feet in golden sandals peeping out from under the hem of the silken gown, and﻿—”

“But what about the marks of travel?” asked Leppie.

“Donkey! haven’t I said they weren’t there? If I say they weren’t, then they weren’t. She hadn’t travelled at all.”

“Oh, parrakeets!” cried little Frank.

Four pairs of eyes went up to the bright green flock that was passing over the garden.
===Chinese===
「你们这下把我的兴致全搅没了。」劳拉（Laura）生气地喊道。「好吧，照我方才说的，她裙子的裙边全是泥——不对，我不想这么讲了；干干净净的听起来更美。于是裙子带着又长、又直、又美的褶裥垂到脚踝，王子看见两只穿金色凉鞋的小脚从绸裙的裙裾底下露出来，还有——」

「那旅途风尘的痕迹呢？」莱皮（Leppie）问。

「蠢驴！我不是说了没有痕迹吗？我说没有，就没有。她压根儿就没赶过路。」

「噢，长尾鹦鹉！」小弗兰克（Frank）喊道。

四双眼睛一齐抬起，望向掠过花园上空的那群亮绿色的鸟儿。

===Original===
“Now you’ve all interrupted, and I shan’t tell any more,” said Laura in a proud voice.

“Oh, yes, please do, Wondrous Fair! Tell what happened next,” begged Pin and Leppie.

“No, not another word. You can only think of sheets and parrakeets.”

“Please, Wondrous Fair,” begged little Frank.

“No, I can’t now. Another thing: I don’t mind if you call me Laura today, as it’s the last day.”
===Chinese===
「这下你们全都打了岔，我再也不讲了。」劳拉带着傲气说。

「噢，讲吧，求求你，奇妙佳人！讲讲后来怎么样了。」萍和莱皮央求道。

「不行，一个字也不讲了。你们满脑子只装着床单和长尾鹦鹉。」

「求求你，奇妙佳人。」小弗兰克央求道。

「不行，这会儿讲不成了。还有一件事：今天你们就叫我劳拉好了，我不介意，反正是在家的最后一天了。」

===Original===
She lay back on the grass, her hands clasped under her head. A voice was heard, loud, imperative.

“Laura, I want you. Come here.”

“That’s mother calling,” said Pin.

Laura kicked her heels. The two little boys laughed approval.

“Go on, Laura,” coaxed Pin. “Mother’ll be angry. I’ll come, too.”

Laura raised herself with a grumble. “It’s to try on that horrid dress.”
===Chinese===
她躺回草地上，双手交叠着枕在脑后。一个声音传来，响亮而急切，不容分说。

「劳拉，我要你过来。到这儿来。」

「是妈妈在叫。」萍说。

劳拉蹬了蹬脚后跟。两个小男孩笑着叫好。

「去吧，劳拉。」萍哄着她。「妈妈要生气了。我也一块儿去。」

劳拉一边嘟囔一边撑起身子。「准是又要试那件讨厌的裙子了。」

===Original===
In very fact Mother was standing, already somewhat impatient, with the dress in her hand. Laura wriggled out of the one she had on, and stood stiffly and ungraciously, with her arms held like pokers from her sides, while Mother on her knees arranged the length.

“Don’t put on a face like that, miss!” she said sharply on seeing Laura’s air. “Do you think I’m making it for my own pleasure?” She had sewn at it all day, and was hot and tired.

“It’s too short,” said Laura, looking down.

“It’s nothing of the kind,” said Mother, with her mouth full of pins.

“It is, it’s much too short.”

Mother gave her a slight shake. “Don’t you contradict *me*! Do you want to tell me I don’t know what length you’re to wear your dresses?”
===Chinese===
果不其然，妈妈正拿着那条裙子站在那里，已经有些不耐烦了。劳拉扭着身子脱下身上那条，直挺挺、没好气地站着，两条胳膊像两根火钳似的直直撑在身侧，妈妈跪在地上替她比量裙长。

「别给我摆那副脸子，小丫头！」看到劳拉的神气，她厉声说道。「你以为我做这条裙子是图自己痛快？」她为它缝了一整天，又热又乏。

「太短了。」劳拉低头说。

「根本没那回事。」妈妈说，嘴里还咬满了别针。

「就是太短了，太短了。」

妈妈轻轻摇了摇她。「不许你跟我顶嘴！你是想说，你该穿多长的裙子，我倒不知道了？」

===Original===
“I won’t wear it at all if you don’t make it longer,” said Laura defiantly.

Pin’s chubby, featureless little face lengthened with apprehension.

“Do let her have it just a tiny bit longer, mother dear, dear!” she pleaded.

“Now, Pin, what have you got to do with it I’d like to know!” said Mother, on the verge of losing her temper over the back folds, which *would* not hang.

“I’m going to school tomorrow, and it’s a shame,” said Laura in the low, passionate tone that never failed to exasperate Mother, so different was it from her own hearty fashion of venting displeasure. Pin began to sniff, in sheer nervous anxiety.

“Very well then, I won’t do another stitch to it!” and Mother, now angry in earnest, got up and bounced out of the room.
===Chinese===
「你要是不把它加长，我就干脆不穿了。」劳拉倔强地说。

萍那张胖乎乎、眉眼还没长开的小脸，愁得拉长了。

「就给她放长那么一点点吧，好妈妈，亲爱的好妈妈！」她哀求道。

「我说萍，我倒要问问，这事跟你有什么相干！」妈妈说，她正为后身那几道偏偏不肯服帖的褶子窝着火，眼看就要按捺不住了。

「我明天就要上学去了，这太不公道了。」劳拉说，声音低沉而激愤——这种腔调从不落空，回回都能把妈妈惹火，因为它跟妈妈自己那套痛痛快快发泄不痛快的路数截然两样。萍纯粹是急得心慌，抽起鼻子来。

「那好，我一针也不会再缝它了！」妈妈这回是真动了气，站起身，砰的一声摔门出去了。

===Original===
“Laura, how can you?” said Pin, dissolving. “It’s only you who make her so cross.”

“I don’t care,” said Laura rebelliously, though she was not far off tears herself. “It *is* a shame. All the other girls will have dresses down to the tops of their boots, and they’ll laugh at me, and call me a baby;” and touched by the thought of what lay before her, she, too, began to sniffle. She did not fail, however, to roll the dress up and to throw it unto a corner of the room. She also kicked the ewer, which fell over and flooded the floor. Pin cried more loudly, and ran to fetch Sarah.

Laura returned to the garden. The two little boys came up to her; but she waved them back.

“Let me alone, children. I want to think.”
===Chinese===
「劳拉，你怎么能这样？」萍说着，整个人都要哭化了。「全是你，才惹得她生这么大的气。」

「我才不在乎呢。」劳拉带着反叛的口气说，虽然她自己离掉泪也不远了。「这就是不公道。别的女孩的裙子都垂到靴口，她们会笑话我，管我叫小娃娃；」想到摆在面前的处境，她心里一酸，也抽抽搭搭起来。不过她还是没忘了把那条裙子卷起来，扔到房间的一个角落里去。她又踢了一脚水罐，水罐翻倒，流了满地的水。萍哭得更响了，跑去找萨拉。

劳拉回到花园。两个小男孩朝她凑上来，却被她挥手赶了回去。

「别来烦我，孩子们。我要想想事情。」

===Original===
She stood in a becoming attitude by the garden-gate, her brothers hovering in the background. Then Mother called once more.

“Laura, where are you?”

“Here, mother. What is it?”

“Did you knock this jug over or did Pin?”
===Chinese===
她姿态可人地倚在花园门口，两个弟弟在背后不远不近地转悠。这时妈妈又喊了。

「劳拉，你在哪儿？」

「在这儿，妈妈。什么事？」

「水罐是你弄倒的，还是萍？」

===Original===
“I did, mother.”

“Did you do it on purpose?”

“Yes.”

“Come here to me.”
===Chinese===
「是我，妈妈。」

「你是故意的吗？」

「是。」

「到我这边来。」

===Original===
She went, with lagging steps. But Mother’s anger had passed: she was at work on the dress again, and by squinting her eyes Laura could see that a piece was being added to the skirt. She was penitent at once; and when Mother in a sorry voice said: “I’m ashamed of you, Laura. And on your last day, too,” her throat grew narrow.

“I didn’t mean it, mother.”

“If only you would ask properly for things, you would get them.”

Laura knew this; knew indeed that, did she coax, Mother could refuse her nothing. But coaxing came hard to her; something within her forbade it. Sarah called her “high-stomached,” to the delight of the other children and her own indignation; she had explained to them again and again what Sarah really meant.
===Chinese===
她拖着脚步走了过去。可妈妈的火气已经消了：她又在缝那条裙子了，劳拉眯起眼睛斜着瞄，看得出裙身正在接一段料子。她顿时悔了；当妈妈用难过的声气说「我替你害臊，劳拉。还偏偏是在你在家最后一天」，她的喉咙一下哽住了。

「我不是故意的，妈妈。」

「你要是肯规规矩矩地开口要东西，就能得到。」

这层劳拉明白；她确实明白，只要她肯软声软气地央求，妈妈什么也不会回绝她。可撒娇央求对她说来太难了；她心里有个什么东西不许她这样做。萨拉说她「心高气傲」（high-stomached），弟妹们听了乐不可支，她自己却气恼得很；她一遍又一遍向他们解释，萨拉这话到底是什么意思。

===Original===
On leaving the house she went straight to the flowerbeds: she would give Mother, who liked flowers very well but had no time to gather them, a bouquet the size of a cabbage. Pin and the boys were summoned to help her, and when their hands were full, Laura led the way to a secluded part of the garden on the farther side of the detached brick kitchen. In this strip, which was filled with greenery, little sun fell: two thick fir trees and a monstrous blue-gum stood there; high bushes screened the fence; jessamine climbed the wall of the house and encircled the bedroom windows; and on the damp and shady ground only violets grew. Yet, with the love children bear to the limited and compact, the four had chosen their own little plots here rather than in the big garden at the back of the house; and many were the times they had all begun anew to dig and to rake. But if Laura’s energy did not fizzle out as quickly as usual﻿—she was the model for the rest﻿—Mother was sure to discover that it was too cramped and dark for them in there, and send Sarah to drive them off.
===Chinese===
一走出屋子，她就直奔花坛：她要送给妈妈一束花，一束卷心菜那么大的花——妈妈很爱花，却没工夫去采。萍和两个男孩被唤来帮忙，等大家手里都捧满了，劳拉领头，走向花园里一处僻静的所在，在主屋之外单起的那座砖砌厨房的另一头。这一条地界绿意塞得满满的，很少有阳光照进来：两棵粗壮的冷杉和一株巨大的蓝桉树立在那里；高高的灌木遮住了栅栏；素馨花攀上屋墙，绕着卧室的窗子；潮湿背阴的地面上，只长紫罗兰。然而，出于孩子们对又小又紧凑之物所怀的那份偏爱，四个人宁肯在这里、而不去屋后的大花园里，各占一块自己的小地盘；他们不知多少回重新动手，又挖又耙。只是，就算劳拉的热乎劲没有像平常那样三两下就烟消云散——她是其余几个的榜样——妈妈也准会发现那里对他们来说又挤又暗，打发萨拉来把他们轰走。

===Original===
Here, safely screened from sight, Laura sat on a bench and made up her bouquet. When it was finished﻿—red and white in the centre with a darker border, the whole surrounded by a ring of violet leaves﻿—she looked about for something to tie it up with. Sarah, applied to, was busy ironing, and had no string in the kitchen, so Pin ran to get a reel of cotton. But while she was away Laura had an idea. Bidding Leppie hold the flowers tight in both his sticky little hands, she climbed in at her bedroom window, or rather, by lying on the sill with her legs waving in the air, she managed to grab, without losing her balance, a pair of scissors from the chest of drawers. With these between her teeth she emerged, to the excited interest of the boys who watched her open-mouthed.
===Chinese===
在这里，遮得严严实实不怕人瞧见，劳拉坐在一条长凳上扎她的花束。花束扎好了——中间红白相间，外缘围一圈颜色深些的花，整束再环上一圈紫罗兰叶子——她四下里找东西来捆它。去问萨拉，萨拉正忙着熨衣，厨房里又没有绳子，萍便跑去取一只线轴。可趁她走开的当口，劳拉有了个主意。她让莱皮用两只黏糊糊的小手把花攥紧，自己从卧室的窗户往里爬；说得更确切些，她趴在窗台上，两条腿在半空乱蹬，居然没失去平衡，从五斗柜上够下了一把剪刀。她用牙齿咬着剪刀钻了出来，两个弟弟看得张大了嘴，兴奋得不得了。

===Original===
Laura had dark curls, Pin fair, and both wore them flapping at their backs, the only difference being that Laura, who was now twelve years old, had for the past year been allowed to bind hers together with a ribbon, while Pin’s bobbed as they chose. Every morning early, Mother brushed and twisted, with a kind of grim pride, these silky ringlets round her finger. Although the five odd minutes the curling occupied were durance vile to Laura, the child was proud of her hair in her own way; and when in the street she heard someone say: “Look﻿—what pretty curls!” she would give her head a toss and send them all a-rippling. In addition to this, there was a crowning glory connected with them: one hot December morning, when they had been tangled and Mother had kept her standing too long, she had fainted, pulling the whole dressing-table down about her ears; and ever since, she had been marked off in some mysterious fashion from the other children. Mother would not let her go out at midday in summer: Sarah would say: “Let that be, can’t you!” did she try to lift something that was too heavy for her; and the younger children were to be quelled by a threat to faint on the spot, if they did not do as she wished. “Laura’s faint” had become a byword in the family; and Laura herself held it for so important a fact in her life that she had more than once begun a friendship with the words: “Have you ever fainted? I have.”
===Chinese===
劳拉长一头深色的鬈发，萍一头金发，两人都让鬈发披在背后拍打着；唯一的不同是，如今已经十二岁的劳拉，从去年起获准用一根缎带把头发束在一起，而萍的鬈发还是爱怎么颠就怎么颠。每天一清早，妈妈便带着一种冷峻的得意，把这些丝一般滑的发卷绕在手指上梳拢捻卷。卷头发那短短的五分钟，对劳拉来说虽是活受罪的拘禁，这孩子却自有她一头秀发的骄傲；走在街上听见有谁说『看——多漂亮的鬈发！』，她就会把头一甩，让满头发卷荡起一片涟漪。除此之外，这头鬈发还连着一桩无上的荣光：一个炎热的十二月清晨，头发打了结，妈妈又让她站了太久，她晕了过去，把整张梳妆台都扯翻在自己身上；打那以后，她就以某种神秘的方式与其余孩子区别开了。夏天，妈妈不许她正午出门；她要是去提对她来说太重的东西，萨拉就会说：『放下，行不行！』；弟弟妹妹们若不顺着她，一句当场晕倒的威胁就能把他们镇住。『劳拉晕倒』成了家里的一句口头禅；劳拉自己也把这件事当作平生头等大事，不止一次用这样一句话开启一段交情：『你晕倒过吗？我晕倒过。』

===Original===
From among these long, glossy curls, she now cut one of the longest and most spiral, cut it off close to the root, and with it bound the flowers together. Mother should see that she did know how to give up something she cared for, and was not as selfish as she was usually supposed to be.

“Oh‑h‑h!” said both little boys in a breath, then doubled up in noisy mirth. Laura was constantly doing something to set their young blood in amazement: they looked upon her as the personification of all that was startling and unexpected. But Pin, returning with the reel of thread, opened her eyes in a different way.

“Oh, Laura﻿ ﻿… !” she began, tearful at once.

“Now, res’vor!” retorted Laura scornfully﻿—“res’vor” was Sarah’s name for Pin, on account of her perpetual wateriness. “Be a crybaby, do.” But she was not damped, she was lost in the pleasure of self-sacrifice.

Pin looked after her as she danced off, then moved submissively in her wake to be near at hand should intercession be needed. Laura was so unsuspecting, and Mother would be so cross. In her dim, childish way Pin longed to see these, her two nearest, at peace; she understood them both so well, and they had little or no understanding for each other. So she crept to the house at her sister’s heels.
===Chinese===
就在这一头长长的、油亮的鬈发里，她此刻剪下一绺最长最卷的，齐根剪断，拿它把花束捆了起来。妈妈该看看，她并非不懂得舍弃自己心爱的东西，并不像平日大家认定的那样自私。

「噢——！」两个小男孩异口同声地惊呼，随即笑得前仰后合。劳拉三天两头总要干出点让他们那颗年幼的心惊诧不已的事来：他们把她奉为一切骇人听闻、出人意表之事的化身。可是抱着线轴回来的萍，却是以另一种方式睁大了眼睛。

「噢，劳拉……！」她刚起了个头，眼泪马上就涌了上来。

「得了，『雷索沃』！」劳拉轻蔑地回敬道——『雷索沃』（res’vor）是萨拉给萍起的诨名，把 reservoir（水库）念走了样，因为她整天眼泪汪汪。「爱哭就哭个够吧。」可她并没有被浇灭兴致，她正沉醉在自我牺牲的欢愉里。

萍望着姐姐蹦蹦跳跳地走远，随后乖乖地跟在她身后，好在需要有人说情的时候随时搭得上手。劳拉这么没有防备，妈妈又要发那么大的火。萍以她懵懵懂懂的孩童心思，一心盼着这最亲最近的两个人能和睦相处；他们两个她都摸得透透的，他们彼此却几乎互不理解。于是她跟在姐姐脚后，悄悄蹭回屋里去。

===Original===
Laura did not go indoors; hiding against the wall of the flagged verandah, she threw her bouquet in at the window, meaning it to fall on Mother’s lap.

But Mother had dropped her needle, and was just lifting her face, flushed with stooping, when the flowers hit her a thwack on the head. She groped again, impatiently, to find what had struck her, recognised the peace-offering, and thought of the surprise cake that was to go into Laura’s box on the morrow. Then she saw the curl, and her face darkened. Was there ever such a tiresome child? What in all the world would she do next?

“Laura, come here, directly!”
===Chinese===
劳拉没有进屋；她贴着铺石游廊的墙藏好，把花束从窗口扔了进去，想让它落在妈妈膝上。

可妈妈恰好掉了针，正抬起俯得通红的脸，花束啪的一声正打在她头上。她又是不耐烦地四下摸索，想弄清是什么打的，认出了这份赔罪的心意，想起明天要放进劳拉箱子里的那只惊喜蛋糕。随后她看见了那绺头发，脸沉了下来。天下可曾有过这样叫人头疼的孩子？她接下来到底还会干出什么事来？

「劳拉，马上给我过来！」

===Original===
Laura had moved away; she was not expecting recognition. If Mother were pleased she would call Pin to put the flowers in water for her, and that would be the end of it. The idea of a word of thanks would have made Laura feel uncomfortable. Now, however, at the tone of Mother’s voice, her mouth set stubbornly. She went indoors as bidden, but was already up in arms again.

“You’re a very naughty girl indeed!” began Mother as soon she appeared. “How dare you cut off your hair? Upon my word, if it weren’t your last night I’d send you to bed without any supper!”﻿—an unheard-of threat on the part of Mother, who punished her children in any way but that of denying them their food. “It’s a very good thing you’re leaving home tomorrow, for you’d soon be setting the others at defiance, too, and I should have four naughty children on my hands instead of one. But I’d be ashamed to go to school such a fright if I were you. Turn round at once and let me see you!”

Laura turned, with a sinking heart. Pin cried softly in a corner.

“She thought it would please you, mother,” she sobbed.

“I *will* not have you interfering, Pin, when I’m speaking to Laura. She’s old enough by now to know what I like and what I don’t,” said Mother, who was vexed at the thought of the child going among strangers thus disfigured. “And now get away, and don’t let me see you again. You’re a perfect sight.”
===Chinese===
劳拉已经走开了；她本来就没指望得到夸奖。妈妈若是高兴，自会喊萍替她把花插到水里去，事情也就到此为止了。真要有人对她说上一句谢谢，劳拉反倒会浑身不自在。然而此刻，听妈妈话音里的那股气，她的嘴倔强地绷紧了。她依言进了屋，心里却又一次摆开了对抗的架势。

「你可真是个淘气透顶的女孩！」她一露面，妈妈就开了腔。「你怎么敢把头发剪了？说真的，要不是你在家最后一晚，我就罚你不吃晚饭就上床睡觉！」——这话出自妈妈之口，可是闻所未闻的狠话，她惩罚孩子什么法子都使得出来，唯独不会不给孩子饭吃。「幸亏你明天就离家，不然用不了多久，你也会带得其余几个不服管教，我手里就要有四个淘气孩子，不是一个了。不过要是我，把自己弄成这副吓人的模样去上学，可真臊得慌。马上转过去，让我瞧瞧你！」

劳拉转过身去，心头一沉。萍在角落里轻声哭。

「她想着这样能让您高兴，妈妈。」她抽抽噎噎地说。

「我正跟劳拉说话呢，萍，就是不许你插嘴。她如今这个年纪，早该知道我喜欢什么、不喜欢什么了。」妈妈说，一想到孩子这副破了相的模样就要走到陌生人中间去，她就有气。「这就给我走开，别让我再看见你。你这模样简直没法看。」

===Original===
“Oh, Laura, you do look funny!” said Leppie and Frank in weak chorus, as she passed them in the passage.

“Well, you ’ave made a guy of yourself this time, Miss Laura, and no mistake!” said Sarah, who had heard the above.

Laura went into her own room and locked the door, a thing Mother did not allow. Then she threw herself on the bed and cried. Mother had not understood in the least; and she had made herself a sight into the bargain. She refused to open the door, though one after another rattled the handle, and Sarah threatened to turn the hose in at the window. So they left her alone, and she spent the evening in watery dudgeon on her pillow. But before she undressed for the night she stealthily made a chink and took in the slice of cake Pin had left on the doormat. Her natural buoyancy of spirit was beginning to reassert itself. By brushing her hair well to one side she could cover up the gap, she found; and after all, there was something rather pleasant in knowing that you were misunderstood. It made you feel different from everyone else.

Mother﻿—sewing hard after even the busy Sarah had retired﻿—Mother smiled a stern little smile of amusement to herself; and before locking up for the night put the dark curl safely away.
===Chinese===
「噢，劳拉，你这模样可真逗！」她从过道里经过时，莱皮和弗兰克有气无力地齐声说。

「嗬，这一回你可真把自己弄成个活宝了，劳拉小姐，半点不假！」听到了上面这番话的萨拉说。

劳拉走进自己的房间，锁上了门——这可是妈妈不许的事。随后她扑到床上哭起来。妈妈一点儿也不理解她；她还把自己的模样弄得没法见人。人们一个接一个来拧门把手，她死活不肯开门，萨拉还吓唬说要拿浇园子的水管从窗户往屋里灌水。于是大家由她去了，她便趴在枕头上，眼泪汪汪地生了一晚上闷气。不过，夜里更衣之前，她还是悄悄拉开一道门缝，把萍留在蹭鞋垫上的那块蛋糕拿了进来。她天性里的那股乐天劲儿又开始抬头了。她发现，只要把头发好好梳向一边，那道缺口就能盖住；再说，知道自己不被人理解，这滋味里其实还有几分妙处呢。这让你觉得，自己跟谁都不一样。

妈妈——连里里外外一把抓的萨拉都歇下了，她还在埋头飞针走线——妈妈独自微微一笑，笑意板正，带着几分好笑；夜里锁门之前，她把那绺深色的发卷妥妥帖帖地收好了。

## II / 二

===Original===
Laura, sleeping flat on her stomach, was roused next morning by Pin who said:

“Wake up, Wondrous Fair, mother wants to speak to you. She says you can get into bed in my place, before you dress.” Pin slept warm and cosy at Mother’s side.

Laura rose on her elbow and looked at her sister: Pin was standing in the doorway holding her nightgown to her, in such a way as to expose all of her thin little legs.

“Come on,” urged Pin. “Sarah’s going to give me my bath while you’re with mother.”

“Go away, Pin,” said Laura snappily. “I told you yesterday you could say Laura, and﻿ ﻿… and you’re more like a spider than ever.”

===Chinese===
第二天早晨，劳拉正趴着睡得香，被萍（Pin）唤醒了，萍说：

「醒醒，奇妙佳人（Wondrous Fair），妈妈有话跟你说。她说你可以先钻到我睡的地方躺下，然后再穿衣服。」萍睡在妈妈身边，那里又暖和又舒服。

劳拉撑起胳膊肘，望向妹妹：萍站在门口，睡衣只往身上虚拢着，两条细伶伶的小腿整个儿露在外面。

「快来呀，」萍催促道，「趁你陪妈妈的时候，萨拉（Sarah）要给我洗澡呢。」

「走开，萍，」劳拉没好气地说，「我昨天说过，你尽管叫劳拉好了，还有……还有你这模样，比从前更像一只蜘蛛了。」

===Original===
“Spider” was another nickname for Pin, owed to her rotund little body and mere sticks of legs﻿—she was “all belly” as Sarah put it﻿—and the mere mention of it made Pin fly; for she was very touchy about her legs.

As soon as the door closed behind her, Laura sprang out of bed and, waiting neither to wash herself nor to say her prayers, began to pull on her clothes, confusing strings and buttons in her haste, and quite forgetting that on this eventful morning she had meant to dress herself with more than ordinary care. She was just lacing her shoes when Sarah looked in.

“Why, Miss Laura, don’t you know your ma wants you?”

“It’s too late. I’m dressed now,” said Laura darkly.

Sarah shook her head. “Missis’ll be fine an’ angry. An’ you needn’t ’ave ’ad a row on your last day.”

===Chinese===
『蜘蛛』是萍的又一个绰号，因为她那圆滚滚的小身子底下，只有两根细棍儿似的腿——用萨拉的话说，她『浑身都是肚子』——而只要有人一提这个词，萍就气得跳起来；她对自个儿这两条腿，可敏感着呢。

她前脚刚出门，劳拉便一骨碌跳下床，脸也顾不上洗，晨祷也顾不上做，就手忙脚乱地穿起衣裳来，慌慌张张地把带子和扣子全都缠乱了，全然忘了在这个非同小可的早晨，她本打算穿得比平时格外仔细些。她正弯腰系鞋带，萨拉探头进来。

「哎哟，劳拉小姐，您不知道太太正叫您吗？」

「来不及了。我已经穿好啦。」劳拉阴沉着脸说。

萨拉摇了摇头。「太太准会气得不轻。您也犯不着在家的最后一天，还平白挨一顿骂呀。」

===Original===
Laura stole out of the door and ran down the garden to the summerhouse. This, the size of a goodly room, was formed of a single dense, hairy-leafed tree, round the trunk of which a seat was built. Here she cowered, her elbows on her knees, her chin in her hands. Her face wore the stiff expression that went by the name of “Laura’s sulks,” but her eyes were big, and as watchful as those of a scared animal. If Sarah came to fetch her she would hold on to the seat with both hands. But even if she had to yield to Sarah’s greater strength﻿—well, at least she was up and dressed. Not like the last time﻿—about a week ago Mother had tried this kind of thing. Then, she had been caught unawares. She had gone into Pin’s warm place, curious and unsuspecting, and thereupon Mother had begun to talk seriously to her, and not with her usual directness. She had reminded Laura that she was growing up apace and would soon be a woman; had told her that she must now begin to give up childish habits, and learn to behave in a modest and womanly way﻿—all disagreeable, disturbing things, which Laura did not in the least want to hear. When it became clear to her what it was about, she had thrown back the bedclothes and escaped from the room. And since then she had been careful never to be long alone with Mother.

But now half an hour went by and no one came to fetch her: her grim little face relaxed. She felt very hungry, too, and when at length she heard Pin calling, she jumped up and betrayed her hiding-place.

“Laura! Laura, where are you? Mother says to come to breakfast and not be silly. The coach’ll be here in an hour.”

Taking hands the sisters ran to the house.

===Chinese===
劳拉溜出房门，顺着园子一路跑进那座凉亭。这凉亭由一棵枝叶浓密、叶面带绒毛的大树长成，足有一间不小的屋子那么大，树干四周圈着一条坐凳。她蜷在这里，胳膊肘支在膝上，双手托着下巴。脸上摆出一副家里叫做『劳拉的赌气』的僵硬神情，可她的眼睛睁得老大，像受惊的野兽一样警觉。要是萨拉来找她，她就用双手死死扒住坐凳。不过就算最后不得不屈服于萨拉更大的力气——哼，至少她已经起了床、穿好了衣裳。不像上一回——大约一星期前，妈妈就使过这一手。那一次，她被打了个措手不及。她带着好奇、毫无戒心地钻进萍那个热乎乎的被窝，妈妈跟着就郑重其事地跟她谈了起来，而且一反平日有话直说的做派。妈妈提醒劳拉，说她眼看一天天长大，很快就要成为大人；说她现在就得开始改掉孩子气的习惯，学着举止端庄、有个姑娘家的样子——净是些叫人厌烦、叫人心慌的事，劳拉一点儿也不想听。等她听明白这话是冲什么来的，便一把掀开被子，逃出了房间。打那以后，她就处处留神，再也不肯跟妈妈单独待上很久。

可是这回，半个钟头过去了，也没有人来找她：她那张绷紧的小脸松了下来。她还饿得慌，等到终于听见萍的喊声，便一跃而起，自个儿暴露了藏身之处。

「劳拉！劳拉，你在哪儿？妈妈说快来吃早饭，别犯傻。马车一个钟头后就到。」

姐妹俩手拉着手，跑回屋去。

===Original===
In the passage, Sarah was busy roping a battered tin box. With their own hands the little boys had been allowed to paste on this a big sheet of notepaper, which bore, in Mother’s writing, the words:

> Miss Laura Tweedle Rambotham 

 The Ladies’ College 

 Melbourne.

Mother herself was standing at the breakfast-table cutting sandwiches.

===Chinese===
过道里，萨拉正忙着给一只碰得坑坑洼洼的铁皮箱捆绳子。两个小弟弟获准亲自动手，在箱子上糊了一大张信纸，上面是妈妈的手笔，写着：

> 劳拉·特威德尔·拉姆博瑟姆小姐 

 女子学院 

 墨尔本。

妈妈亲自站在早餐桌旁切三明治。

===Original===
“Come and eat your breakfast, child,” was all she said at the moment. “The tea’s quite cold.”

Laura sat down and fell to with appetite, but also with a side-glance at the generous pile of bread and meat growing under Mother’s hands.

“I shall never eat all that,” she said ungraciously; it galled her still to be considered a greedy child with an insatiable stomach.

“I know better than you do what you’ll eat,” said Mother. “You’ll be hungry enough by this evening I can tell you, not getting any dinner.”

Pin's face fell at this prospect. “Oh, mother, won’t she really get any dinner?” she asked: and to her soft little heart going to school began to seem one of the blackest experiences life held.

===Chinese===
「过来吃早饭吧，孩子。」她此刻只说了这一句。「茶都凉透了。」

劳拉坐下便吃了起来，胃口很好，一边还斜着眼，瞟妈妈手底下越摞越高的一大堆面包和肉。

「我怎么也吃不了那么多。」她没好气地说；人家还把她当成贪嘴、肚子填不满的孩子，这仍旧让她来气。

「你能吃多少，我比你自己清楚，」妈妈说，「我告诉你，到今天晚上，你准会饿得够呛，午饭可没有吃的。」

一听这话，萍的脸垮了下来。「哎呀，妈妈，她真连饭都吃不上吗？」她问道：在她那颗软和的小心里，上学这件事，开始显得是人生里顶顶凄惨的遭遇之一了。

===Original===
“Why, she’ll be in the train, stupid, ’ow can she?” said Sarah. “Do you think trains give you dinners?”

“Oh, mother, please cut ever such a lot!” begged Pin sniffing valiantly.

Laura began to feel somewhat moved herself at this solicitude, and choked down a lump in her throat with a gulp of tea. But when Pin had gone with Sarah to pick some nectarines, Mother’s face grew stern, and Laura’s emotion passed.

“I feel more troubled about you than I can say, Laura. I don’t know how you’ll ever get on in life﻿—you’re so disobedient and self-willed. It would serve you very well right, I’m sure, for not coming this morning, if I didn’t give you a penny of pocket-money to take to school.”

Laura had heard this threat before, and thought it wiser not to reply. Gobbling up the rest of her breakfast she slipped away.

===Chinese===
「嗐，她那会儿在火车上呢，傻瓜，她怎么吃得上？」萨拉说。「你当火车还管饭不成？」

「哎呀，妈妈，求您切一大堆吧，越多越好！」萍一面勇敢地吸着鼻子，一面恳求道。

见妹妹这般牵挂，劳拉自己也不免有些动情，一口茶咽下去，把喉头那一块哽咽压了下去。可是等萍跟萨拉去摘油桃之后，妈妈的脸色沉了下来，劳拉的感动也就过去了。

「劳拉，我心里为你操的这份心，比嘴上能说出来的还多。我不知道你往后可怎么在这世上过活——你这么不听话，这么任性。我敢说，就为你今儿早上不肯过来，我一个便士的零花钱也不给你带去学校，那才叫罪有应得。」

这样的吓唬话，劳拉从前就听过，觉得还是不吭声为妙。她三口两口把剩下的早饭扒完，便溜了出去。

===Original===
With the other children at her heels she made a round of the garden, bidding goodbye to things and places. There were the two summerhouses in which she had played house; in which she had cooked and eaten and slept. There was the tall fir-tree with the rung-like branches by which she had been accustomed to climb to the very treetop; there was the wilderness of bamboo and cane where she had been Crusoe; the ancient, broadleaved cactus on which she had scratched their names and drawn their portraits; here, the high aloe that had such a mysterious charm for you, because you never knew when the hundred years might expire and the aloe burst into flower. Here again was the old fig tree with the rounded, polished boughs, from which, seated as in a cradle, she had played Juliet to Pin’s Romeo, and vice versa﻿—but oftenest Juliet: for though Laura greatly preferred to be the ardent lover at the foot, Pin was but a poor climber, and, as she clung trembling to her branch, needed so much prompting in her lines﻿—even then to repeat them with such feeble emphasis﻿—that Laura invariably lost patience with her and the love-scene ended in a squabble. Passing behind a wooden fence which was a tangle of passionflower, she opened the door of the fowl-house, and out strutted the mother-hen followed by her pretty brood. Laura had given each of the chicks a name, and she now took Napoleon and Garibaldi up in her hand and laid her cheek against their downy breasts, the younger children following her movements in respectful silence. Between the bars of the rabbit hutch she thrust enough greenstuff to last the two little occupants for days; and everywhere she went she was accompanied by a legless magpie, which, in spite of its infirmity, hopped cheerily and quickly on its stumps. Laura had rescued it and reared it; it followed her like a dog; and she was only less devoted to it than she had been to a native bear which died under her hands.

“Now listen, children,” she said as she rose from her knees before the hutch. “If you don’t look well after Maggy and the bunnies, I don’t know what I’ll do. The chicks’ll be all right. Sarah’ll take care of them, ’cause of the eggs. But Maggy and the bunnies don’t have eggs, and if they’re not fed, or if Frank treads on Maggy again, then they’ll die. Now if you let them die, I don’t know what I’ll do to you! Yes, I do: I’ll send the devil to you at night when the room’s dark, before you go to sleep. So there!”

===Chinese===
她身后跟着弟弟妹妹，在园子里兜了一遭，向各样东西、各个地方一一道别。这里有那两座凉亭，她在里面玩过家家，做过饭、用过餐、睡过觉；有那棵高高的冷杉，枝杈一级一级像梯子的横档，她惯常顺着它们一直爬到树梢；有那片竹子和藤丛交织的荒野，她在那里做过鲁滨逊；有那株古老阔叶的仙人掌，她在上面刻过他们的名字、画过他们的肖像；这儿，还有那株高高的龙舌兰，它对你有一种说不出的神秘魅力，因为你永远不知道那一百年会在哪一天到期，龙舌兰会轰然开花。这儿又是那棵老无花果树，枝条浑圆、磨得光滑，人坐在上面就像坐在摇篮里，她在树上配着萍演过朱丽叶对罗密欧，也掉换过角色——不过演得最多的是朱丽叶：因为虽然劳拉更喜欢做树底下那位热烈求爱的人，萍爬起树来却实在不济，她抖抖索索地抱住枝子，台词得人一句句提——提了，还念得那样有气无力——劳拉于是总耐不住性子，一场爱情戏每每以拌嘴收场。她绕过一道缠满西番莲的木栅栏，推开鸡舍的门，老母鸡神气活现地迈步而出，身后跟着一窝漂亮的小鸡。每只鸡雏劳拉都给起过名字，这会儿她把拿破仑（Napoleon）和加里波第（Garibaldi）捧在手心里，把脸颊贴上它们绒茸茸的胸脯，弟弟妹妹们屏住呼吸，满怀敬意地注视着她的一举一动。她又从兔笼的栅栏间塞进足敷那两位小房客吃好几天的青菜；而无论她走到哪里，身后总跟着一只没有腿的喜鹊，它虽然身有残缺，却踩着两条腿桩子，又欢快又利落地蹦跳。这只喜鹊是劳拉救回来又养大的；它像狗一样跟定她；她疼它，只差没能像当年疼那只死在她手里的树袋熊（native bear）那样。

「都听着，孩子们，」她从兔笼前直起身来说道，「要是你们不好好照看玛吉（Maggy）和这两只小兔子，我不知道我会干出什么来。小鸡不用操心，萨拉会照看它们，为了鸡蛋嘛。可玛吉和小兔子不下蛋，要是没人喂它们，或者弗兰克（Frank）再踩着玛吉，它们就会死。你们要是让它们死了，我不知道我会怎么收拾你们！不对，我知道：夜里屋里黑了、你们还没睡着的时候，我就派魔鬼来找你们。就是这样！」

===Original===
“How can you if you’re not here?” asked Leppie.

Pin, however, who believed in ghosts and apparitions with all her fearful little heart, promised tremulously never, never to forget; but Laura was not satisfied until each of them in turn had repeated, in a low voice, with the appropriate gestures, the sacred secret, and forbidden formula:

> Is my finger wet?
 

 Is my finger dry?
 

 God’ll strike me dead,
 

 If I tell a lie.

===Chinese===
「你人都不在这儿，怎么派得来？」莱皮（Leppie）问。

萍却不一样，她那颗胆怯的小心肝对鬼魂幽灵是全信的，哆哆嗦嗦地应承着，说永远、永远不忘；可劳拉还不满意，直到他们一个挨一个，压低嗓音、比着该有的手势，念过那桩神圣的秘密、那道禁咒般的口诀：

> 我的手指湿不湿？
 

 我的手指干不干？
 

 上帝就要劈死我，
 

 只要我撒一句谎。

===Original===
Then Sarah’s voice was heard calling, and the boys went out into the road to watch for the coach. Laura’s dressing proved a lengthy business, and was accomplished amid bustle, and scolding, and little peacemaking words from Pin; for in her hurry that morning Laura had forgotten to put on the clean linen Mother had laid beside the bed, and consequently had now to strip to the skin.

The boys announced the coming of the coach with shrill cries, and simultaneously the rumble of wheels was heard. Sarah came from the kitchen drying her hands, and Pin began to cry.

“Now, shut up, res’vor!” said Sarah roughly: her own eyes were moist. “You don’t see Miss Laura be such a silly-billy. Anyone ’ud think you was goin’, not ’er.”

The ramshackle old vehicle, one of Cobb’s Royal Mail Coaches, big-bodied, lumbering, scarlet, pulled by two stout horses, drew up before the door, and the driver climbed down from his seat.

===Chinese===
这时传来萨拉的喊声，男孩子们跑到大路上去张望马车。劳拉这回的梳洗穿戴成了桩旷日持久的工程，是在一阵忙乱、一阵数落，外加萍几句打圆场的话里完成的；因为那天早上她一时慌张，忘了换上妈妈搁在床边的干净内衣，这会儿只好脱个精光，从头再来。

男孩子们尖着嗓子喊马车来了，与此同时，车轮的辘辘声也听见了。萨拉从厨房出来，正在擦手，萍放声哭了起来。

「行了，闭嘴，小东西！」萨拉粗声粗气地说，她自己的眼睛也湿润了。「你瞧劳拉小姐都没有这么傻里傻气。人家还当要走的是你，不是她呢。」

这辆摇摇晃晃的破旧车子，是科布皇家邮车（Cobb’s Royal Mail Coaches）里的一辆，车身宽大、笨重，通体猩红，由两匹壮马拉着，在门前停住，车夫从座位上爬了下来。

===Original===
“Now good day to you, ma’am, good day, miss”﻿—this to Sarah who, picking up the box, handed it to him to be strapped on under the apron. “Well, well, and so the little girl’s goin’ to school, is she? My, but time flies! Well do I remember the day ma’am, when I drove you all across for the first time. These children wasn’t big enough then to git up and down be thimselves. Now I warrant you they can﻿—just look at ’em, will you?﻿—But my! Ain’t you ashamed of yourself”﻿—he spoke to Pin﻿—“pipin’ your eye like that? Why, you’ll flood the road if you don’t hould on. Yes, yes, ma’am, bless you, I’ll look after her, and put her inter the train wid me own han’s. Don’t you be oneasy. The Lord he cares for the widder and the orphun, and if He don’t, why Patrick O’Donnell does.”

This was O’Donnell’s standing joke; he uttered it with a loud chuckle. While speaking he had let down the steps and helped the three children up﻿—they were to ride with Laura to the outskirts of the township. The little boys giggled excitedly at his assertion that the horses would not be equal to the weight. Only Pin wept on, in undiminished grief.

===Chinese===
「您好啊，太太，您好，小姐」——这句是对萨拉说的，她正拎起箱子递给他，好捆到车前的围毯底下。「嗳，嗳，这么说小丫头是要上学去啦？天哪，日子过得可真快！太太，你们全家头一回坐着车过来那天，我还记得清清楚楚呢。那会儿这几个孩子还小，自个儿上下车都不成。如今我敢保他们全行了——您就瞧瞧他们，瞧见没有？——哎呀！你也不害臊」——他这话是对萍说的——「眼睛哭得水汪汪的？再这样淌下去，你都要把马路给淹啦。是，是，太太，您放心，我会照应她的，亲手把她送上火车。您甭惦记。上帝看顾寡妇和孤儿，上帝要是不管，还有帕特里克·奥唐奈（Patrick O’Donnell）管呢。」

这是奥唐奈挂在嘴边的一句老笑话；他说完，自己咯咯地放声笑起来。说话的当儿，他已放下踏板，把三个孩子一个个抱上车——他们要陪着劳拉坐到镇子边上。他说这两匹马怕是经不住这份分量，小家伙们听得兴奋地咯咯直笑。只有萍还在哭，那份悲伤一点儿也没消减。

===Original===
“Now, Miss Laura.”

“Now, Laura. Goodbye, darling. And do try and be good. And be sure you write once a week. And tell me everything. Whether you are happy﻿—and if you get enough to eat﻿—and if you have enough blankets on your bed. And remember always to change your boots if you get your feet wet. And don’t lean out of the window in the train.”

For some time past Laura had had need of all her self-control, not to cry before the children. As the hour drew near it had grown harder and harder; while dressing, she had resorted to counting the number of times the profile of a Roman emperor appeared in the flowers on the wallpaper. Now the worst moment of all was come﻿—the moment of goodbye. She did not look at Pin, but she heard her tireless, snuffly weeping, and set her own lips tight.

===Chinese===
「好了，劳拉小姐。」

「好了，劳拉。再见，宝贝。千万要听话，学做好人。每星期务必来一封信。什么都告诉我。你过得快活不快活——吃得够不够——床上被子厚不厚。还有记住，脚要是打湿了，一定要换靴子。坐火车别把身子探出窗外。」

一段时间以来，为了不在弟弟妹妹面前哭出来，劳拉一直使着全部的自制力。动身的时辰越近，这越难做到；穿衣裳的当儿，她只好靠数壁纸上花朵里罗马皇帝的侧脸出现了多少回来分神。此刻，最难熬的一刻终于来了——道别的一刻。她不去看萍，可萍那没个停、抽抽嗒嗒的哭声一直钻进她耳朵里，她把自己的嘴唇抿得紧紧的。

===Original===
“Yes, mother﻿ ﻿… no, mother,” she answered shortly, “I’ll be all right. Goodbye.” She could not, however, restrain a kind of dry sob, which jumped up her throat.

When she was in the coach Sarah, whom she had forgotten, climbed up to kiss her; and there was some joking between O’Donnell and the servant while the steps were being folded and put away. Laura did not smile; her thin little face was very pale. Mother’s heart went out to her in a pity which she did not know how to express.

“Don’t forget your sandwiches. And when you’re alone, feel in the pocket of your ulster and you’ll find something nice. Goodbye, darling.”

“Goodbye﻿ ﻿… goodbye.”

===Chinese===
「嗯，妈妈……不，妈妈，」她简短地答道，「我会好好的。再见。」然而有一声干哑的呜咽猛地蹿上喉咙，她没能忍住。

她上了车，被她忘在脑后的萨拉爬上来亲了亲她；踏板折起收好的工夫，奥唐奈和女仆之间还打了几句趣。劳拉没有笑；她那张瘦削的小脸煞白。妈妈心里对她的怜惜直往上涌，却不知道该怎样表达出来。

「别忘了你的三明治。等你一个人的时候，伸手摸摸大衣口袋，会摸到一样好东西。再见，宝贝。」

「再见……再见。」

===Original===
The driver had mounted to his seat, he unwound the reins cried “Get up!” to the two burly horses, the vehicle was set in motion and trundled down the main street. Until it turned the corner by the Shire Gardens, Laura let her handkerchief fly from the window. Sarah waved hers; then wiped her eyes and lustily blew her nose. Mother only sighed.

“It was all she could do to keep up,” she said as much to herself as to Sarah. “I do hope she’ll be all right. She seems such a child to be sending off like this. Yet what else could I do? To a State School, I’ve always said it, my children shall never go﻿—not if I have to beg the money to send them elsewhere.”

But she sighed again, in spite of the energy of her words, and stood gazing at the place where the coach had disappeared. She was still a comparatively young woman, and straight of body; but trouble, poverty and night-watches had scored many lines on her forehead.

“Don’t you worry,” said Sarah. “Miss Laura’ll be all right. She’s just a bit too clever﻿—brains for two, that’s what it is. An’ children *will* grow up an’ get big﻿ ﻿… an’ change their feathers.” She spoke absently, drawing her metaphor from a brood of chickens which had strayed across the road, and was now trying to mount the wooden verandah﻿—“Shooh! Get away with you!”

===Chinese===
车夫已登上座位，抖开缰绳，朝那两匹壮马吆喝一声「驾！」，车身便动了起来，顺着大街辘辘驶去。直到马车拐过郡花园（Shire Gardens）旁的街角，劳拉一直让手帕在车窗外飘舞。萨拉挥动她的手帕，随后擦擦眼睛，使劲擤了擤鼻子。妈妈只是叹了口气。

「她能一直撑着不掉泪，已经很不容易了。」这话与其说是对萨拉讲的，不如说是对她自己讲的。「我真希望她一切平安。这样就把她送出门，她看上去还这么小，还是个孩子。可我还有什么法子呢？州立学校——这话我不知说过多少遍——我的孩子们永远不去，哪怕要我去讨钱，也要送他们到别处念书。」

可是尽管话说得这样斩钉截铁，她又叹了一口气，站在原地，望着马车消失的地方出神。她还算年轻，身板挺直；只是忧患、贫困和一夜一夜的守候，已在她额头上刻下了许多道纹路。

「您就别愁了，」萨拉说。「劳拉小姐会好好的。她就是脑子太灵了点儿——一副脑子顶两副使，就是这么回事。再说，孩子嘛，偏偏就是要长大、要抽条的……还要换羽毛的。」她说得心不在焉，这个比方，是从一窝溜达到马路上、这会儿正想往木头游廊上蹦的小鸡身上借来的——「嘘！去你们的！」

===Original===
“I know that. But Laura﻿—The other children have never given me a moment’s worry. But Laura’s different. I seem to get less and less able to manage her. If only her father had been alive to help!”

“I’m sure no father livin’ could do more than you for those blessed children,” said Sarah with impatience. “You think of nothin’ else. It ’ud be a great deal better if you took more care o’ yourself. You sit up nights an’ don’t get no proper sleep slavin’ away at that blessed embroid’ry an’ stuff, so as Miss Laura can get off to school an’ to ’er books. An’ then you want to worry over ’er as well. She’ll be all right. Miss Laura’s like peas. You’ve got to get ’em outer the pod﻿—they’re in there sure enough. An’ b’sides I guess school’ll knock all the nonsense out of ’er.”

“Oh, I hope they won’t be too hard on her,” said Mother in quick alarm. “Shut the side gate, will you. Those children have left it open again. And, Sarah, I think we’ll turn out the drawing-room.”

Sarah grunted to herself as she went to close the gate. This had not entered into her scheme of work for the day, and her cooking was still undone. But she did not gainsay her mistress, as she otherwise would have made no scruple of doing; for she knew that nothing was more helpful to the latter in a crisis than hard, manual work. Besides, Sarah herself had a sneaking weakness for what she called “dra’in’-room days.” For the drawing-room was the storehouse of what treasures had remained over from a past prosperity. It was crowded with bric-a-brac and ornament; and as her mistress took these objects up one by one, to dust and polish them, she would, if she were in a good humour, tell Sarah where and how they had been bought, or describe the places they had originally come from: so that Sarah, pausing broom in hand to listen, had with time gathered some vague ideas of a country like “Inja,” for example, whence came the little silver “pagody,” and the expressionless brass god who squatted vacantly and at ease.

===Chinese===
「这我知道。可劳拉——别的孩子从来没叫我操过一分钟的心。劳拉不一样。我好像越来越管不住她了。她爸爸要是还活着、能帮衬一把就好了！」

「我敢说，天底下哪个活着的爹，为这些宝贝孩子做的也不会比您更多，」萨拉不无焦躁地说。「您心里想的就没有别的。您多顾惜顾惜自个儿才好呢。您夜夜熬着不睡，守着那要命的绣活儿什么的埋头苦干，连个囫囵觉都睡不上，为的就是劳拉小姐能去上学、念她的书。到头来您还要为她发愁。她会好好的。劳拉小姐就像豌豆，总得有人把豆子从荚里剥出来——豆子准是在里头的。再说，我估摸着，学校会把她那些傻气全给敲打光。」

「哎，但愿他们别对她太狠。」妈妈慌忙说道。「把边门关上，好吗。那几个孩子又敞着门没关。还有，萨拉，我看咱们把客厅彻底翻出来收拾收拾吧。」

萨拉朝边门走去，一边暗自哼了一声。这可不在她今天当差的盘算里，何况她的饭食还没做呢。但她没有驳女主人的话——换了平时，她驳起来是毫不含糊的；因为她知道，每逢这种难过的关口，再没有什么比下力气的粗活更能让女主人排遣的了。何况，萨拉自己对所谓的『客厅日』，也存着一份说不出口的偏爱。原来这客厅是个宝库，收藏着昔日殷实岁月里留存下来的珍奇物件。屋里摆满了古玩摆设；女主人把这些东西一件件拿起来掸灰、擦拭的时候，若是兴致好，还会告诉萨拉它们是在哪儿、怎么买下的，或者讲讲它们最早出自什么地方：于是萨拉拄着扫帚停下来听着，天长日久，也约略攒下了对某一个国度的模糊印象，比如她口中的『印佳』（Inja，即印度）——那座小银『宝塔』和那尊面目木然、悠然蹲坐的铜神像，就是打那儿来的。

## III / 三

===Original===
As long as the coach rolled down the main street Laura sat bolt upright at the window. In fancy she heard people telling one another that this was little Miss Rambotham going to school. She was particularly glad that just as they went past the Commercial Hotel, Miss Perrotet, the landlord’s red-haired daughter, should put her fuzzy head out of the window﻿—for Miss Perrotet had also been to boarding-school, and thought very highly of herself in consequence, though it had only been for a year, to finish. At the National Bank the manager’s wife waved a friendly hand to the children, and at the Royal Mail Hotel where they drew up for passengers or commissions, Mrs. Paget, the stout landlady, came out, smoothing down her black satin apron.

“Well, I’m sure I wonder your ma likes sendin’ you off so alone.”

The ride had comforted Pin a little; but when they had passed the chief stores and the flour-mill, and were come to a part of the road where the houses were fewer, her tears broke out afresh. The very last house was left behind, the high machinery of the claims came into view, the watery flats where Chinamen were forever rocking washdirt in cradles; and O’Donnell dismounted and opened the door. He lifted the three out one by one, shaking his head in humorous dismay at Pin, and as little Frank showed sighs of beginning, too, by puckering up his face and doubling up his body, the kindly man tried to make them laugh by asking if he had the stomachache. Laura had one more glimpse of the children standing hand in hand﻿—even in her trouble Pin did not forget her charges﻿—then a sharp bend in the road hid them from her sight.

===Chinese===
马车顺着大街驶去的这一路，劳拉始终笔直地坐在车窗边。她在想象里听见人们互相转告：这就是拉姆博瑟姆家的小姐，上学去啦。路过商业旅馆（Commercial Hotel）时，店主家那个红头发的女儿佩罗特小姐（Miss Perrotet）恰好把她毛茸茸的脑袋探出窗外，这尤其叫她高兴——因为佩罗特小姐也上过寄宿学校，并因此把自己看得极高，虽说那不过是读了一年，为的是结业完事。到了国民银行（National Bank），经理太太朝孩子们友好地挥了挥手；到了皇家邮车旅馆（Royal Mail Hotel）——马车要在那儿停一停，捎客带货——胖胖的老板娘佩吉太太（Mrs. Paget）走了出来，一面抚平她的黑缎围裙。

「嗳呀，您妈竟舍得叫你这么孤零零一个人出门，我可真纳闷。」

坐这一趟车，萍本已安静了些；可是过了主要的几家铺子和面粉厂，来到房舍渐稀的路上，她的眼泪又涌了上来。最后一家房子落在了身后，矿场上高耸的机械映入眼帘，还有那片水汪汪的洼地，中国人永远在那里摇着淘金槽淘洗矿砂；奥唐奈跳下车，打开了车门。他把三个孩子一个个抱下车，朝萍滑稽地摇着头、做出没辙的样子；小弗兰克皱起小脸、弓着身子，也显出要哭的开头，这位好心的车夫便想法子逗他们笑，问他是不是肚子疼了。劳拉又望了一眼手拉手站着的弟妹们——萍纵然伤心，也没忘记自己照看弟妹的差事——随后道路一个急弯，便把他们遮得看不见了。

===Original===
She was alone in the capacious body of the coach, alone, and the proud excitement of parting was over. The staunchly repressed tears welled up with a gush, and flinging herself down across the seat she cried bitterly. It was not a childishly irresponsible grief like Pin’s: it was more passionate, and went deeper; and her overloaded feelings were soon relieved. But as she was not used to crying, she missed the moment at which she might have checked herself, and went on shedding tears after they had become a luxury.

“Why, goodness gracious, what’s this?” cried a loud, cheerful and astonished voice, and a fat, rosy face beamed in on Laura. “Why, here’s a little girl in here, cryin’ fit to break ’er heart. Come, come, my dear, what’s the matter? Don’t cry like that, now don’t.”

The coach had stopped, the door opened and a stout woman climbed in, bearing a big basket, and followed by a young man with straw-coloured whiskers. Laura sat up like a dart and pulled her hat straight, crimson with mortification at being discovered in such a plight. She had instantly curbed her tears, but she could not disguise the fact that she had red eyes and a swollen nose﻿—that she was in short what Sarah called “all bunged up.” She made no reply to the newcomer’s exclamations, but sat clutching her handkerchief and staring out of the window. The woman’s good-natured curiosity, however, was not to be done.

===Chinese===
宽敞的车厢里只剩下她一个人，只她一个人，临别时那股傲然的兴奋劲儿也已过去。一直硬压下去的眼泪一下子涌了上来，她横扑在座位上，伤心地哭起来。这悲伤不像萍的那种，是小孩子家不知深浅的哭：它更炽烈，扎得也更深；满溢的感情很快就宣泄掉了。只是她不惯哭，错过了本可以收住的那一刻，等眼泪已经成了纯粹的奢侈，还在一个劲儿往下掉。

「哎呀，我的老天爷，这是怎么啦？」一个响亮、快活而吃惊的声音喊道，一张红扑扑的胖脸凑到劳拉跟前，笑开了。「哟，这儿坐着个小姑娘，哭得心都要碎啦。好了，好了，亲爱的，怎么回事？别这么哭，可别这么哭。」

马车停了下来，车门开了，一个胖女人挎着一只大篮子爬上车，身后跟着一个蓄着草黄色络腮胡的年轻人。劳拉一骨碌坐直，把帽子扶正，叫人撞见这副狼狈相，臊得满脸通红。她的眼泪当即收住了，可眼睛红红的、鼻子肿肿的，这事实却遮掩不住——简而言之，她正是萨拉所说的那种『鼻子眼睛全哭堵了』。对新客人的连声惊叹，她一句也没有答，只坐在那里攥着手帕，眼睛望着窗外。然而这位妇人心地虽好，好奇心却压不下去。

===Original===
“You poor little thing, you!” she persisted. “Wherever are you goin’, my dear, so alone?”

“I’m going to boarding-school,” said Laura, and shot a glance at the couple opposite.

“To boardin’-school? Peter! D’you hear?﻿—Why, whatever’s your ma thinkin’ of to send such a little chick as you to boardin’-school?﻿ ﻿… and so alone, too.”

Laura’s face took on a curious air of dignity.

===Chinese===
「你这可怜的小人儿哟！」她追问不停。「亲爱的，你一个人究竟要上哪儿去呀？」

「我去上寄宿学校，」劳拉说，朝对面那对男女瞟了一眼。

「上寄宿学校？彼得（Peter）！你听见没有？——哎呀，你妈是怎么想的，把像你这样一只小雏儿送去寄宿学校？……还这么孤零零一个人。」

劳拉的脸上摆出一副古怪的庄重神气。

===Original===
“I’m not so very little,” she answered; and went on to explain, in phrases which she had heard so often that she knew them by heart: “Only small for my age. I was twelve in spring. And I have to go to school, because I’ve learnt all I can at home.”

This failed to impress the woman.

“Snakes alive!﻿—that’s young enough in all conscience. And such a delicate little creature, too. Just like that one o’ Sam MacFarlane’s that popped off last Christmas﻿—isn’t she, Peter?”

Peter, who avoided looking at Laura, sheepishly mumbled something about like enough she was.

===Chinese===
「我也不是那么小，」她答道；接着用一套听过太多遍、早已背得滚瓜烂熟的话解释起来：「不过是照年纪显得小了点。我春天满的十二岁。再说我也非上学不可，因为家里的东西，我已经学得不能再学了。」

这话没能让妇人肃然起敬。

「乖乖！——这岁数也实在够小的了。偏偏又是这么娇嫩的一个小人儿。活脱脱像山姆·麦克法兰（Sam MacFarlane）家那个去年圣诞节说没就没了的小家伙——你说是不是，彼得？」

彼得一直避着不去看劳拉，只讪讪地咕哝了一句，说八成是像。

===Original===
“And who *is* your ma, my dear? What’s your name?” continued her interrogator.

Laura replied politely; but there was a reserve in her manner which, together with the name she gave, told enough: the widow, Laura’s mother, had the reputation of being very “stuck-up,” and of bringing up her children in the same way.

The woman did not press Laura further; she whispered something behind her hand to Peter, then searching in her basket found a large, red apple, which she held out with an encouraging nod and smile.

“Here, my dear. Here’s something for you. Don’t cry any more, don’t now. It’ll be all right.”

===Chinese===
「那你妈究竟是谁呀，亲爱的？你叫什么名字？」盘问的人接着问。

劳拉客气地回答了；可她态度里那份矜持，连同她报出的名字，已经把话说尽了：这位寡妇——劳拉的母亲——是有名的『傲气得很』，还把孩子也照同样孤高的路子栽培。

妇人没有再追问劳拉；她用手挡着嘴，对彼得嘀咕了一句什么，然后在篮子里翻寻，找出一只又大又红的苹果，带着鼓劲的点头和微笑递了过来。

「拿着，亲爱的。这是给你的。别再哭啦，别哭了。一切都会好的。」

===Original===
Laura, who was well aware that she had not shed a tear since the couple entered the coach, coloured deeply, and made a movement, half shy, half unwilling, to put her hands behind her.

“Oh no, thank you,” she said in extreme embarrassment, not wishing to hurt the giver’s feelings. “Mother doesn’t care for us to take things from strangers.”

“Bless her soul!” cried the stout woman in amaze. “It’s only an apple! Now, my dear, just you take it, and make your mind easy. Your ma wouldn’t have nothin’ against it today, I’m sure o’ that﻿—goin’ away so far and all so alone like this. It’s sweet and juicy.”

“It’s Melb’m you’ll be boun’ for I dessay?” said the yellow-haired Peter so suddenly that Laura started.

===Chinese===
劳拉心里一清二楚，自打这一男一女上了车，她就再没掉过一滴眼泪，闻言涨红了脸，半是害羞、半是不情愿地动了一下，想把两只手藏到背后去。

「哦，不用了，谢谢您，」她窘迫至极地说，又不愿伤了给东西的人的心。「妈妈不愿意我们拿陌生人的东西。」

「上帝保佑她吧！」胖妇人惊诧地喊道。「不过是一只苹果呀！好了，亲爱的，你就拿着吧，把心放宽。你妈今天绝不会说什么的，这我敢保——瞧你路这么远，又这样一个人走。这苹果又甜又水灵。」

「我猜你这趟是上墨尔本去的吧？」黄头发的彼得突然开腔，倒把劳拉吓了一跳。

===Original===
She confirmed this, and let her solemn eyes rest on him wondering why he was so red and fidgety and uncomfortable. The woman said: “Tch, tch, tch!” at the length of the journey Laura was undertaking, and Peter, growing still redder, volunteered another remark.

“I was nigh to bein’ in Melb’m once meself,” he said.

“Aye, and he can’t never forget it, the silly loon,” threw in the woman, but so good-naturedly that it was impossible, Laura felt, for Peter to take offence.

===Chinese===
她点头称是，一双严肃的眼睛端详着他，心里纳闷，他为什么这么脸红、这么坐立不安、这么不自在。听说劳拉要走这么远的路，妇人连声称「啧，啧，啧」；彼得脸红得更厉害了，又主动搭了一句。

「有一回，我自个儿差一点儿就到墨尔本了。」他说。

「可不是嘛，这只呆鹅就忘不了这个，」妇人插嘴道；不过语气那样和气，劳拉觉得，彼得听了绝不见怪。

===Original===
She gazed at the pair, speculating upon the relation they stood in to each other. She had obediently put out her hand for the apple, and now sat holding it, without attempting to eat it. It had not been Mother’s precepts alone that had weighed with her in declining it; she was mortified at the idea of being bribed, as it were, to be good, just as though she were Pin or one of the little boys. It was a punishment on her for having been so babyish as to cry; had she not been caught in the act, the woman would never have ventured to be so familiar. The very largeness and rosiness of the fruit made it hateful to her, and she turned over in her mind how she could get rid of it.

As the coach bumped along, her fellow-passengers sat back and shut their eyes. The road was shadeless; beneath the horses’ feet a thick red dust rose like smoke. The grass by the wayside, under the scattered gum trees or round the big black boulders that dotted the hillocks, was burnt to straw. In time, Laura also grew drowsy, and she was just falling into a doze when, with a jerk, the coach pulled up at the “Halfway House.” Here her companions alighted, and there were more nods and smiles from the woman.

“You eat it, my dear. I’m sure your ma won’t say nothin’,” was her last remark as she pushed the swing-door and vanished into the house, followed by Peter.

===Chinese===
她打量着这一对男女，揣度他们彼此是什么关系。她已经乖乖伸出手接过了苹果，此刻便那样捧着坐在那里，并没有要吃的意思。方才推辞时，在她心里掂分量的不单是妈妈的教训；更叫她羞恼的是，这简直像是在贿赂她学好，就仿佛她是萍，或是哪个小弟弟似的。这是对她幼稚到哭鼻子的惩罚；倘若不是被当场撞见，这妇人绝不敢这样跟她套近乎。这苹果偏偏又大又红，愈发叫她看着有气，她心里翻来覆去地盘算，怎样才能把它打发掉。

马车颠簸着往前赶，同车的两位靠上椅背，闭起了眼睛。这条路没有一处荫凉；马蹄底下腾起厚厚的红尘，像烟一样。道旁的青草——散生的桉树底下，丘岗上点缀的黑色巨石四周——都晒得枯黄如稻草。渐渐地，劳拉也瞌睡上来，正要入梦，车身猛地一顿，停在了「中途客栈」（Halfway House）门前。同车的两人在这里下了车，妇人又是几番点头微笑。

「把它吃了吧，亲爱的。你妈保准不会说什么的。」这是她临别的话；说完推开弹簧门，走进屋去不见了，彼得跟在后面。

===Original===
Then the driver’s pleasant face appeared at the window of the coach. In one hand he held a glass, in the other a bottle of lemonade.

“Here, little woman, have a drink. It’s warm work ridin’.”

Now this was quite different from the matter of the apple. Laura’s throat was parched with dust and tears. She accepted the offer gratefully, thinking as she drank how envious Pin would be, could she see her drinking bottle-lemonade.

===Chinese===
接着，车夫那张和气的脸出现在车窗外。他一只手端着玻璃杯，另一只手拿一瓶柠檬水。

「来，小闺女，喝一口吧。赶车可是件热人的活儿。」

这可同苹果的事全然两样了。劳拉的喉咙叫尘土和眼泪折腾得直冒火。她感激地接过递来的柠檬水，一面喝一面想，萍要是能瞧见她喝瓶装柠檬水，该多么眼馋啊。

===Original===
Then the jolting and rumbling began anew. No one else got in, and when they had passed the only two landmarks she knew﻿—the leprous Chinaman’s hut and the market garden of Ah Chow, who twice a week jaunted at a half-trot to the township with his hanging baskets, to supply people with vegetables﻿—when they had passed these, Laura fell asleep. She wakened with a start to find that the coach had halted to apply the brakes, at the top of the precipitous hill that led down to the railway township. In a two-wheeled buggy this was an exciting descent; but the coach jammed on both its brakes, moved like a snail, and seemed hardly able to crawl.

At the foot of the hill the little town lay sluggish in the sun. Although it was close on midday, but few people were astir in the streets; for the place had long since ceased to be an important mining centre: the chief claims were worked out; and the coming of the railway had been powerless to give it the impetus to a new life. It was always like this in these streets of low, verandahed, redbrick houses, always dull and sleepy, and such animation as there was, was invariably to be found before the doors of the many public-houses.

At one of these the coach stopped and unloaded its goods, for an interminable time. People came and looked in at the window at Laura, and she was beginning to feel alarmed lest O’Donnell, who had gone inside, had forgotten all about her having to catch the train, when out he came, wiping his lips.

===Chinese===
随后，颠簸轰响又开了头。再没有别人上车；等过了她认得的仅有的两处标志——一处是那害麻风的中国人的棚屋，一处是阿周（Ah Chow）的菜园子，此人每星期两回，挑着悬筐、迈着碎步小跑到镇上来，给各家送菜——过了这两处，劳拉便睡着了。她猛地惊醒，发现马车已停在一道陡坡的坡顶上煞住了闸，这道坡一路通到山下有火车站的镇子。坐双轮轻马车下这道坡，是桩叫人心跳的事；可驿车两头闸一齐煞死，走得像蜗牛，简直眼看就要爬不动了。

山脚下，小镇懒洋洋地卧在太阳地里。虽然已近正午，街上走动的人却寥寥无几；因为这地方早已不成其为一个重要的采矿中心：几处主要的矿场都采空了；铁路通了，也没能给它一股新生的推力。这些低矮的红砖街巷——家家带游廊——素来就是这个样子，永远沉闷困倦，仅有的那点活气，照例都聚在众多酒馆门前。

马车在其中一家酒馆门前停下卸货，一卸就是老半天。人们走过来，隔着车窗打量劳拉；奥唐奈进屋里去了，她正开始发慌，生怕他把她还得赶火车这茬忘了个干净，恰在这时他出来了，抹着嘴唇。

===Original===
“Now for the livin’ luggage!” he said with a wink, and Laura drew back in confusion from the laughter of a group of larrikins round the door.

It was indeed high time at the station; no sooner was her box dislodged and her ticket taken than the train steamed in. O’Donnell recommended her to the guard’s care; she shook hands with him and thanked him, and had just been locked into a carriage by herself when he came running down the platform again, holding in his hand, for everyone to see, the apple, which Laura believed she had safely hidden under the cushions of the coach. Red to the roots of her hair she had to receive it before a number of heads put out to see what the matter was, and she was even forced to thank O’Donnell into the bargain. Then the guard came along once more, and told her he would let no one get in beside her: she need not be afraid.

“Yes. And will you please tell me when we come to Melbourne.”

===Chinese===
「这就来卸活行李喽！」他挤挤眼说；门口围着一群小混混（larrikins），哄笑声里，劳拉臊得直往后缩。

到了车站，时间果然已经紧巴巴的了；她的箱子刚卸下来、车票刚拿到手，火车便喷着汽进了站。奥唐奈把她托付给列车员照应；她同他握了手道了谢，刚被单独锁进一节车厢，他又顺着月台跑了回来，手里高举着那只苹果给众人看——劳拉原以为已经把它妥妥帖帖藏在车座垫子底下了。当着一颗颗探出来看出了什么事的人头，她红得连头发根都发烫，只得把苹果收下，甚至还不得不反过来向奥唐奈道了谢。随后列车员又走过来，告诉她不会放任何人进她这节车厢：她用不着害怕。

「好的。还请您到时候告诉我一声，到墨尔本了。」

===Original===
Directly the train was clear of the station, she lowered a window and, taking aim at a telegraph post, threw the apple from her with all her might. Then she hung out of the window, as far out as she could, till her hat was nearly carried off. This was the first railway journey she had made by herself, and there was an intoxicating sense of freedom in being locked in, alone, within the narrow compass of the compartment. She was at liberty to do everything that had previously been forbidden her: she walked up and down the carriage, jumped from one seat to another, then lay flat on her back singing to herself, and watching the telegraph poles fly past the windows, and the wires mount and descend. But now came a station and, though the train did not stop, she sat up, in order that people might see she was travelling alone.

===Chinese===
火车刚一出站，她就摇下一扇车窗，瞄准一根电线杆，使出全身的力气把苹果掷了出去。随后她把身子探出窗外，能探多远探多远，直到帽子差点儿叫风刮走。这是她头一回独自坐火车旅行，独自被锁在这节车厢的方寸之地里，竟有一种醉人的自由感。从前一切不许她做的事，如今她都可以放开了做：她在车厢里走来走去，从一个座位蹦到另一个座位，然后仰面躺平，自己给自己唱着歌，看电线杆从窗外飞掠而过，看电线一起一伏。可是前头到站了，虽然火车并不停，她还是坐起身来，好叫人瞧见她是独自出门旅行的。

===Original===
She grew hungry and attacked her lunch, and it turned out that Mother had not provided too much after all. When she had finished, had brushed herself clean of crumbs and handled, till her fingertips were sore, the pompous half-crown she had found in her pocket, she fell to thinking of them at home, and of what they would now be doing. It was between two and three o’clock: the sun would be full on the flagstones of the back verandah; inch by inch Pin and Leppie would be driven away to find a cooler spot for their afternoon game, while little Frank slept, and Sarah splashed the dinner-dishes in the brick-floored kitchen. Mother sat sewing, and she would still be sitting there, still sewing, when the shadow of the fir tree, which at noon was shrunken like a dwarf, had stretched to giant size, and the children had opened the front gate to play in the shade of the public footpath. At the thought of these shadows, of all the familiar things she would not see again for months to come, Laura’s eyelids began to smart.

They had flashed through several stations; now they stopped; and her mind was diverted by the noise and bustle. As the train swung into motion again, she fell into a pleasanter line of thought. She painted to herself, for the hundredth time, the new life towards which she was journeying, and, as always, in the brightest colours.

===Chinese===
她饿了，便向午餐发起进攻，结果证明妈妈备下的饭食并不算多。吃完之后，她掸净身上的面包屑，又把口袋里摸到的那枚气派十足的半克朗（half-crown）银币来回摩挲，直摩挲得指尖生疼，然后便想起家里的人，想他们这会儿正在做什么。此刻是两三点钟之间：太阳正满满地晒在后游廊的石板上；萍和莱皮会被暑气一步一步撵走，去另找个凉快地方玩下午的游戏，小弗兰克睡着觉，萨拉在砖铺地的厨房里哗啦哗啦洗着饭碗。妈妈坐着做针线，还要一直坐在那里、一直做下去，直到正午缩得像侏儒的那棵枞树，把影子拉成巨人一般大，孩子们推开前院的大门，到公共人行道的荫凉里去玩。一想到这些影子，一想到往后好几个月再见不着的这一切熟识东西，劳拉的眼皮开始刺痛。

火车风驰电掣地掠过好几站；这时停了下来，嘈杂忙乱岔开了她的心思。待火车重新晃动着开动，她又坠入一条愉快的思路。她第一百次向自己描绘此去奔赴的新生活，而且一如既往，涂的全是最明亮的颜色。

===Original===
She had arrived at school, and in a spacious apartment, which was a kind of glorified Mother’s drawing-room, was being introduced to a bevy of girls. They clustered round, urgent to make the acquaintance of the newcomer, who gave her hand to each with an easy grace and an appropriate word. They were too well-bred to cast a glance at her clothes, which, however she might embellish them in fancy, Laura knew were not what they ought to be: her ulster was some years old, and so short that it did not cover the flounce of her dress, and this dress, and her hat with it, were Mother’s taste, and consequently, Laura felt sure, nobody else’s. But her new companions saw that she wore these clothes with an elegance that made up for their shortcomings; and she heard them whisper: “Isn’t she pretty? What black eyes! What lovely curls!” But she was not proud, and by her ladylike manners soon made them feel at home with her, even though they stood agape at her cleverness: none of *them* could claim to have absorbed the knowledge of a whole house. With one of her admirers she had soon formed a friendship that was the wonder of all who saw it: in deep respect the others drew back, forming a kind of allée, down which, with linked arms, the two friends sauntered, blind to everything but themselves. And having embarked thus upon her sea of dreams, Laura set sail and was speedily borne away.

===Chinese===
她已经到了学校，在一间宽敞的大屋子里——那屋子好比妈妈客厅的升华版——被人引见给一群女孩子。她们围拢上来，急切地想结识这位新同学；新同学向每一个人伸出手去，姿态从容优雅，措辞恰到好处。她们教养太好，绝不朝她的衣裳瞟一眼；可那身衣裳，任凭她在幻想里怎样装点，劳拉都清楚并不合乎应有的体统：她那件大衣已经穿了几年，短得盖不住裙子的荷叶边；这条裙子，连同那顶帽子，都是妈妈的趣味，因而——劳拉敢肯定——不会是任何别人的趣味。可她的新伙伴们看得出来，她穿这一身穿出了一种优雅，足以补衣裳本身的不足；她还听见她们悄声说：「她多漂亮啊！多黑的眼睛！多可爱的鬈发！」但她并不骄傲，很快便以大家闺秀的风度让众人跟她处得自自在在，尽管她们对她的聪明佩服得张口结舌：她们当中谁也不敢夸口，说自己已经把一整座房子的学问都吸收进了肚里。她同一位仰慕者很快结下了一段友谊，叫所有瞧见的人都惊叹不已：其余的人满怀敬意退向两旁，排成一条夹道，两位朋友挽着胳膊，顺着这条道信步而行，眼中除彼此外再无一物。就这样登上了自己的梦之海，劳拉扬起帆，不多时便被载向远方。

===Original===
“Next station you’ll be there, little girl.”

She sprang up and looked about her, with vacant eyes. This had been the last stoppage, and the train was passing through the flats. In less than two minutes she had collected her belongings, tidied her hair and put on her gloves.

Some time afterwards they steamed in alongside a gravelled platform, among the stones of which a few grass-blades grew. This was Melbourne. At the nearer end of the platform stood two ladies, one stout and elderly in bonnet and mantle, with glasses mounted on a black stick, and shortsighted, peering eyes; the other stout and comely, too, but young, with a fat, laughing face and rosy cheeks. Laura descried them a long way off; and, as the carriage swept past them, they also saw her, eager and prominent at her window. Both stared at her, and the younger lady said something, and laughed. Laura instantly connected the remark, and the amusement it caused the speaker, with the showy red lining of her hat, at which she believed their eyes had been directed. She also realised, when it was too late, that her greeting had been childish, unnecessarily effusive; for the ladies had responded only by nods. Here were two thrusts to parry at once, and Laura’s cheeks tingled. But she did not cease to smile, and she was still wearing this weak little smile, which did its best to seem easy and unconcerned, when she alighted from the train.

===Chinese===
「下一站你就到啦，小姑娘。」

她一跃而起，睁着一双空茫茫的眼睛四下张望。方才那一站已是最后一停，火车正驶过一片平坦的洼地。不到两分钟，她已收拾好随身物件，理好头发，戴上了手套。

过了一阵，火车喷着汽，驶到一条铺砾石的月台边，石缝里长着几茎青草。这就是墨尔本了。月台近端站着两位女士：一位年纪老些，身材丰腴，戴软帽、披斗篷，手执一柄黑色的长柄眼镜，一双近视的眼睛眯缝着往前探看；另一位也丰腴标致，却年轻些，生着一张胖乎乎的笑脸和红扑扑的脸颊。劳拉远远便望见了她们；车厢从她们身边掠过时，她们也望见了她——她探在车窗前，一脸急切，格外显眼。两人都盯着她看，年轻些的那位说了句什么，笑了。劳拉当即认定，那句话，连同它引出说话人那一点乐劲，都同她帽子里那扎眼的红衬里有关——她相信她们方才的眼睛正落在这上头。她也醒悟过来——可惜已经迟了——自己那声招呼太孩子气，热情得毫无必要；因为两位女士只点了点头作答。这一下两记刺来，得同时招架，劳拉的双颊火辣辣地烧起来。但她没有收起微笑；待她走下火车时，脸上还挂着那抹乏力的小小的微笑，竭力要显得从容自在、满不在乎。

## IV / 四

===Original===
The elderly lady was Laura’s godmother; she lived at Prahran, and it was at her house that Laura would sometimes spend a monthly holiday. Godmother was good to them all in a brusque, sharp-tongued fashion; but Pin was her especial favourite and she made no secret of it. Her companion on the platform was a cousin of Laura’s, of at least twice Laura’s age, who invariably struck awe into the children by her loud and ironic manner of speech. She was an independent, manly person, in spite of her plump roundnesses; she lived by herself in lodgings, and earned her own living as a clerk in an office.

The first greetings over, Godmother’s attention was entirely taken up by Laura’s box: after this had been picked out from among the other luggage, grave doubts were expressed whether it could be got on to the back seat of the pony-carriage, to which it was conveyed by a porter and the boy. Laura stood shyly by and waited, while Cousin Grace kept up the conversation by putting abrupt and embarrassing questions.

“How’s your ma?” she demanded rather than asked, in the slangy and jocular tone she employed. “I guess she’ll be thanking her stars she’s got rid of you;” at which Laura smiled uncertainly, not being sure whether Cousin Grace spoke in jest or earnest.

“I suppose you think no end of yourself going to boarding-school?” continued the latter.

“Oh no, not at all,” protested Laura with due modesty; and as both at question and answer Cousin Grace laughed boisterously, Laura was glad to hear Godmother calling: “Come, jump in. The ponies won’t stand.”

===Chinese===
那位上了年纪的太太是劳拉的教母（Godmother）；她住在普拉兰（Prahran），劳拉有时会到她家去度过每月一度的假日。教母待他们几个孩子都不错，只是作风生硬、嘴巴尖刻；不过萍是她格外偏爱的，这一点她也毫不掩饰。站台上陪着她的是劳拉的一位表姐，年纪至少比劳拉大一倍，说起话来大嗓门又带讥诮，总能叫孩子们肃然生畏。她虽则浑身圆滚滚、一团团富态，却是个独立不羁、男子汉做派的人物；她独自赁屋而居，在一家事务所里当文书，自食其力。

见面的寒暄一过，教母的心思就全在劳拉的箱子上了：箱子从一堆行李里挑出来之后，众人一本正经地犯了疑，不知它能不能弄到小马车的后座上去——搬运工和跟车的小马倌把它抬了过去。劳拉怯生生地站在一旁等着，格雷斯表姐（Cousin Grace）则用一句句突兀又叫人难堪的问话，勉强维持着谈话。

「你妈近来可好？」与其说是问，不如说是审，用的正是她那种俚俗打趣的腔调。「我猜呀，她这会儿正谢天谢地，总算把你打发掉了。」劳拉闻言笑得没个准，拿不准格雷斯表姐这话是玩笑还是当真。

「我琢磨着，你上了寄宿学校，怕是把自己看得天一般高了吧？」后者接着说。

「噢，不，可没那回事。」劳拉不失分寸地谦逊分辩道；问的答的都惹得格雷斯表姐放声大笑，劳拉幸而听见教母在喊：「来呀，上车。两匹小马站不住了。」

===Original===
Godmother was driving herself﻿—a low basket-carriage, harnessed to two buff-coloured ponies. Laura sat with her back to them. Godmother flapped the reins and said: “Get up!” but she was still fretted about the box, which was being held on behind by the boy. An inch larger, she asserted, and it would have had to be left behind. Laura eyed its battered sides uneasily. Godmother might remember, she thought, that it contained her whole wardrobe; and she wondered how many of Godmother’s own ample gowns could be compressed into so small a space.

“All my clothes are inside,” she explained; “that I shall need for months.”

“Ah, I expect your poor mother has sat up sewing herself to death, that you may be as well dressed as the rest of them,” said Godmother, and heaved a doleful sigh. But Cousin Grace laughed the wide laugh that displayed a mouthful of great healthy teeth.

“What? All your clothes in there?” she cried. “I say! You couldn’t be a queen if you hadn’t more togs than that.”

“Oh, I know,” Laura hastened to reply, and grew very red. “Queens need a lot more clothes than I’ve got.”

“Tut, tut!” said Godmother: she did not understand the allusion, which referred to a former ambition of Laura’s. “Don’t talk such nonsense to the child.”

===Chinese===
教母亲自驾车——一辆低低的篮式马车，套着两匹浅黄褐色的小马。劳拉背对着马坐在车上。教母抖了抖缰绳，喝一声：「驾！」可她还在为那只箱子烦心，小马倌正在车后按着它呢。她说，这箱子但凡再大一寸，就只好撂下不带了。劳拉不安地打量着箱子四壁磕碰的痕迹。她心想，教母可别忘了，那里头装的是她的全部行头；她又纳闷，教母自己那些肥大的长裙，能有几条塞进这么小的一方空间里去。

「我的衣裳全在里头，」她解释道，「往后好几个月要穿的全在这儿了。」

「唉，我料想你那可怜的妈妈准是夜夜熬着针线，人都缝得没命了，好叫你穿得不比她们谁差。」教母说着，发出一声悲叹。格雷斯表姐却咧开大嘴笑了，露出一口结实健康的大牙。

「什么？你所有的衣裳都在那里面？」她嚷道。「哎呀！就这么点儿行头，你可当不成王后。」

「噢，这我知道，」劳拉忙不迭地答，脸涨得通红。「王后需要的衣裳，比我的多得多呢。」

「啧啧！」教母说：她没听懂这话的典故——那说的是劳拉从前的抱负。「别跟孩子胡说八道。」

===Original===
She drove very badly, and they went by quiet by-streets to escape the main traffic: the pony-chaise wobbled at random from one side of the road to the other, obstacles looming up only just in time for Godmother to see them. The ponies shook and tossed their heads at the constant sawing of the bits, and Laura had to be continually ducking, to keep out of the way of the reins. She let the unfamiliar streets go past her in a kind of dream; and there was silence for a time, broken only by Godmother’s expostulations with the ponies, till Cousin Grace, growing tired of playing her bright eyes first on this, then on that, brought them back to Laura and studied her up and down.

“I say, who on earth trimmed your hat?” she asked almost at once.

“Mother,” answered Laura bravely, while the colour mounted to her cheeks again.

“Well, I guess she made up her mind you shouldn’t get lost as long as you wore it,” went on her cousin with disconcerting candour. “It makes you look just like a great big red double dahlia.”

“Let the child be. She looks well enough,” threw in Godmother in her snappish way. But Laura was sure that she, too disapproved; and felt more than she heard the muttered remark about “Jane always having had a taste for something gay.”

“Oh, I like the colour very much. I chose it myself,” said Laura, and looked straight at the two faces before her. But her lips twitched. She would have liked to snatch the hat from her head, to throw it in front of the ponies and hear them trample it under their hoofs. She had never wanted the scarlet lining of the big, upturned brim; in a dislike to being conspicuous which was incomprehensible to Mother, she had implored the latter to “leave it plain.” But Mother had said: “Nonsense!” and “Hold your tongue!” and “I know better,”﻿—with this result.

===Chinese===
她车驾得很糟，为了躲开大街上的车马，他们专拣清静的小街走：小马车东倒西歪地忽而奔到路这边、忽而奔到路那边，障碍物总是到最后一刻才撞进教母眼里。缰绳在马嚼子上不住地拉锯，两匹小马甩头晃脑，劳拉只得一个劲儿地缩脖闪避，免得叫缰绳扫着。她恍恍惚惚地任那些陌生的街道从眼前滑过；有一阵子谁也不作声，只听得见教母数落小马的声音，直到格雷斯表姐把那双亮眼睛看了这边又看那边、终于看腻了，才把它们收回到劳拉身上，上上下下地打量起来。

「哎，我说，你这帽子到底是谁给镶饰的？」她几乎立刻就问。

「妈妈。」劳拉硬着头皮答道，红晕又爬上了脸颊。

「嘿，我看她是拿定了主意，只要你戴着它，就保险走不丢啦，」表姐接着说，坦率得叫人下不来台。「它把你打扮得活像一朵又大又红的重瓣大丽花。」

「随孩子去吧。她这样够好了。」教母用她那呛人的口气插话道。可劳拉拿得准，教母心里也不以为然；还有那句嘀咕——『简（Jane，劳拉的母亲）向来爱挑花哨鲜亮的东西』——她觉出来的比听清的还多。

「噢，我很喜欢这颜色。是我自己挑的。」劳拉说，直直地望着面前的那两张脸。可她的嘴唇抽动了。她真想把帽子从头上扯下来，扔到小马跟前，听马蹄把它踏个稀烂。她本来就不想要那圈大翻帽檐上的猩红衬里；她天生不爱招眼，这一点妈妈百思不解，她曾央告妈妈『就让它素着吧』。可妈妈说：『胡说！』『住嘴！』『我比你懂！』——结果就是这样。

===Original===
Oh yes, she saw well enough how Godmother signed with her eyes to Cousin Grace to say no more; but she pretended not to notice, and for the remainder of the drive nobody spoke. They went past long lines of grey houses, joined one to another and built exactly alike; past large, fenced-in public parks where all kinds of odd, unfamiliar trees grew, with branches that ran right down their trunks, and bushy leaves. The broad streets were hilly; the wind, coming in puffs, met them with clouds of gritty white dust. They had just, with bent heads, their hands at their hats, passed through one of these miniature whirlwinds, when turning a corner they suddenly drew up, and the boy sprang to the ponies’ heads. Laura, who had not been expecting the end so soon, saw only a tall wooden fence; but Cousin Grace looked higher, gave a stagey shudder and cried: “Oh my eye Betty Martin! Aren’t I glad it isn’t me that’s going to school! It looks just like a prison.”

===Chinese===
噢，是的，教母怎样拿眼色示意格雷斯表姐别再往下说，她看得再清楚不过；可她只装没瞧见，余下的一路，谁也没再开口。他们驶过一长排一长排的灰房子，房子一幢挨着一幢，造得一模一样；驶过一片片围着栏杆的大公园，园里长着各种古怪、眼生的树木，枝条顺着树干一直垂到地上，叶子密簇簇的。宽阔的街道高低起伏；风一阵一阵地刮来，卷起一团团掺着沙砾的白尘，迎面扑到他们身上。他们刚低下头、用手按住帽子，穿过这样一场小小的旋风，一拐弯，车忽然停住了，小马倌抢步上前抓住马头。劳拉没料到这么快就到了，只看见一道高高的木栅栏；格雷斯表姐却往更高处望了望，做作地打了个寒噤，喊道：「哎呀我的老天爷！亏得上学的那个人不是我，我真要谢天谢地！这地方活脱脱是座监狱。」

===Original===
It certainly was an imposing building viewed from within, when the paling-gate had closed behind them. To Laura, who came from a township of one-storied brick or weatherboard houses, it seemed vast in its breadth and height, appalling in its sombre greyness. Between Godmother and Cousin Grace she walked up an asphalted path, and mounted the steps that led to a massive stone portico. The bell Godmother rang made no answering sound, but after a very few seconds the door swung back, and a slender maidservant in cap and apron stood before them. She smiled at them pleasantly, as, in Chinaman-fashion, they crossed the threshold; then, inclining her head at a murmured word from Godmother, she vanished as lightly as she had come, and they sat and looked about them. They were in a plainly furnished but very lofty waiting-room. There were two large windows. The venetian blinds had not been lowered, and the afternoon sun, beating in, displayed a shabby patch on the carpet. It showed up, too, a coating of dust that had gathered on the desk-like, central table. There was the faint, distinctive smell of strange furniture. But what impressed Laura most was the stillness. No street noises pierced the massy walls, but neither did the faintest echo of all that might be taking place in the great building itself reach their ears: they sat aloof, shut off, as it were, from the living world. And this feeling soon grew downright oppressive: it must be like this to be dead, thought Laura to herself; and inconsequently remembered a quarter of an hour she had once spent in a dentist’s anteroom: there as here the same soundless vacancy, the same anguished expectancy. Now, as then, her heart began to thump so furiously that she was afraid the others would hear it. But they, too, were subdued; though Cousin Grace tittered continually you heard only a gentle wheezing, and even Godmother expressed the hope that they would not be kept waiting long, under her breath. But minute after minute went by; there they sat and nothing happened. It began to seem as if they might sit on forever.

===Chinese===
木栅门在他们身后合上之后，从里面看，这确乎是一座气派逼人的建筑。劳拉来自一个只有单层砖房和木板房的小镇，在她眼里，这楼开阔高耸，那阴沉的灰色更叫人心惊。她走在教母和格雷斯表姐中间，踏上一条铺着沥青的小径，登上通向一座粗大石门廊的台阶。教母按铃，铃声没有任何回应，可不过几秒钟，门便向里旋开，一个头戴小帽、系着围裙的纤细女仆立在她们面前。她们像中国人过门槛似的跨进门去，她和气地朝她们笑笑；随后，听了教母低声说的一句什么话，她微微颔首，又像来时一样轻盈地隐去了，于是她们坐下来打量四周。这是一间陈设简朴但极为高敞的接待室。有两扇大窗。百叶帘没有放下，午后的阳光直射进来，照出地毯上一块磨旧的斑秃，也照出了屋子中央那张书桌似的桌子上积的一层灰。屋里有一股淡淡的、陌生家具特有的气味。然而给劳拉印象最深的，是那份寂静。街上的一切声响都透不进这厚重的墙壁，可这大楼内部无论在发生什么，最微弱的回声也传不进她们耳朵：她们仿佛被人世隔绝在外，断绝了来往。这感觉很快就变得实实在在的压迫人：人死了大概就是这样吧，劳拉暗想；随后不着边际地记起她有一次在牙医候诊室里度过的那一刻钟：那里和这里一样，同样无声的空落，同样焦灼的等待。此刻和当年一样，她的心开始狂跳，跳得她生怕别人听见。可她们两个也蔫了；尽管格雷斯表姐一直在窃笑，你也只能听见轻轻的喘气声，就连教母，也是压着嗓子表示但愿不会让她们久等。可是一分钟又一分钟过去，她们坐在那里，什么事也没有。渐渐的，倒像她们要在这里坐到地老天荒了。

===Original===
All of a sudden, from out the spacious halls of which they had caught a glimpse on arriving, brisk steps began to come towards them over the oilcloth﻿—at first as a mere tapping in the distance, then rapidly gaining in weight and decision. Laura’s palpitations reached their extreme limit﻿—another second and they might have burst her chest. Cousin Grace ceased to giggle; the door opened with a peculiar flourish; and all three rose to their feet.

The person who entered was a very stately lady; she wore a cap with black ribbons. With the door-handle still in her hand she made a slight obeisance, in which her whole body joined, afterwards to become more erect than before. Having introduced herself to Godmother as Mrs. Gurley, the Lady Superintendent of the institution, she drew up a chair, let herself down upon it, and began to converse with an air of ineffable condescension.

While she talked Laura examined her, with a child’s thirst for detail. Mrs. Gurley was large and generous of form, and she carried her head in such a haughty fashion that it made her look taller than she really was. She had a high colour, her black hair was touched with grey, her upper teeth were prominent. She wore gold eyeglasses, many rings, a long gold chain, which hung from an immense cameo brooch at her throat, and a black apron with white flowers on it, one point of which was pinned to her ample bosom. The fact that Laura had just such an apron in her box went only a very little way towards reviving her spirits; for altogether Mrs. Gurley was the most impressive person she had ever set eyes on. Beside her, Godmother was nothing but a plump, shortsighted fidgety lady.

Particularly awe-inspiring was Mrs. Gurley when she listened to another speaking. She held her head a little to one side, her teeth met her underlip and her beringed hands toyed incessantly with the long gold chain, in a manner which seemed to denote that she set little value on what was being said. Awful, too, was the habit she had of suddenly lowering her head and looking at you over the tops of her glasses: when she did this, and when her teeth came down on her lip, you would have liked to shrink to the size of a mouse. Godmother, it is true, was not afraid of her; but Cousin Grace was hushed at last and as for Laura herself, she consciously wore a fixed little simper, which was meant to put it beyond doubt that butter would not melt in her mouth.

===Chinese===
冷不丁地，从她们来时瞥见过一眼的那几间宽敞大厅里，一阵轻快的脚步声踩着油地毡朝这边来了——起初只是远处嗒嗒的轻响，随即很快地越来越沉、越来越笃定。劳拉的心悸到了极点——再有一秒钟，只怕要从胸口蹦出来了。格雷斯表姐不笑了；房门带着一种特别的甩荡劲头开了；三个人一齐站起身来。

进来的是一位十分威严的太太；她戴着一顶缀黑缎带的软帽。门把手还握在手里，她就行了个浅浅的屈膝礼，浑身上下都随着躬了躬，随后立得比先前更直。她向教母自我介绍，说自己是格利太太（Mrs. Gurley），这所学府的女学监（Lady Superintendent），然后拖过一把椅子，款款落座，带着一种说不出的居高临下的气派攀谈起来。

她说话的当儿，劳拉怀着孩子对细节的渴求打量她。格利太太身形高大丰腴，头昂得那么倨傲，以致显得比实际个头还高。她脸色红艳，黑发里掺了几茎灰白，上门牙微微前突。她戴着一副金边夹鼻眼镜，手上好些戒指，一条长长的金链从喉间一枚硕大的浮雕宝石胸针上垂下来，还系着一条印白花的黑围裙，围裙的一角用别针别在她丰满的胸前。劳拉的箱子里恰好也有这么一条围裙，可这个事实只让她的精神稍稍振作了一点儿；因为总的说来，格利太太是她平生所见最威风慑人的人物。在她旁边，教母不过是个胖乎乎、近视、毛毛躁躁的太太罢了。

格利太太听人说话的时候尤其叫人敬畏。她把头微微侧向一边，牙齿咬着下唇，戴满戒指的双手不停摆弄那条长金链，那副样子仿佛在示意：人家说的话，在她看来不值几个钱。她还有个吓人的习惯，会忽然低下头，从眼镜上沿瞅你：每逢她这样、牙齿又咬上嘴唇的时候，你恨不得缩成一只老鼠那么大。诚然，教母并不怕她；可格雷斯表姐总算也哑了声，至于劳拉自己，她自觉脸上挂着一抹僵住的小假笑，务求让人确信，她乖顺得嘴里含着黄油都化不了。

===Original===
Godmother now asked if she might say a few words in private, and the two ladies left the room. As the door closed behind them Cousin Grace began to be audible again.

“Oh, snakes!” she giggled, and her double chin spread itself. “There’s a Tartar for you! Don’t I thank my stars it’s not me that’s being shunted off here! She’ll give you what-for.”

“I don’t think so. I think she’s very nice,” said Laura staunchly, out of an instinct that made her chary of showing fear, or pain, or grief. But her heart began to bound again, for the moment in which she would be left alone.

“You see!” said Cousin Grace. “It’ll be bread and water for a week, if you can’t do *amare* first go-off﻿—not to mention the deponents.”

“What’s *amare*?” asked Laura anxiously, and her eyes grew so big that they seemed to fill her face.

But Cousin Grace only laughed till it seemed probable that she would burst her bodice; and Laura blushed, aware that she had compromised herself anew.

===Chinese===
这时教母提出可否私下说几句话，两位太太便出了房间。门在她们身后一关上，格雷斯表姐的声音又冒出来了。

「嗬，好家伙！」她咯咯地笑，双下巴都堆开了。「瞧这母夜叉！谢天谢地，被甩到这儿来的可不是我！她有你好受的。」

「我不信。我看她好得很。」劳拉语气很硬，这是出于一种本能——她向来不肯轻易露出害怕，或痛苦，或伤心。可她的心又咚咚跳起来，为了那即将独自被留下的时刻。

「瞧见没有！」格雷斯表姐说。「要是你一上来就变不出 *amare*（拉丁语的『爱』），管保一个礼拜只叫你吃面包喝白水——那些异态动词（deponents）就更别提了。」

「什么叫 *amare* 呀？」劳拉焦急地问，眼睛瞪得那么大，简直占满了整张脸。

可格雷斯表姐只是一个劲儿地笑，笑得简直要把胸衣撑破；劳拉红了脸，知道自己又露了一回怯。

===Original===
There followed a long and nervous pause.

“I bet Godmother’s asking her not to wallop you too often,” the tease had just begun afresh, when the opening of the door forced her to swallow her sentence in the middle.

Godmother would not sit down; so the dreaded moment had come.

“Now, Laura. Be a good girl and learn well, and be a comfort to your mother. Not that there’s much need to urge her to her books,” Godmother interrupted herself, turning to Mrs. Gurley. “The trouble her dear mother has always had has been to keep her from them.”

Laura glowed with pleasure. Now at least the awful personage would know that she was clever, and loved to learn. But Mrs. Gurley smiled the chilliest thinkable smile of acknowledgment, and did not reply a word.

She escorted the other to the front door, and held it open for them to pass out. Then, however, her pretence of affability faded clean away: turning her head just so far that she could look down her nose at her own shoulder, she said: “Follow me!”﻿—in a tone Mother would not have used even to Sarah.

===Chinese===
接着是一段长长的、令人心慌的静默。

「我敢打赌，教母这会儿正求她别太常揍你呢，」这番取笑才刚重新开了个头，门一开，硬生生逼得她把话咽了半截。

教母不肯再坐下；于是那叫人胆寒的时刻到了。

「好了，劳拉。做个乖孩子，好好念书，让你妈妈得到安慰。倒也不用怎么催她用功，」教母说到一半，转过身去对格利太太说，「她亲爱的妈妈一向操心的，从来是怎么把她从书本上拽开。」

劳拉喜得脸上放光。这下，那位可怕的人物总算要知道她聪明、爱学习了。可格利太太回了一个想得出的顶冷的领首微笑，一个字也没答。

她把教母送到大门口，拉开门送她们出去。可就在这时，她和颜悦色的做派褪得干干净净：她把头偏偏到恰好能越过自己的肩膀、从鼻尖往下瞧的地方，说：「跟我来！」——那腔调，连妈妈对萨拉都不曾用过。

===Original===
“Oh please, I had a box﻿—with my clothes in it!” she cried. “Oh, I hope they haven’t forgotten and taken it away again.”

But she might as well have spoken to the hatstand: Mrs. Gurley had sailed off, and was actually approaching a turn in the hall before Laura made haste to follow her and to keep further anxiety about her box to herself. They went past one staircase, round a bend into shadows as black as if, outside, no sun were shining, and began to ascend another flight of stairs, which was the widest Laura had ever seen. The banisters were as thick as your arm, and on each side of the stair-carpeting the space was broad enough for two to walk abreast: what a splendid game of trains you could have played there! On the other hand the landing windows were so high up that only a giant could have seen out of them.

These things occurred to Laura mechanically. What really occupied her, as she trudged behind, was how she could please this hard-faced woman and make her like her, for the desire to please, to be liked by all the world, was the strongest her young soul knew. And there must be a way, for Godmother had found it without difficulty.

===Chinese===
「噢，求您了，我还有一只箱子——里头装着我的衣裳！」她喊道。「噢，可别是他们忘了，又把它拉走啦。」

可她这话说给帽架听也是白说：格利太太已经扬长而去，都快走到门厅拐角了，劳拉才赶紧跟上去，把对箱子进一步的担忧咽进肚里。她们走过一道楼梯，拐了个弯，走进一片黑黢黢的暗影里，黑得仿佛外头压根儿没有太阳，然后开始登上另一道楼梯——那是劳拉生平见过的最宽的楼梯。栏杆有你的胳膊那么粗，楼梯地毯两侧各空出好宽的地界，足够两个人并排走：在那儿玩一列开火车的游戏该多带劲呀！可另一方面，楼梯平台上的窗户又开得那么高，只有巨人才瞧得见外头。

这些念头在劳拉脑子里机械地一掠而过。她跟在后头一步步走，心里真正盘算的，是怎样讨这个面色冷硬的女人欢心、让她喜欢自己，因为讨人喜欢、叫普天下的人都喜爱，是她年幼的心灵里最强烈的欲望。何况一定有法子，教母不就轻而易举地找到过嘛。

===Original===
She took two steps at once, to get nearer to the portly back in front of her.

“What a *very* large place this is!” she said in an insinuating voice.

She hoped the admiration, thus subtly expressed in the form of surprise, would flatter Mrs. Gurley, as a kind of co-proprietor; but it was evident that it did nothing of the sort: the latter seemed to have gone deaf and dumb, and marched on up the stairs, her hands clasped at her waist, her eyes fixed ahead, like a walking stone-statue.

On the top floor she led the way to a room at the end of a long passage. There were four beds in this room, a washhand-stand, a chest of drawers, and a wall cupboard. But at first sight Laura had eyes only for the familiar object that stood at the foot of one of the beds.

“Oh, *there’s* my box!” she cried, “Someone must have brought it up.”

It was unroped; she had simply to hand over the key. Mrs. Gurley went down on her knees before it, opened the lid, and began to pass the contents to Laura, directing her where to lay and hang them. Overawed by such complaisance, Laura moved nimbly about the room shaking and unfolding, taking care to be back at the box to the minute so as not to keep Mrs. Gurley waiting. And her promptness was rewarded; the stern face seemed to relax. At the mere hint of this, Laura grew warm through and through; and as she could neither control her feelings nor keep them to herself, she rushed to an extreme and overshot the mark.

===Chinese===
她一步跨两级，凑近前面那具肥壮的背影。

「这地方可——真大呀！」她用讨好的声调说。

她指望这份赞叹——赞叹就这样巧妙地化成吃惊说了出来——能奉承到格利太太，就当她是这地方的半个主人；可显然一点儿效果也没有：后者仿佛又聋又哑，径自往楼上走，双手交叠在腰前，眼睛直视前方，活像一尊走动着的石像。

到了顶层，她领头走向长廊尽头的一间屋子。屋里有四张床、一个脸盆架、一口五斗柜和一个壁柜。可劳拉一眼望去，只看见一个熟悉的物件立在其中一张床的床脚边。

「噢，我的箱子在——那儿呢！」她喊道，「准是有人把它搬上来了。」

捆绳已经解开了；她只消把钥匙交出去。格利太太在箱前跪下去，掀开箱盖，动手把里面的东西一件件递给劳拉，指点她往哪儿放、往哪儿挂。这样屈尊俯就的殷勤叫劳拉受宠若惊，她在屋里轻快地跑来跑去，抖开衣裳、展平褶皱，只小心掐着点儿准时回到箱子跟前，免得让格利太太久等。她的麻利得了报偿；那张严厉的脸似乎松动了些。光是这样一点点迹象，劳拉就浑身热乎乎的；她既管不住自己的感情，又藏不住，一下子冲过了头，越了界。

===Original===
“I’ve got an apron like that. I think they’re so pretty,” she said cordially, pointing to the one Mrs. Gurley wore.

The latter abruptly stopped her work, and, resting her hands on the sides of the box, gave Laura one of the dreaded looks over her glasses, looked at her from top to toe, and as though she were only now beginning to see her. There was a pause, a momentary suspension of the breath, which Laura soon learned to expect before a rebuke.
