But when Mr. Strachey left the field of personal narration and went on to the moral aspects of the affair, Laura ceased to be gripped by him, and turned anew to study the pale, dogged face of the accused, though she had to crane her neck to do it. Before such a stony mask as this, she was driven to imagine what must be going on behind it; and, while thus engrossed, she felt her arm angrily tweaked. It was Tilly.
===Chinese===
劳拉没法把眼睛从这场面上挪开：她的目光灼灼地燃烧着好奇，从安妮·约翰斯（Annie Johns）身上移到斯特雷奇先生身上，又移回这个可怜的小偷身上。校长先就犯罪问题泛泛讲了几句开场白，接着转到眼前的案子，细细描述起来，劳拉被他的口才迷住了，目不转睛地望着他。他把一切都讲得活灵活现；她听得出神，欣赏他的一处处妙笔，欣赏他把高潮层层推起的娴熟手法。可是话又说回来，她自己尝过穷的滋味——安妮·约翰斯从前正是那样穷过来的。她懂得，一个落雨的早晨拿不出电车钱是什么滋味——据斯特雷奇先生说，这正是这几桩偷窃的原动力——因为一家糖果店朝你伸出了章鱼的腕足。她还能打着寒噤想象到：头几枚便士丢了没被察觉，再往下偷三便士币，再从三便士偷到六便士，该是多么顺当。尤其因为这些钱，无一例外，都是从不缺钱的口袋里拿的。劳拉敢肯定，这并不是像斯特雷奇先生以为的那样，为了免得暴露，而是因为对钱多得那种地步的人来说，几枚零星硬币根本算不了什么。她不知道别人在这点上是不是都跟他想的一样。老师们又是怎么想的呢？——她的眼睛扫过那一排人，想从脸上读出各人的意见。可那些脸还是一样地板着。她想，只有好心的老查普曼看上去有点难过，还有齐林斯基小姐——不错，齐林斯基小姐在哭呢！这个发现让劳拉一阵兴奋——正像看戏的时候，有一位看客落了泪，反倒叫邻座的兴致更浓。可是等到斯特雷奇先生撇开一桩桩具体情节，转到这件事的道德层面上来，劳拉便觉得抓不住他了，重新回过头去研究被告那张苍白执拗的脸，虽然得伸长脖子才看得见。对着这样一张石头面具般的脸，她不由得要去想象面具后面正翻腾着什么；正想得出神，她觉得胳膊上被人怒气冲冲地拧了一把。是蒂莉。

===Original===
“You *are* a beast to stare like that!”

“I’m not staring.”

She turned her eyes away at once, more than half believing her own words; and then, for some seconds, she tried to do what was expected of her: to feel a decent unconcern. At her back, Bertha’s purry crying went steadily on. What on earth did she cry for? She had certainly not heard a word Mr. Strachey said. Laura fidgeted in her seat, and stole a side-glance at Tilly’s profile. She could not, really could not miss the last scene of all, when, in masterly fashion, the Principal was gathering the threads together. And so, feeling rather like “Peeping Tom,” she cautiously raised her eyes again, and this time managed to use them without turning her head.

All other eyes were still charitably lowered. Several girls were crying now, but without a sound. And, as the last, awful moments drew near, even Bertha was hushed, and of all the odd hundreds of throats not one dared to cough. Laura’s heart began to palpitate, for she felt the approach of the final climax, Mr. Strachey’s periods growing ever slower and more massive.
===Chinese===
「你这么死盯着人看，真*是*头畜生！」

「我没有盯着看。」

她立刻把眼睛挪开，倒有七八分相信自己的话；随后有那么几秒钟，她试着去做大家指望她做的事：装出一副得体的漠不关心。她背后，伯莎那咕噜咕噜像猫打呼噜似的哭声还在不住气地进行。她到底哭个什么劲呢？斯特雷奇先生说的话，她分明一个字也没听进去。劳拉在座位上坐不住，偷偷侧眼瞟了瞟蒂莉的侧脸。可最后一场——校长正以娴熟的手段收拢千头万绪——她实在、实在舍不得错过。于是，自觉颇有几分『偷窥的汤姆』（Peeping Tom）的味道，她又小心翼翼地抬起眼睛，这一回总算学会了不转头也能看。

所有旁人的眼睛仍旧善解人意地垂着。这会儿已有好几个姑娘在哭，但一声不响。而随着那可怕的最后时刻逼近，连伯莎也收了声，数不上号的好几百条喉咙，没有一条敢咳嗽一下。劳拉的心开始怦怦狂跳，因为她觉出最后的高潮临近了：斯特雷奇先生的句子越来越迟缓，越来越厚重。

===Original===
When, after a burst of eloquence which, the child felt, would not have shamed a Bishop, the Principal drew himself up to his full height, and, with uplifted arm, thundered forth: “Herewith, Miss Annie Johns, I publicly expel you from the school! Leave it, now, this moment, and never darken its doors again!”﻿—when this happened, Laura was shot through by an ecstatic quiver, such as she had felt once only in her life before; and that was when a beautiful, golden-haired Hamlet, who had held a Ballarat theatre entranced for a whole evening, fell dead by Laertes’ sword, to the rousing plaudits of the house. Breathing unevenly, she watched, lynx-eyed, every inch of Annie Johns’ progress: watched her pick up her books, edge out of her seat and sidle through the rows of desks; watched her walk to the door with short jerky movements, mount the two steps that led to it, fumble with the handle, turn it, and vanish from sight; and when it was all over, and there was nothing more to see, she fell back in her seat with an audible sigh.
===Chinese===
校长先倾泻出一阵雄辩——孩子觉得，这口才便是主教听了也不丢份——然后挺直了整个身子，高举手臂，雷鸣般喝道：「特此，安妮·约翰斯小姐，我当众将你开除出校！离开这里，马上，此刻就走，永远不许再登这学校的门！」——当这一切发生的时候，劳拉浑身被一阵销魂的战栗贯穿，这样的感觉她平生只体味过一回；那是在巴拉腊特（Ballarat），一位俊美的金发哈姆雷特让一家剧院的观众痴迷了整整一晚，末了死在雷欧提斯（Laertes）的剑下，博得满场雷动的喝彩。她呼吸不匀，目光山猫般尖利，盯住安妮·约翰斯一路的每一寸动静：看她收起书本，侧身挤出座位，贴着一排排课桌挨挨蹭蹭地溜过去；看她迈着细碎僵直的步子走向门口，登上通到门口的两级台阶，摸索门把手，拧开，从视线里消失；等一切都完了，再没什么可看的了，她跌坐回椅子上，发出一声人人听得见的长叹。

===Original===
It was too late after this for the winding of the snaky line about the streets and parks of East Melbourne, which constituted the boarders’ daily exercise. They were despatched to stretch their legs in the garden. Here, as they walked round lawns and tennis-courts, they discussed the main event of the afternoon, and were a little more vociferous than usual, in an attempt to shake off the remembrance of a very unpleasant half-hour.

“I bet you Sandy rather enjoyed kicking up that shindy.”

“*Did* you see Puggy’s boots again? Girls, he *must* take twelves!”

“And that old blubber of a Ziely’s handkerchief! It was filthy. I told you yesterday I was sure she never washed her neck.”
===Chinese===
这之后再来绕着东墨尔本的街道和公园蜿蜒行进那条长蛇阵——那是寄宿生每天的散步功课——已经太迟了。她们被打发到花园里去活动活动腿脚。在那里，她们绕着草坪和网球场边走边议下午那件大事，嗓门比平常响了一些，想把那极不愉快的半个钟头的记忆甩掉。

「我敢打赌，桑迪（Sandy，姑娘们给校长的诨名）闹这一出，心里多半还挺受用。」

「你们*到底*又见着普吉（Puggy，普夫森博士的诨名）那双靴子了没有？姑娘们，他*准*得穿十二号的！」

「还有老哭包齐莉那条手绢！脏透了。我昨天就说过，她准是连脖子都不洗。」

===Original===
Bertha, whose tears had dried as rapidly as sea-spray, gave Laura a dig in the ribs. “What’s up with you, old Tweedledum? You’re as glum as a lubra.”

“No, I’m not.”

“It’s my belief that Laura was sorry for that pig,” threw in Tilly.

“Indeed I wasn’t!” said Laura indignantly.

“Sorry for a thief?”

“I tell you I *wasn’t*!”﻿—and this was true. Among the divers feelings Laura had experienced that afternoon, pity had not been included.
===Chinese===
伯莎的眼泪干得像海上的浪沫一样快，这会儿捅了捅劳拉的肋部。「你怎么啦，老特威德尔当？绷着张脸闷闷不乐的，活像个土著女人（lubra）似的。」

「我才没有。」

「我看哪，劳拉是可怜了那头猪。」蒂莉插嘴道。

「我才没有呢！」劳拉愤愤地说。

「可怜一个小偷？」

「我告诉你，我*没有*！」——这话不假。那天下午劳拉体味过的种种情绪里，确实不包括怜悯。

===Original===
“If you want to be chums with such a mangy beast, you’d better go to school in a lockup.”

“I don’t know what my father’d say, if he knew I’d been in the same class as a pickpocket,” said the daughter of a minister from Brisbane. “I guess he wouldn’t have let me stop here a week.”

Laura went one better. “My mother wouldn’t have let me stop a day.”

Those standing by laughed, and a girl from the Riverina said: “Oh, no, of course not!” in a tone that made Laura wince and regret her readiness.
===Chinese===
「你要是想跟这么个生癞的畜生交朋友，顶好上拘留所里去上学。」

「要是我父亲知道我跟一个扒手同过班，真不知道他会说什么呢，」一位布里斯班（Brisbane）牧师的女儿说。「我想他连一个星期也不会让我在这儿待下去。」

劳拉更进了一步。「我妈妈连一天也不会让我待。」

站在旁边的人都笑了，一个里弗赖纳（Riverina）来的姑娘说：「哦，那当然不会！」那口气叫劳拉缩了一下，后悔自己嘴快。

===Original===
Before tea, she had to practise. The piano stood in an outside classroom, where no one could hear whether she was diligent or idle, and she soon gave up playing and went to the window. Here, having dusted the gritty sill with her petticoat, she leaned her chin on her two palms and stared out into the sunbaked garden. It was empty now, and very still. The streets that lay behind the high palings were deserted in the drowsy heat; the only sound to be heard was a gentle tinkling to vespers in the neighbouring Catholic Seminary. Leaning thus on her elbows, and balancing herself first on her heels, then on her toes, Laura went on, in desultory fashion, with the thoughts that had been set in motion during the afternoon. She wondered where Annie Johns was now, and what she was doing; wondered how she had faced her mother, and what her father had said to her. All the rest of them had gone back at once to their everyday life; Annie Johns alone was cut adrift. What would happen to her? Would she perhaps be turned out of the house?﻿ ﻿… into the streets?﻿—and Laura had a lively vision of the guilty creature, in rags and tatters, slinking along walls and sleeping under bridges, eternally moved on by a ruthless London policeman (her only knowledge of extreme destitution being derived from the woeful tale of “Little Jo”). And to think that the beginning of it all had been the want of a trumpery tram-fare. How safe the other girls were! No wonder they could allow themselves to feel shocked and outraged; none of *them* knew what it was not to have threepence in your pocket. While she, Laura﻿ ﻿… Yes, and it must be this same incriminating acquaintance with poverty that made her feel differently about Annie Johns and what she had done. For her feelings *had* been different﻿—there was no denying that. Did she now think back over the half-hour spent in Number One, and act honest Injun with herself, she had to admit that her companions’ indignant and horrified aversion to the crime had not been hers, let alone their decent indifference towards the criminal. No, to be candid, she had been deeply interested in the whole affair, had even managed to extract an unseemly amount of entertainment from it. And that, of course, should not have been. It was partly Mr. Strachey’s fault, for making it so dramatic; but none the less she genuinely despised herself, for having such a queer inside.
===Chinese===
吃茶点之前，她还得练琴。钢琴摆在一间外头的教室里，谁也听不出她是勤快还是偷懒，不多会儿她就不弹了，走到窗前。在这里，她用衬裙掸了掸窗台上硌人的沙尘，下巴搁在两只手掌上，凝望着外面晒得发烫的花园。花园里此刻空荡荡的，非常寂静。高板墙外的街道在恹恹的暑气里杳无人迹；唯一听得见的，是隔壁天主教神学院里一阵轻柔的晚祷铃声。她这样支着胳膊肘，先脚跟后脚尖地晃着身子找平衡，有一搭没一搭地接着想下午转动起来的那些念头。她不知道安妮·约翰斯这会儿在哪儿，正在做什么；不知道她怎样面对她的母亲，她父亲又对她说了什么。别人全都立刻回到了各自的日常生活里去；唯独安妮·约翰斯被砍断了缆绳，孤零零漂了出去。她会落到什么地步呢？会不会被撵出家门？……撵到街上去？——劳拉活灵活现地看见那个有罪的人衣衫褴褛，贴着墙根溜，睡在桥洞底下，永远被一个狠心的伦敦警察赶来赶去（她对赤贫的全部认识，都得自『小乔』（Little Jo）那个惨兮兮的故事）。而这一切的起头，竟不过是区区一笔电车钱。别的姑娘们多稳当啊！难怪她们尽可以又震惊又愤慨；她们中间*谁*也没有尝过口袋里连三便士都没有的滋味。而她，劳拉……是啊，想必正是这同一种会招来罪嫌的、对贫穷的熟识，使她对安妮·约翰斯和她的所作所为另有一番感受。她的感受*的确*与众不同——这没法否认。此刻她若回想在一号教室里度过的那半个钟头，跟自己来一句『印第安人的老实话』（honest Injun，孩子赌咒用语，意即千真万确），就得承认：同伴们对那桩罪行的义愤和惊骇的憎恶，她不曾有过；至于她们对罪犯那份得体的冷漠，就更不用说了。不，说句实话，她对整件事情深感兴趣，甚至想法子从中榨出了多得不成体统的乐趣。而这当然是不应该的。这有一部分得怪斯特雷奇先生，谁让他把事情弄得这样戏剧化；可尽管如此，她还是真心实意地瞧不起自己，怪自己里面竟装着这么一副古怪的心肠。

===Original===
“Pig﻿—pig﻿—pig!” she muttered under her breath, and wrinkled her nose in a grimace.

The real reason of her pleasurable absorption was, she supposed, that she had understood Annie Johns’ motive better than anyone else. Well, she had had no business to understand﻿—that was the long and the short of it: nice-minded girls found such a thing impossible, and turned incuriously away. And her companions had been quick to recognise her difference of attitude, or they would never have dared to accuse her of sympathy with the thief, or to doubt her chorusing assertion with a sneer. For them, the gap was not very wide between understanding and doing likewise. And they were certainly right. Oh! the last wish in the world she had was to range herself on the side of the sinner; she longed to see eye to eye with her comrades﻿—if she had only known how to do it. For there was no saying where it might lead you, if you persisted in having odd and peculiar notions; you might even end by being wicked yourself. Let her take a lesson in time from Annie’s fate. For, beginning perhaps with ideas that were no more unlike those of her schoolfellows than were Laura’s own, Annie was now a branded thief and an outcast. And the child’s feelings, as she stood at the window, were not very far removed from prayer. Had they found words, they would have taken the form of an entreaty that she might be preserved from having thoughts that were different from other people’s; that she might be made to feel as she ought to feel, in a proper, ladylike way﻿—and especially did she see a companion convicted of crime.
===Chinese===
「猪——猪——猪！」她压着嗓子咕哝，皱起鼻子做了个鬼脸。

她那样乐在其中，真正的缘故，她想，是她比谁都更明白安妮·约翰斯的动机。可是，她本来就不该明白——长话短说，症结就在这里：心思干净的好姑娘只会觉得这种事绝无可能，便不好奇地掉头而去。而她的同伴们一眼就认出了她态度上的不同，否则她们绝不敢指责她同情小偷，也不敢冷笑着不信她随大伙一齐喊出的表白。在她们看来，从理解到照样去做，中间隔的沟并不宽。而她们肯定是对的。哦！她天底下最最不愿意的，就是把自己划到罪人那一边去；她巴望着跟同伴们所见完全一致——只要她知道该怎样做到就好了。因为，一个人要是执意抱着古里古怪的念头，天晓得会落到什么地步；说不定到头来自己就变坏了。还是趁早从安妮的下场里领个教训吧。因为安妮起头的那些念头，也许并不比劳拉自己的念头离同学们的更远，如今却成了打了烙印的小偷、被人摈弃的人。孩子站在窗前，这时候的心情离祈祷已经相去不远。这些心情若能找到言辞，便会化作这样的恳求：求老天保佑她，别让她生出跟旁人两样的想法；求老天叫她按理该有的感觉去感觉，用一种规规矩矩、淑女式的方式——尤其是在亲眼看见一个同伴被定罪的时候。

===Original===
Below all this, in subconscious depths, a chord of fear seemed to have been struck in her as well﻿—the fear of stony faces, drooped lids, and stretched, pointing fingers. For that night she started up, with a cry, from dreaming that not Annie Johns but she was being expelled; that an army of spear-like first fingers was marching towards her, and that, try as she would, she could not get her limp, heavy legs to bear her to the schoolroom door.

And this dream often returned.
===Chinese===
在这一切底下，在意识的深水处，她心里似乎也同样被敲响了一根恐惧的弦——怕一张张铁石的面孔，一双双低垂的眼皮，一根根直挺挺指着人的手指。因为当天夜里，她叫了一声惊坐起来，梦见被开除的不是安妮·约翰斯，而是她自己；一支标枪般的食指组成的大军正朝她开过来，而她无论怎么使劲，也使不动那两条瘫软沉重的腿，把自己挪到教室门口去。

这梦后来还常常回来。

## XIII / 十三

===Original===
On her honourable promotion the following Christmas﻿—she mounted two forms this time﻿—Laura was a thin, middle-sized girl of thirteen, who still did not look her age. The curls had vanished. In their place hung a long, dark plait, which she bound by choice with a red ribbon.

Tilly was the only one of her intimates who skipped a class with her; hence she was thrown more exclusively than before on Tilly’s companionship; for it was a melancholy fact: if you were not in the same class as the girl who was your friend, your interests and hers were soon fatally sundered. On their former companions, Tilly and Laura, from their new perch, could not but look down: the two had masters now for all subjects; Euclid loomed large; Latin was no longer bounded by the *First Principia*; and they fussed considerably, in the others’ hearing, over the difficulties of the little blue books that began: *Gallia est omnis divisa in partes tres*.

In the beginning, they held very close together; for their new fellows were inclined to stand on their dignity with the pair of interlopers from Class Two. They were all older than Tilly and Laura, and thought themselves wiser: here were girls of sixteen and seventeen years of age, some of whom would progress no farther along the high-road of education. As for the boarders who sat in this form, they made up a jealous little clique, and it was some time before the younger couple could discover the secret bond.

===Chinese===
到第二年圣诞节光荣升班时——她这回连跳了两级——劳拉已是十三岁，一个瘦瘦的、中等身材的姑娘，看上去仍旧不像她的实际年龄。一头鬈发不见了，原处垂着一条长长的深色发辫，她特意用一根红丝带把它扎住。

她的密友当中，只有蒂莉随她一起跳了班；于是她比从前更加只能专靠蒂莉做伴；因为这是一桩令人黯然的事实：你若不和你那位朋友同班，你的兴趣和她的兴趣，不久便要无可挽回地分道扬镳。对旧日的伙伴，蒂莉和劳拉站在新的高处，不能不带有几分俯视：她们如今门门功课都有男教师执教；欧几里得赫然登场；拉丁文也不再囿于《初级拉丁读本》第一部；而那些开篇便是 *Gallia est omnis divisa in partes tres*（高卢全境分作三部分）的蓝色小书，她们还特意当着旁人的面，大叹其难。

起初，她们两个紧靠在一起；因为新班里的同学，对第二班闯进来的这一对不速之客，动不动就要端架子。她们个个都比蒂莉和劳拉年长，也自以为更聪明：坐在眼前的，是十六七岁的姑娘，其中有几位，在教育的大路上再也不会向前挪动一步了。至于坐进这一班的寄宿生，她们结成了一个眼红排外的小圈子，这一对小的过了好一阵子，才探出其中暗结的纽带。

===Original===
Then, one morning, the two were sitting with a few others on the verandah bench, looking over their lessons for the day. Mrs. Gurley had snatched a moment’s rest there, on her way to the secretary’s office, and as long as she allowed her withering eye to play upon things and people, the girls conned their pages with a great show of industry. But no sooner had she sailed away than Kate Horner leant forward and called to Maria Morell, who was at the other end of the seat: “I say, Maria, Genesis 51:32.”﻿—She held an open Bible in her hand.

Maria Morell frowned caution. “Dash it, Kate, mind those kids!”

“Oh, they won’t savvy.”

But Laura’s eyes were saucers of curiosity, for Tilly, who kept her long lashes lowered, had given her a furious nudge. With a wink and a beck to each other, the bigger girls got up and went away.

===Chinese===
后来，一天早晨，两个姑娘和另外几个人坐在游廊的长凳上，温习当天的功课。格利太太在去秘书办公室的路上，正在那儿偷闲歇一会儿；只要她那双煞人的眼睛还在人和物上逡巡，姑娘们便做出一副勤奋非常的样子，咿咿唔唔地诵读书页。可她刚一挺身扬长而去，凯特·霍纳便探身朝长凳另一头的玛丽亚·莫雷尔唤道：「我说，玛丽亚，《创世记》第 51 章第 32 节。」——她手里摊着一本《圣经》。

玛丽亚·莫雷尔皱起眉头，示意她谨慎。「该死，凯特，当心这几个小家伙！」

「哦，她们听不出门道的。」

可劳拉的两只眼睛好奇得瞪成了茶碟，因为蒂莉垂着长长的睫毛，在旁边狠狠捅了她一下。大姑娘们彼此使了个眼色、点了点头，起身走开了。

===Original===
“I say, what did you poke me so hard for?” inquired Laura as she and Tilly followed in their wake, at the clanging of the public prayer-bell.

“You soft, didn’t you hear what she said?”

“Of course I did”﻿—and Laura repeated the reference.

“Let’s look it up then.” Under cover of the prayer Tilly sought it out, and together they bent their heads over it.

===Chinese===
「哎，你干吗这么狠狠地捅我？」待到公祷的钟声铿然敲响，劳拉同蒂莉跟在她们身后走时，这样问道。

「你个木头，没听见她刚才说的什么吗？」

「我当然听见了。」——劳拉把那章那节复述了一遍。

「那就查查看。」蒂莉借着祈祷声的掩护翻出了那一处，两个人便一同低头凑上去。

===Original===
On this occasion, Tilly was more knowing than Laura; but on this alone; for when Laura once grasped what they were driving at, she was as nimble-witted as any.

Only a day or two later it was she who, in face of Kate and Maria, invited Tilly to turn up chapter and verse.

Both the elder girls burst out laughing.

“By dad!” cried Kate Horner, and smacked her thigh. “This kid knows a thing or two.”

“You bet! I told you she wasn’t born yesterday.”﻿—And Maria laid her arm round Laura’s shoulders.

Thus was Laura encouraged, put on her mettle; and soon there was no more audacious Bible-reader in the class than she.

===Chinese===
这一回，蒂莉比劳拉内行；但也只有这一回；因为劳拉一旦领会了她们话里的用意，她脑子的灵光便不输任何人。

不过一两天之后，当着凯特和玛丽亚的面，报出一个章节、请蒂莉去查的，正是她本人。

两个大姑娘顿时哈哈大笑。

「好家伙！」凯特·霍纳喊道，一巴掌拍在大腿上。「这小家伙还真懂点儿门道。」

「那还用说！我不是说过嘛，她可不是昨天才出世的。」——说着，玛丽亚伸出胳膊搂住劳拉的肩膀。

劳拉就这样受了鼓舞，好胜心也给激发起来；不久，全班再没有比她更大胆的查经人了。

===Original===
The girls were thrown thus upon the Book of Books for their contraband knowledge, since it was the only frankly outspoken piece of literature allowed within the College walls: the classics studied were rigidly expurgated; the school library was kept so dull that no one over the age of ten much cared to borrow a volume from it. And, by fair means or unfair, it was necessary to obtain information on matters of sex; for girls most of whom were well across the threshold of womanhood the subject had an invincible fascination.

Such knowledge as they possessed was a strange jumble, picked up at random: in one direction they were well primed; in another, supremely ignorant. Thus, though they received lectures on what was called “Physiology,” and for these were required to commit to memory the name of every bone and artery in the body, yet all that related to a woman’s special organs and chief natural function was studiously ignored. The subject being thus chastely shrouded in mystery, they were thrown back on guesswork and speculation﻿—with the quaintest results. The fancies woven by quite big girls, for instance, round the physical feat of bringing a child into the world, would have supplied material for a volume of fairytales. On many a summer evening at this time, in a nook of the garden, heads of all shades might have been seen pressed as close together as a cluster of settled bees; and like the humming of bees, too, were the busy whisperings and subdued buzzes of laughter that accompanied this hot discussion of the “how”﻿—as a living answer to which, each of them would probably some day walk the world. Innumerable theories were afloat, one more fantastic than another; and the wilder the conjecture, the greater was the respect and applause it gained.

===Chinese===
姑娘们的违禁知识，就这样只能到这书中之书里去讨，因为在学院高墙之内准许存在的文学作品里，唯有它是直言不讳的一部：课上研读的古典著作，一律经过严格的删削净化；学校图书馆又办得枯燥透顶，十岁开外的人没谁乐意从里面借一册书。而关于性的事，无论用正当还是不正当的手段，总得弄到些消息不可；对于这伙多半早已跨过少女时代门槛的姑娘，这个题目有着一股不可战胜的魔力。

她们所拥有的这一星半点知识，是个信手拾来、胡乱拼起的奇怪大杂烩：朝这一个方向，她们装得满满当当；朝另一个方向，又无知到无以复加。譬如说，她们虽然听所谓『生理学』的讲课，为此还得把周身每一块骨头、每一条动脉的名称一一背熟，但凡涉及女性特有器官与主要天然机能的一切，都被刻意绕开，只字不提。这个题目既然被如此贞洁地裹进神秘之中，她们便只好退回到瞎猜与臆想上去——结出的果子也怪诞到了家。比方说，好几个挺大的姑娘围绕着把孩子生到世上这桩身体上的壮举编织出来的种种奇想，尽够写成一整本童话书。在这个时期，多少个夏日的黄昏，在花园的一处角落里，都可以看见各色的脑袋挤作一团，密得像一窝落定的蜜蜂；伴着对那个『怎样』的热烈讨论的，是忙碌的耳语和压得低低的嗡嗡笑语，也正像蜂群的嗡营——而作为这个问题活生生的答案，她们每个人，日后多半都要亲身行走于人世。数不清的理论四处漂浮，一个比一个想入非非；而且猜想越是荒诞离奇，博得的敬意和喝彩就越大。

===Original===
On the other hand, of less profitable information they had amassed a goodly store. Girls who came from upcountry could tell a lively tale of the artless habits of the blacks; others, who were at home in mining towns, described the doings in Chinese camps﻿—those unavoidable concomitants of gold-grubbing settlements; rhymes circulated that would have staggered a back-blocker; while the governesses were without exception, young and old, kindly and unkindly, laid under such flamboyant suspicions as the poor ladies had, for certain, never heard breathed﻿—since their own impudent schooldays.

This dabbling in the illicit﻿—it had little in common with the opener grime of the ordinary schoolboy﻿—did not even widen the outlook of these girls. For it was something to hush up and keep hidden away, to have qualms, even among themselves, about knowing; and, like all knowledge that fungus-like shrinks from the sun, it was stunted and unlovely. Their minds were warped by it, their vision was distorted: viewed through its lens, the most natural human relations appeared unnatural. Thus, not the primmest patterns of family life could hope for mercy in their eyes; over the family, too, man, as read by these young rigorists, was held to leave his serpent’s trail of desire.

===Chinese===
另一方面，不大有益处的知识，她们倒囤下了可观的一库。从内地乡下来的姑娘，能把当地原住民天真直露的习性讲成一段活故事；另一些在矿镇上土生土长的，则描绘华人营里的种种行径——那是淘金聚落甩不掉的必然伴生物；流传开的顺口溜，连荒僻内地的老住户听了也要咋舌；至于那些女教师，则不论老少、不论和气不和气，无一例外都被安上了种种耸人听闻的嫌疑——这些苦命的女人自打度过自己那无法无天的学生时代以来，肯定还不曾听人这样咬着耳朵讲过。

这种对违禁之物的沾染——它同寻常男学生公然挂在嘴边的乌七八糟并无多少共通之处——甚至连这些姑娘的眼界也未能拓宽。因为这是一桩要捂着盖着、藏藏掖掖的事，即便在自己人中间，知道了也要心虚犯嘀咕；而且，像一切如真菌般畏避阳光的知识一样，它是畸零而丑陋的。她们的心思被它扭歪了，眼光被它折曲了：透过这副镜片望去，最自然的人伦关系也显得不自然。于是，连最古板守礼的家庭生活样板，也休想在她们眼里得到宽宥；在这些年轻严峻派读来，就是在家庭之内，男人也被认定要拖出他那条欲望的蛇迹。

===Original===
For out of it all rose the vague, crude picture of woman as the prey of man. Man was animal, a composite of lust and cruelty, with no aim but that of brutally taking his pleasure: something monstrous, yet to be adored; annihilating, yet to be sought after; something to flee and, at the same time, to entice, with every art at one’s disposal.

As long as it was solely a question of clandestine knowledge and ingenious surmisings, Laura went merrily with the rest: here no barrier shut her off from her companions. Always a very inquisitive little girl, she was now agog to learn new lore. Her mind, in this direction, was like a clean but highly sensitised plate. And partly because of her previous entire ignorance, partly because of her extreme receptiveness, she soon outstripped her comrades, and before long, was one of the most skilful improvisers of the group: a dexterous theorist; a wicked little adept at innuendo.

===Chinese===
因为从这一切之中，升起了一幅模糊而粗粝的图景：女人是男人的猎物。男人是动物，是淫欲与残酷的复合体，除了粗暴地取乐再无别的目的：是某种怪物，却要受人崇拜；是毁灭一切的，却偏惹人追逐；是应当躲避的东西，同时又是不惜使出浑身解数也要去诱惹的东西。

只要事情还只限于偷来的知识和巧妙的臆测，劳拉便同大伙儿一道走得欢天喜地：在这里，没有一道栅栏把她挡在同伴之外。她素来是个极爱打听的姑娘，这时更是心痒难熬地要学新的学问。她的头脑在这个方向上，就像一块干净却高度感光的底片。一半由于先前的全然无知，一半由于那极端的易于接受，她不久便把同伴们甩在了身后，没多久，就成了这群人里最老练的即兴编造手之一：一个灵巧的理论家；一个坏心眼的小行家，最会含沙射影。

===Original===
But that was all; a step farther, and she ran her head against a stone wall. For the invisible yeast that brought this ferment of natural curiosity to pass, was the girls’ intense interest in the opposite sex: a penned-up interest that clamoured for an outlet; an interest which, in the life of these prospective mothers, had already usurped the main place. Laura, on the other hand, had so far had scant experience of boys of a desirable age, nor any liking for such as she had known; indeed she still held to her childish opinion that they were “silly”﻿—feckless creatures, in spite of their greater strength and size﻿—or downright disagreeable and antagonistic, like Godmother’s Erwin and Marmaduke. No breath of their possible dangerous fascination had hitherto reached her. Hence, an experience that came her way, at the beginning of the autumn was of the nature of an awakening.

===Chinese===
但也仅止于此；再往前一步，她便一头撞上了石墙。因为使这股天然好奇的发酵得以发生的无形酵母，是姑娘们对异性的强烈兴趣：一种被圈禁起来的兴趣，吵嚷着要一个出口；一种在这些未来母亲的生活里早已篡占了头把交椅的兴趣。劳拉呢，到目前为止，几乎没有接触过年岁相当的男孩，对认识的那几个也毫无好感；她甚至仍旧抱着孩童时代的见解，认为他们『傻气』——尽管力气更大、个头更高，到底是些没出息的东西——再不然就干脆叫人讨厌、叫人对立，像教母家的欧文和马默杜克。男孩们可能怀有的那种危险的魅力，至今连一丝风声也不曾吹到她耳边。因此，入秋时分落到她身上的一段经历，性质上便无异于一场觉醒。

## XIV / 十四

===Original===
“My cousin Bob’s awfully gone on you.”

Laura gaped at Tilly, in crimson disbelief. “But I’ve never spoken to him!”

“Doesn’t count. He’s seen you in church.”

“Go on!﻿—you’re stuffing.”

“Word of honour!﻿—And I’ve promised him to ask aunt if I can bring you with me to lunch next Saturday.”

Laura looked forward to this day with mixed feelings. She was flattered at being invited to the big house in town where Tilly’s relatives lived; but she felt embarrassed at the prospect, and she had not the least idea what a boy who was “gone” on you would expect you to be or to do. Bob was a beautiful youth of seventeen, tall, and dark, and slender, with milk-white teeth and Spanish eyes; and Laura’s mouth dried up when she thought of perhaps having to be sprightly or coquettish with him.

===Chinese===
「我表哥鲍勃（Bob）迷你迷得不得了。」

劳拉瞪着蒂莉，涨红了一张满是难以置信的脸。「可我从来没跟他说过话呀！」

「那不算数。他在教堂里见过你。」

「去你的！——你净瞎编。」

「凭名誉起誓！——我已经答应了替他去问姨妈，下星期六能不能带你跟我一起去吃午饭。」

劳拉盼着这一天，心情五味杂陈。受到邀请，到蒂莉亲戚所住的城里大宅去做客，她自觉受宠若惊；可想到那场面又发窘，再说，一个『迷』上了你的男孩子会指望你怎么个样子、怎么个做派，她心里连一点谱也没有。鲍勃是个十七岁的漂亮少年，高挑、黝黑、清瘦，一口牛奶白的牙齿，一双西班牙式的大眼睛；劳拉一想到也许得当着他的面娇俏活泼、卖弄风情，嘴里就发干。

===Original===
On the eventful morning Tilly came to her room while she was dressing, and eyed her critically.

“Oh, I say, don’t put on that brown hat﻿ ﻿… for mercy’s sake! Bob can’t stand brown.”

But the brown was Laura’s best, and she demurred.

“Oh well, if you don’t care to look nice, you know﻿ ﻿…”

Of course she did; she was burning to. She even accepted the loan of a sash from her friend, because “Bob loves blue”; and went out feeling odd and unlike herself, in her everyday hat and borrowed plumes.

===Chinese===
到了那个重大关头的早晨，蒂莉趁她穿衣裳进了屋，挑剔地上下打量她。

「哦，我说，别戴那顶棕色帽子……行行好吧！鲍勃受不了棕色。」

可棕色那顶是劳拉最好的一顶，她支吾着不肯换。

「哦，好吧，你要是不在乎好看不好看，那就随你……」

她当然在乎；她巴不得好看呢。她甚至接受了朋友出借的一条腰带，因为『鲍勃喜欢蓝色』；就这样，头上还是天天那顶家常帽子，身上却是借来的翎毛，走出门去只觉得自己古怪得不像自己了。

===Original===
The Aunt, a pleasant, youthful-looking lady, called for them in a white-hooded wagonette, and set them down at the house with a playful warning.

“Now don’t get up to any mischief, you two!”

“No fear!” was Tilly’s genial response, as Aunt and cab drove off.

They were going to “do the block,” Tilly explained, and would meet Bob there; but they must first make sure that the drive had not disarranged their hair or the position of their hats; and she led the way to her aunt’s bedroom.

Laura, though she had her share of natural vanity, was too impatient to do more than cast a perfunctory glance at her reflected self. At this period of her life when a drive in a hired cab was enough of a novelty to give her pleasure, a day such as the one that lay before her filled her with unbounded anticipation.

She fidgeted from one leg to another while she waited. For Tilly was in no hurry to be gone: she prinked and finicked, making lavish use, after the little swing-glass at school, of the big mirror with its movable wings; she examined her teeth, pulled down her under-lids, combed her eyebrows, twisted her neck this way and that, in an endeavour to view her person from every angle; she took liberties with perfumes and brushes: was, in short, blind and deaf to all but the perfecting of herself﻿—this rather mannish little self, which, despite a most womanly plumpness, affected a boyish bonhomie, and emphasised the role by wearing a stiff white collar and cuffs.

===Chinese===
姨妈（Aunt）是位和气、模样年轻的太太，驾着一辆白色折篷的四轮马车来接她们，把两人送到大宅门口，又戏谑地告诫了一句。

「你们两个，可不许淘气闯祸！」

「才不怕呢！」蒂莉乐呵呵地应道；说话间，姨妈的马车已经驶远了。

按蒂莉的说法，她们要去『逛街区』（do the block），到那儿同鲍勃碰头；不过先得弄确实了，坐这一路车有没有弄乱头发、歪了帽子；于是她在前头带路，进了姨妈的卧室。

劳拉虽然也自有几分天生的虚荣，却等得不耐烦，只朝镜中的自己敷衍地瞥了一眼。在她人生的这个时期，坐一趟雇来的马车还新鲜得足以叫她欢喜，眼下摆在面前的这样一天，更叫她心里涨满了无边无际的盼望。

她等着，两条腿换来换去地倒腾。因为蒂莉一点也不急着动身：她描描画画、挑挑拣拣，在学校只有一面小小的摆镜，如今对着这面带活动侧翼的大穿衣镜恣意享用；她查验牙齿，翻下眼皮，梳拢眉毛，脖子拧过来拧过去，务求从每个角度看清自己这个人；香水呀、刷子呀，她也毫不客气地滥用一气：总而言之，除了把自己拾掇得尽善尽美，她对什么都又瞎又聋——就是这么个带几分男孩子气的小人儿，明明一身十足女人味的丰腴，偏要摆出一副小子的爽朗，还特意戴上白色硬领和硬袖口来坐实这个角色。

===Original===
Laura was glad when she at last decided that she would “do,” and when they stepped out into the radiant autumn morning.

“What a perfectly scrumptious day!”

“Yes, bully. I say, *is* my waist all right?”

“Quite right. And ever so small.”

“I know. I gave it an extra pull-in. Now if only we’re lucky enough to get hold of a man or two we know!”

===Chinese===
好容易等到她断定自己『可以见人了』，两人跨出门去，走进明媚的秋晨，劳拉这才高兴起来。

「这天气真是好到没治了！」

「可不，棒极了。我说，我这腰身当真没问题吧？」

「挺没问题。还特别细呢。」

「那还用说。我特意多勒紧了一道。这会儿只盼咱们运气好，能逮着一两个认得的小伙子！」

===Original===
The air, Australian air, met them like a prickling champagne: it was incredibly crisp, pure, buoyant. From the top of the eastern hill the spacious white street sloped speedily down, to run awhile in a hollow, then mount again at the other end. Where the two girls turned into it, it was quiet; but the farther they descended, the fuller it grew﻿—fuller of idlers like themselves, out to see and to be seen.

Laura cocked her chin; she had not had a like sense of freedom since being at school. And besides, was not a boy, a handsome boy, waiting for her, and expecting her? This was the clou of the day, the end for which everything was making; yet of such stuff was Laura that she would have felt relieved, could the present moment have been spun out indefinitely. The state of suspense was very pleasant to her.

As for Tilly, that young lady was swinging the shoulders atop of the little waist in a somewhat provocative fashion, only too conscious of the grey-blueness of her fine eyes, and the modish cut of her clothes. She had a knack which seemed to Laura both desirable and unattainable: that of appearing to be engrossed in glib chat with her companion, while in reality she did not hear a word Laura said, and ogled everyone who passed, out of the tail of her eye.

===Chinese===
这空气，澳大利亚的空气，像气泡扎舌的香槟酒迎面扑来：清冽、纯净、轻快得叫人难以置信。从东边山顶起，这条宽阔的白色街道一路疾疾地斜落下去，在洼地里走上一段，再从另一头重新爬升。两个姑娘拐进街口的地方还安安静静；可越往下走，街越热闹——热闹的是跟她们一样的闲人，出来看人，也给人看。

劳拉下巴一扬；上学以来，她还不曾有过这样的自由感。再说，不是有一个男孩子、一个漂亮的男孩子正等着她、盼着她吗？这才是这一天的重头戏，桩桩件件都朝这个结局奔去；然而劳拉的材质偏偏是这样：倘使眼下这一刻能够无限拉长下去，她反倒会松一口气。这种悬而未决的状态，她受用得很。

至于蒂莉，那位小姐正撺掇着肩膀，在那把小细腰顶上摇来摆去，摇得颇带几分挑衅意味，一心自知那双美目的灰蓝，自知衣裳剪裁的时新。她有一手在劳拉看来既眼馋又学不来的本领：看似跟同伴口齿伶俐地聊得入港，实际上劳拉说的话她一个字也没听进去，眼角的余光却把每个过路人都要瞟上一瞟。

===Original===
They reached the “block,” that strip of Collins Street which forms the fashionable promenade. Here the road was full of cabs and carriages, and there was a great crowd on the pavement. The girls progressed but slowly. People were meeting their friends, shopping, changing books at the library, eating ices at the confectioner’s, fruit at the big fruit-shop round the corner. There were a large number of high-collared young dudes, some Trinity and Ormond men with coloured hatbands, ladies with little parcels dangling from their wrists, and countless schoolgirls like themselves. Tilly grew momentarily livelier; her big eyes pounced, hawk-like, on every face she met, and her words to Laura became more disjointed than before. Finally, her efforts were crowned with success: she managed, by dint of glance and smile combined, to unhook a youth of her acquaintance from a group at a doorway, and to attach him to herself.

In high good humour now that her aim was accomplished, she set about the real business of the morning﻿—that of promenading up and down. She had no longer even a feigned interest left for Laura, and the latter walked beside the couple a lame and unnecessary third. Though she kept a keen watch for Bob, she could not discover him, and her time was spent for the most part in dodging people, and in catching up with her companions; for it was difficult to walk three abreast in the crowd.

Then she saw him﻿—and with what an unpleasant shock. If only Tilly did not see him, too!

===Chinese===
她们到了『街区』，也就是科林斯街（Collins Street）上充作时髦散步场的那一段。马路上车马拥挤，人行道上人山人海。两个姑娘只能慢慢往前蹭。人们在这儿会朋友、买东西、上图书馆换书，在糖果店吃冰淇淋，在街角那家大水果店吃水果。竖着高领的浮华少年为数众多，有几个三一学院（Trinity）和奥蒙德学院（Ormond）的男生帽箍上缠着彩带；太太小姐们手腕上吊着小小的包裹，还有数不清的跟她们一样的女学生。蒂莉一分钟比一分钟活泛；那双大眼睛鹰隼似的，朝碰见的每一张脸扑过去，对劳拉说的话也越发前言不搭后语。终于，她的功夫大功告成：凭着秋波与微笑双管齐下，她硬是把一个相识的小伙子从门口的一伙人里钩了出来，拴到了自己身边。

目的既已达到，她兴致高昂，着手办这天上午的正事——来回地遛。她对劳拉连装出来的那点兴趣也不剩了，劳拉便跟在那两个人身旁，做了个跛脚又多余的第三个人。她尽管睁大眼睛留意鲍勃，却怎么也找不见他，工夫多半花在躲人、追赶同伴上；因为人堆里三个人并排走实在太难了。

随后她望见了他——那一惊真叫人不受用。但愿蒂莉没有也望见他！

===Original===
But no such luck was hers. “Look out, there’s Bob,” nudged Tilly almost at once.

Alas! there was no question of his waiting longingly for her to appear. He was walking with two ladies, and laughing and talking. He raised his hat to his cousin and her friend, but did not disengage himself, and passing them by disappeared in the throng.

Behind her hand Tilly buzzed: “One of those Woodwards is awfully sweet on him. I bet he can’t get loose.”

This was a drop of comfort. But as, at the next encounter, he still did not offer to join them﻿—could it, indeed, be expected that he would prefer her company to that of the pretty, grown-up girls he was with?﻿—as he again sidled past, Tilly, who had given him one of her most vivacious sparkles, turned and shot a glance at Laura’s face.

“For pity’s sake, look a little more amiable, or he won’t come at all.”

Laura felt more like crying; her sunshine was intercepted, her good spirits were quenched; had she had her will, she would have turned tail and gone straight back to school. She had not wanted Bob, had never asked him to be “gone” on her, and if she had now to fish for him, into the bargain﻿ ﻿… However there was no help for it; the thing had to be gone through with; and, since Tilly seemed disposed to lay the blame of his lukewarmness at her door, Laura glued her mouth, the next time Bob hove in sight, into a feeble smile.

===Chinese===
可她没这个运气。「留神，鲍勃在那儿。」蒂莉几乎立刻用胳膊肘碰了碰她。

唉！哪里谈得上他望眼欲穿地盼她露面。他正陪着两位女士散步，一路谈笑。他朝表妹和她的朋友抬了抬帽子，却没有脱身过来的意思，擦着她们走过去，消失在人潮里。

蒂莉拿手挡在嘴边，压着嗓子嗡嗡地说：「伍德沃德家（Woodward）那几位里，有一个迷他迷得要命。我打赌他脱不开身。」

这总算是一滴安慰。可是等到下一回碰面，他仍旧没有要凑过来的意思——说实在的，难道能指望他撇下身边那两位漂亮的大姑娘，来奉陪她不成？——眼见他又要侧着身子溜过去，蒂莉方才朝他飞过一个最活泼的眼波，这会儿转过脸来，朝劳拉的脸射出一瞥。

「行行好，脸上放和气点儿，不然他压根儿就不会过来了。」

劳拉更想哭；她的阳光叫人半路截了去，好兴致被浇了个透湿；倘随她的意，她早就掉头一溜烟跑回学校去了。她本没有要过鲍勃，也从没求他『迷』上自己，如今倒好，反倒还得去钓他……可是没法子；这一关总得熬过去；而且，既然蒂莉像是存心把他这份不冷不热记到她账上，下一回鲍勃再露面，劳拉便把嘴硬生生抿出一个虚弱的微笑。

===Original===
Soon afterwards he came up to them. His cousin had an arch greeting in readiness.

“Well, you’ve been doing a pretty mash, you have!” she cried, and jogged him with her elbow. “No wonder you’d no eyes for poor us. What price Miss Woodward’s gloves this morning!”﻿—at which Bob laughed, looked sly, and tapped his breast pocket.

It was time to be moving homewards. Tilly and her beau led the way. “For we know you two would rather be alone. Now, Bob, not too many sheep’s-eyes, please!”

Bob smiled, and let fly a wicked glance at Laura from under his dark lashes. Dropping behind, they began to mount the hill. Now was the moment, felt Laura, to say something very witty, or pert, or clever; and a little pulse in her throat beat hard, as she furiously racked her brains. Oh, for just a morsel of Tilly’s loose-tonguedness! One after the other she considered and dismissed: the pleasant coolness of the morning, the crowded condition of the street, even the fact of the next day being Sunday﻿—ears and cheeks on fire, meanwhile, at her own slow-wittedness. And Bob smiled. She almost hated him for that smile. It was so assured, and withal so disturbing. Seen close at hand his teeth were whiter, his eyes browner than she had believed. His upper lip, too, was quite dark; and he fingered it incessantly, as he waited for her to make the onslaught.

===Chinese===
不多时，他朝她们走过来了。他表妹早备好了一句促狭的招呼。

「好啊，你这趟调情调得漂亮，真有你的！」她嚷道，用胳膊肘捅了捅他。「难怪你眼里容不下我们这些可怜人。今儿早上伍德沃德小姐的手套，又是什么行情！」——鲍勃闻言一笑，眼里透着狡黠，拍了拍胸前的口袋。

该往回走了。蒂莉和她的情郎在前头引路。「因为我们知道，你们俩巴不得单独待着呢。好了，鲍勃，拜托，秋波可别抛得太密！」

鲍勃微微一笑，从黑睫毛底下朝劳拉放出促狭的一瞥。两人落在后面，开始爬坡。劳拉觉得，此刻正是时候，该说点儿非常俏皮、或者大胆、或者聪明的话；她拼命绞着脑汁，喉咙口一根小脉突突直跳。唉，哪怕分到蒂莉那副张口就来的伶牙俐齿的一星半点也好哇！早晨的凉爽宜人、街上的熙熙攘攘，甚至连明天是礼拜天这个现成的话题，她一个一个想过来，又一个一个地否决掉——与此同时，耳朵和脸颊火烧火燎，全怨自己脑子钝。而鲍勃只是微笑。为了那个微笑，她几乎恨起他来。那笑太笃定了，又那么叫人心绪不宁。凑近了看，他的牙齿比她原以为的更白，眼睛比她原以为的更棕。他的上唇也黑黢黢的；他一个劲儿摩挲着它，只等她先发动进攻。

===Original===
But he waited in vain; and when they had walked a whole street-block in this mute fashion, it was he who broke the silence.

“Ripping girls, those Woodwards,” he said, and seemed to be remembering their charms.

“Yes, they looked very nice,” said Laura in a small voice, and was extremely conscious of her own thirteen years.

“Simply stunning! Though May’s so slender﻿—May’s the pretty one﻿—and has such a jolly figure﻿ ﻿… I believe I could span her waist with my two hands﻿ ﻿… her service is just A1﻿—at tennis I mean.”

“Is it really?” said Laura wanly, and felt unutterably depressed at the turn the conversation was taking. Her own waist was coarse, her knowledge of tennis of the slightest.

“Ra-*ther*! Overhand, with a cut on it﻿—she plays with a 14-oz. racquet. And she has a back drive, too, by Jove, that﻿—you play, of course?”

===Chinese===
可他白等了；两人就这样一声不吭走完了一整个街区，还是他先开了口。

「伍德沃德家那几个姑娘，真带劲。」他说，仿佛还在回味她们的好处。

「嗯，她们看着是很不错。」劳拉细声细气地说，心里把自己那十三岁意识得再清楚不过。

「简直美呆了！不过梅（May）太瘦——梅是漂亮的那一个——身段又那么好……我看我两只手就能拢住她的腰……她的发球简直一流——我是说打网球。」

「真的吗？」劳拉恹恹地说；话头这样一路转下去，她心里郁结得说不出的难受。她自己的腰身粗笨，网球又几乎一窍不通。

「那——还用说！上手发球，还带切削——她用的是 14 盎司的球拍。她还有一手反手抽击，老天爷，那才叫……你当然也打咯？」

===Original===
“Oh, yes.” Laura spoke up manfully; but prayed that he would not press his inquiries further. At this juncture his attention was diverted by the passing of a fine tandem; and as soon as he brought it back to her again, she said: “You’re at Trinity, aren’t you?”﻿—which was finesse; for she knew he wasn’t.

“Well, yes﻿ ﻿… all but,” answered Bob well pleased. “I start in this winter.”

“How nice!”

There was another pause; then she blurted out: “We church girls always wear Trinity colours at the boat-race.”

She hoped from her heart, this might lead him to say that he would look out for her there; but he did nothing of the kind. His answer was to the effect that this year they jolly well expected to knock Ormond into a cocked hat.

===Chinese===
「哦，打。」劳拉壮起胆子朗声答道；心里却祷告他千万别再往下追问。恰好这时一辆气派的纵列双马马车驶过，把他的注意力岔开了；等他把心思收回到她身上，她便说：「你在三一学院，对吧？」——这是个小手腕；因为她明知道他不在。

「呃，是……差不多吧。」鲍勃答道，心里相当受用。「今年冬天就入学。」

「多好呀！」

又是一阵沉默；随后她冲口而出：「我们上教堂的女孩子，看船赛的时候都戴三一学院的彩带。」

她打心底里盼着，这话能引得他说一句到时候会在那儿留心找她；可他压根儿没那个意思。他的回答大意是：今年他们笃定要把奥蒙德打得一败涂地。

===Original===
Lunch threatened to be formidable. To begin with, Laura, whose natural, easy frankness had by this time all but been successfully educated out of her, Laura was never shyer with strangers than at a meal, where every word you said could be listened to by a tableful of people. Then, too, her vis-à-vis was a small sharp child of five or six, called Thumbby, or Thumbkin, who only removed her bead-like eyes from Laura’s face to be saucy to her father. And, what was worse, the Uncle turned out to be a type that struck instant terror into Laura: a full-fledged male tease. He was, besides, very hairy of face, and preternaturally solemn.

No sooner had he drawn in his chair to the table than he began. Lifting his head and thrusting out his chin, he sniffed the air in all directions with a moving nose﻿—just as a cat does. Everyone looked at him in surprise. Tilly, who sat next him, went pink.

“What is it, dear?” his wife at last inquired in a gentle voice; for it was evident that he was not going to stop till asked why he did it.

===Chinese===
午饭眼看着要成一场吓人的阵仗。首先，劳拉那份天生随和的坦率，到这时候已经被成功地调教得差不多荡然无存，而她跟生人相处，从来没有比在饭桌上更害羞的——在饭桌上，你说的每一个字，都可能落进满桌子人的耳朵。再者，她对面的座位上是个五六岁、精灵尖刻的小丫头，名叫汤比（Thumbby），也叫汤姆金（Thumbkin）；那双珠子似的眼睛，只有在要冲她父亲撒野的时候，才肯从劳拉脸上挪开。更糟的是，姨父（Uncle）竟属于能叫劳拉霎时胆战心惊的那一类人：一个炉火纯青的爱捉弄人的男子。何况他满脸毛发，又肃穆得超乎自然。

椅子刚往桌前一拉，他就开场了。只见他昂起头，探出下巴，鼻子抽动着，朝四面八方嗅空气——活脱脱一只猫。众人都诧异地望着他。坐在他旁边的蒂莉红了脸。

「怎么了，亲爱的？」他太太终于柔声问道；因为明摆着，不问出个所以然来，他是不会住手的。

===Original===
“Mos’ extraor’nary smell!” he replied. “Mother, d’you know, I could take my appledavy someone has been using my scent.”

“Nonsense, Tom.”

“Silly pa!” said the little girl.

Ramming his knuckles into his eyes, he pretended to cry at his daughter’s rebuke; then bore down on Laura.

“D’you know, Miss Ra﻿ ﻿… Ra﻿ ﻿… Rambotham”﻿—he made as if he could not get her name out﻿—“d’you know that I’m a great man for scent? Fact. I take a bath in it every morning.”

===Chinese===
「顶——顶古怪的味儿！」他答道。「太太，你可知，我可以赌上我的苹果誓（appledavy），准有人动过我的香水。」

「胡说，汤姆（Tom）。」

「傻爸爸！」小姑娘说。

他把指关节往眼窝里按，装作被女儿这一骂气哭了；接着便朝劳拉猛扑过去。

「你可知，拉……拉……拉姆博瑟姆小姐」——他做出一副她的名字堵在嘴里吐不出来的样子——「你可知我在香水一道上是个大行家？真的。我每天早晨都在香水里泡个澡。」

===Original===
Laura smiled uncertainly, fixed always by the child.

“Fact, I assure you. Over the tummy, up to the chin. Now, who’s been at it? For it’s my opinion I shan’t have enough left to shampoo my eyebrows. Bob, is it you?”

“Don’t be an ass, pater.”

“Cut me some bread, Bob, please,” said Tilly hastily.

“Mos’ extraor’nary thing!” persisted the Uncle. “Or﻿—good Lord, mother, can it be my monthly attack of D.T.’s beginning already? They’re not due, you know, till next week, Monday, five o’clock.”

“Dear, *don’t* be so silly. Besides it’s my scent, not yours. And anyone is welcome to it.”

===Chinese===
劳拉没个准儿地笑着，始终被那小丫头盯着。

「真的，我向你担保。从肚皮一直泡到下巴。那么，是谁动的？照我看，剩下的都不够我洗眉毛的了。鲍勃，是你不是？」

「别犯傻了，老爸。」

「鲍勃，劳驾，给我切点儿面包。」蒂莉赶忙说。

「怪——怪事一桩！」姨父不屈不挠。「要不然——老天爷，太太，莫不是我月月一犯的酒癫疯（D.T.）提前发作了？要知道，按日子，它该下星期一五点钟才到呢。」

「亲爱的，可别这么傻。再说那是我的香水，又不是你的。谁爱用谁用。」

===Original===
“Well, well, let’s call in the cats!﻿—By the way, Miss Ra﻿ ﻿… Ra﻿ ﻿… Rambotham, are you aware that this son of mine is a professed lady-killer?”

Laura and Bob went different shades of crimson.

“Why has she got so red?” the child asked her mother, in an audible whisper.

“Oh, *chuck* it, pater!” murmured Bob in disgust.

“Fact, I assure you. Put not your trust in Robert! He’s always on with the new love before he’s off with the old. You ask him whose glove he’s still cherishing in the pocket next his heart.”

Bob pushed his plate from him and, for a moment, seemed about to leave the table. Laura could not lift her eyes. Tilly chewed in angry silence.

===Chinese===
「好啦好啦，把猫都唤进来吧！——顺便说一句，拉……拉……拉姆博瑟姆小姐，你可知道，我这个儿子是个挂了号的姑娘杀手？」

劳拉和鲍勃红得深浅不一。

「她怎么红成这样？」小丫头问她母亲，那耳语人人听得见。

「哦，快打住吧，老爸！」鲍勃厌恶地咕哝道。

「真的，我向你担保。切莫把你的信赖放在罗伯特（Robert）身上！他向来是新欢才上手，旧爱还没撒手。你去问问他，贴着心口的那个口袋里，还珍藏着谁的手套。」

鲍勃把盘子往前一推，有一瞬像是就要离席。劳拉抬不起眼来。蒂莉气冲冲地闷头嚼着。

===Original===
Here, however, the child made a diversion.

“You’re a lady-kilda yourself, pa.”

“Me, Thumbkin?﻿—Mother, d’you hear that?﻿—Then it’s the whiskers, Thumbby. Ladies love whiskers﻿—or a fine drooping moustache, like my son Bob’s.” He sang: “ ‘Oh, oh, the ladies loved him so!’ ”

“Tom, dear, *do* be quiet.”

“Tom, Tom, the piper’s son!” chirped Thumbby.

“Well, well, let’s call in the cats!”﻿—which appeared to be his way of changing the subject.

===Chinese===
这时，小丫头出来岔了话头。

「你自己才是个姑娘杀客呢，爸。」

「我吗，汤姆金？——太太，你听见没有？——那就准是这两撇连鬓胡子了，汤比。姑娘们爱连鬓胡子——再不然就爱一撇漂亮下垂的小胡子，像我儿子鲍勃的那样。」他唱了起来：「『哦，哦，姑娘们都那么爱他！』」

「汤姆，亲爱的，千万安静点儿吧。」

「汤姆，汤姆，吹笛人的儿子！」汤比尖声念道。

「好啦好啦，把猫都唤进来吧！」——这似乎是他改换话题的路数。

===Original===
It seemed, after this, as though the remainder of lunch might pass off without further hitch. Then however and all of a sudden, while he was peeling an apple, this dreadful man said, as though to himself: “Ra﻿ ﻿… Ra﻿ ﻿… Rambotham. Now where have I heard that name?”

“Wa﻿ ﻿… Wa﻿ ﻿… Wamboffam!” mocked Thumbkin.

“Monkey, if you’re so sharp you’ll cut yourself!﻿—Young lady, do you happen to come from Warrenega?” he asked Laura, when Thumbkin’s excited chirrup of: “I’ll cut *you*, pa, into little bits!” had died away.

Ready to sink through the floor, Laura replied that she did.

===Chinese===
这之后，午饭剩下的工夫似乎可以顺顺当当地混过去了。谁知突然之间，他削着苹果，这个可怕的男人仿佛自言自语地开了口：「拉……拉……拉姆博瑟姆。我这是在哪儿听过这个姓来着？」

「拉……拉……拉姆博法姆！」汤姆金学舌取笑。

「小猴儿，嘴这么尖，仔细割了你自己！——小姑娘，你莫不是从沃里内加来的？」等汤姆金那句兴奋的尖叫——「我可要把『你』割成一小块一小块，爸！」——平息下去之后，他这样问劳拉。

劳拉恨不得钻进地里去，回答说正是。

===Original===
“Then I’ve the pleasure of knowing your mother. Tall dark woman, isn’t she?”

Under the table, Laura locked the palms of her hands and stemmed her feet against the floor. Was here, now, before them all, and Bob in particular, the shameful secret of the embroidery to come to light? She could hardly force her lips to frame an answer.

Her confusion was too patent to be overlooked. Above her lowered head, signs passed between husband and wife, and soon afterwards the family rose from the table.

But Tilly was so obviously sulky that the tease could not let her escape him thus.

===Chinese===
「那我有幸认识令堂了。高高的个子，黑头发，对不对？」

桌子底下，劳拉十指相扣地绞紧，两脚抵住地板。那件见不得人的刺绣活计的秘密，难道就要在此时此地、当着这一大家人、尤其是当着鲍勃的面，败露出来了吗？她几乎无力驱使嘴唇拼出一个回答。

她的慌乱太明显，想不被人看破也不可能。在她低垂的头顶上方，夫妇俩暗通消息，不久一家人便离了席。

可蒂莉分明在赌气，这位捉弄大王哪肯就这样放过她。

===Original===
He cried: “For God’s sake, Tilly, stand still! What on earth have you got on your back?”

Tilly came from upcountry and her thoughts leapt fearfully to scorpions and tarantulas. Affrighted, she tried to peer over her shoulder, and gave a preliminary shriek. “Gracious!﻿—whatever is it?”

“Hold on!” He approached her with the tongs; the next moment to ejaculate: “Begad, it’s not a growth, it’s a bustle!” and as he spoke he tweaked the place where a bustle used to be worn.

Even Bob had to join in the ensuing boo-hoo, which went on and on till Laura thought the Uncle would fall down in a fit. Then for the third time he invited those present to join him in summoning the cats, murmured something about “humping his bluey,” and went out into the hall, where they heard him swinging Thumbby “round the world.”

It was all the Aunt could do to mollify Tilly, who was enraged to the point of tears. “I’ve never worn a bustle in my life! Uncle’s a perfect *fool*! I’ve never met such a fool as he is!”

===Chinese===
他嚷道：「看在老天的份上，蒂莉，站住别动！你背上到底趴了个什么东西？」

蒂莉是从内地乡下来的，心思吓得一跳，蹿到了蝎子和塔兰图拉毒蛛上。她惊慌地扭头想往肩后看，先发出一声尖叫。「天哪！——到底是什么呀？」

「别动！」他举着火钳向她走近；下一刻便失声叫道：「乖乖，不是个瘤子，是个腰垫（bustle）呀！」说着就伸手掐了掐从前系腰垫的那个部位。

连鲍勃也不得不加入了随后那场大笑，笑个没完没了，笑得劳拉直当姨父要一头栽倒、抽起风来。接着他第三遍邀请在座各位同他一起把猫唤进来，嘟哝了一句什么『背起蓝包袱去流浪』，便出到大厅里去了，众人听见他在那儿抡着汤比『环游世界』。

姨妈费了九牛二虎之力，才把气得眼泪直转的蒂莉安抚下来。「我这辈子就没戴过什么腰垫！姨父是个十足的大傻瓜！我从没见过像他这样傻的人！」

===Original===
Still boiling, she disappeared to nurse her ruffled temper in private; and she remained absent from the room for over half an hour. During this time Laura and Bob were alone together. But even less than before came of their intercourse: Bob, still smarting from his father’s banter, was inclined to be standoffish, as though afraid Laura might take liberties with him after having been made to look so small; Laura, rendered thoroughly unsure to begin with, by the jocular tone of the luncheon-table, had not recovered from the shock of hearing her parentage so bluffly disclosed. And since, at this time, her idea of the art of conversation was to make jerky little remarks which led nowhere, or to put still more jerky questions, Bob was soon stifling yawns, and not with the best success. He infected Laura; and there the two of them sat, doing their best to appear unconscious of the terrible spasms which, every few seconds, distorted their faces. At last Bob could stand it no longer and bolted from the room.

Laura was alone, and seemed to be forgotten. The minutes ticked by, and no one came﻿—or no one but a little grey kitten, which arrived as if from nowhere, with a hop and a skip. She coaxed the creature to her lap, where it joined head to tail and went to sleep. And there she sat, in the gloomy, overfilled drawing-room, and stroked the kitten, which neither cracked stupid jokes nor required her to strain her wits to make conversation.

When at length Tilly came back, she expressed a rather acid surprise at Bob’s absence, and went to look for him; Laura heard them whispering and laughing in the passage. On their return to the drawing-room it had been decided that the three of them should go for a walk. As the sky was overcast and the girls had no umbrellas, Bob carried a big one belonging to the Uncle. Tilly called this a “family umbrella”; and the jokes that were extracted from the pair of words lasted the walkers on the whole of their outward way; lasted so long that Laura, who was speedily finished with her contribution, grew quite stupefied with listening to the other two.

===Chinese===
她兀自怒气翻腾，躲出去独自将养那份被触恼的脾气；她离开客厅足有半个多钟头。这段时间里，劳拉和鲍勃单独待在一起。可是两人的交谈比先前更没成果：鲍勃还被父亲那番打趣刺痛着，不免冷淡疏远，仿佛怕劳拉见他下了这么大台面之后，会跟他放肆起来似的；劳拉呢，起头就被午饭桌上那副打趣的腔调弄得心里全没了底，又还没从自己的家底被人这么大大咧咧抖搂出来的惊吓里缓过来。何况在这个时期，她对于谈话之术的全部见解，无非是蹦出几句哪儿也到不了的小话，或者问几个更加突兀的问题，于是鲍勃很快便憋起了哈欠，还憋得不大成功。他又传染给劳拉；两个人就那么坐着，竭力装作浑然不觉那每隔几秒钟便扭曲一次他们面孔的可怖痉挛。末了鲍勃再也受不了，夺门而出。

劳拉独自一人，像是被人忘了。时间嘀嗒嘀嗒地过去，谁也不来——来的只有一只小灰猫，不知打哪儿连蹦带跳地就出现了。她把它哄到膝上，它头尾相接，蜷成一团睡着了。她就这样坐在那间阴暗、塞得满满当当的客厅里，抚摸着小猫；这小东西既不打愚蠢的诨，也不逼她绞尽脑汁地找话说。

好容易蒂莉回来了，她对鲍勃的不在场表示了一番相当尖酸的惊讶，便去找他；劳拉听见他们在走廊里嘀嘀咕咕地笑。等他们回到客厅，事情已经定夺：三人一同出去散步。天上阴了，姑娘们又没带伞，鲍勃便扛了姨父的一把大伞。蒂莉管它叫『全家伞』；从这两个字里榨出来的玩笑，陪着散步的人走完了去路的全程；榨得那么久，以致劳拉——自己那一份贡献早早就告罄了——听得脑袋都木了。

===Original===
Collins Street was now as empty as a bush road. The young people went into Bourke Street, where, for want of something better to do, they entered the Eastern Market and strolled about inside. The noise that rose from the livestock, on ground floor and upper storey, was earsplitting: pigs grunted; cocks crowed, turkeys gobbled, parrots shrieked; while rough human voices echoed and reechoed under the lofty roof. There was a smell, too, an extraordinary smell, composed of all the individual smells of all these living things: of fruit and vegetables, fresh and decayed; of flowers, and butter, and grain; of meat, and fish, and strong cheeses; of sawdust sprinkled with water, and freshly wet pavements﻿—one great complicated smell, the piquancy of which made Laura sniff like a spaniel. But after a very few minutes Tilly, whose temper was still short, called it a “vile stink” and clapped her handkerchief to her nose, and so they hurried out, past many enticing little side booths hidden in dark corners on the ground floor, such as a woman without legs, a double-headed calf, and the like.

Outside it had begun to rain; they turned into a Waxworks Exhibition. This was a poor show, and they were merely killing time when the announcement caught their eye that a certain room was open to “Married People Only.” The quips and jokes this gave rise to again were as unending as those about the umbrella; and Laura grew so tired of them, and of pretending to find them funny, that her temper also began to give way; and she eased her feelings by making the nippy mental note on her companions, that jokes were evidently “in the blood.”

When they emerged, it was time for the girls to return to school. They took a hansom, Bob accompanying them. As they drove, Laura sitting sandwiched between the other two, it came over her with a rush what a miserable failure the day had been. A minute before, her spirits had given a faint flicker, for Bob had laid his arm along the back of the seat. Then she saw that he had done this just to pull at the little curls that grew on Tilly’s neck. She was glad when the cab drew up, when Tilly ostentatiously took the fat half-crown from her purse, and Bob left them at the gate with a: “Well, so long, ladies!”

===Chinese===
科林斯街这时空得像条丛林小路。几个年轻人转进伯克街（Bourke Street），无事可做，便进了东市场（Eastern Market），在里面闲逛。楼上楼下，牲口摊上腾起的聒噪震耳欲聋：猪哼哼，公鸡打鸣，火鸡咯咯，鹦鹉尖叫；粗嘎的人声在高大的棚顶下回荡了又回荡。还有一股味儿，一股非同寻常的味儿，由所有这些活物各自的气味调和而成：有水果蔬菜的，新鲜的和腐烂的；有鲜花、黄油、谷物的；有肉、鱼、浓味干酪的；有洒了水的锯末和刚打湿的石板地的——一股庞大的、成分复杂的味儿，那股辛辣劲儿熏得劳拉像只小猎犬似的直抽鼻子。可是没过几分钟，脾气还没顺过来的蒂莉就骂它是『一股恶臭』，一把将手帕捂上鼻子，于是他们匆匆跑了出去，一路经过底层的暗角落里藏着的许多诱人的小摊子——什么没有腿的女人啦、双头的牛犊啦，诸如此类。

门外下起了雨；他们拐进了一家蜡像展览馆。这展览寒酸得很，他们不过是消磨时间，忽然一张告示撞进眼里：某间展室『仅限已婚人士』参观。这一来引出的俏皮话和玩笑，又跟伞的那一场一样没完没了；劳拉听得烦透了，还得装着觉得好笑，脾气也渐渐撑不住；便靠在心里刻毒地记下同伴一笔来顺气：爱开玩笑，显然是『血里带来的』。

他们出来时，姑娘们也该回学校了。她们雇了一辆双轮马车，鲍勃一路陪同。车子走着，劳拉夹在另外两人中间，忽然一下子明白过来：这一天败得有多惨。一分钟前，她的情绪还微微闪了一下光，因为鲍勃把一条胳膊搭上了座位的椅背。随即她便看见，他这样做，只是为了捻一捻蒂莉脖颈上天生的小卷毛。等马车停到校门口，蒂莉故意张扬地从钱包里掏出那枚厚墩墩的半克朗，鲍勃把两人送到大门口，丢下一句「好啦，回见，二位女士！」——这时候她只觉得庆幸。

===Original===
The boarders spent the evening in sewing garments for charity. Laura had been at work for weeks on a coarse, red flannel petticoat, and as a rule was under constant reprimand for her idleness. On this night, having separated herself from Tilly, she sat down beside a girl with a very long plait of hair and small, narrow eyes, who went by the name of “Chinky.” Chinky was always making up to her, and could be relied on to cover her silence. Laura sewed away, with bent head and pursed lips, and was so engrossed that the sole rebuke she incurred had to do with her diligence.

Miss Chapman exclaimed in horror at her stiffly outstretched arm.

“How *can* you be so vulgar, Laura? To sew with a thread as long as that!”

===Chinese===
寄宿生们这个晚上都在缝制慈善衣物。一条粗布的红法兰绒衬裙，劳拉已经做了好几个星期，平日里总为她的怠惰挨训。这天晚上，她避开蒂莉，挨着一个梳长辫子、眯着细小眼睛的姑娘坐下——此人绰号『中国眼』（Chinky）。中国眼一向爱巴结她，靠得住能替她遮掩沉默。劳拉低着头、抿着嘴，只管飞针走线，缝得太投入，这一晚挨的唯一一句责备，反倒是因为她太用功。

查普曼小姐看见她那条直挺挺伸出去的胳膊，惊恐地叫出了声。

「你怎么『能』这么俗气，劳拉！用那么长的一根线做针线！」

## XIX / 十九

===Original===
Thus Laura went to Coventry. Not that the social banishment she now suffered was known by that name. To the majority of the girls Coventry was just a word in the geography book, a place where ribbons were said to be made, and where for a better-read few, someone had hung with grooms and porters on a bridge; this detail, odd to say, making a deeper impression on their young minds than the story of Lady Godiva, which was looked upon merely as a naughty anecdote.

But, by whatever name it was known, Laura’s ostracism was complete. She had been sampled, tested, put on one side. And not the softest-hearted could find an excuse for her behaviour.

It was but another instance of how misfortune dogs him who is down, that Chinky should choose this very moment to bring further shame upon her.

===Chinese===
就这样，劳拉被「送去了考文垂」（send one to Coventry，英语俚语，指众人齐心与某人绝交）。倒不是说，她如今遭受的这种社交流放，在众人嘴里当真叫这个名字。在大多数姑娘眼里，考文垂不过是地理课本上的一个词，一个据说出产缎带的地方；至于读书多些的少数人，还知道曾有一个人跟马夫们、脚夫们一起吊死在那里的一座桥上——说来奇怪，这个细节在她们幼小的心田里烙下的印子，竟比戈黛娃夫人（Lady Godiva）的故事还深；那个故事，不过被她们当成一段有伤风化的香艳轶闻。

然而，不论它叫什么名字，劳拉遭的放逐是彻头彻尾的。她被掂量过了，检验过了，撇到一边去了。连心肠最软的人，也找不出半点借口来为她的行为开脱。

中国眼偏偏挑中这个当口，再来给劳拉添一重羞辱——这不过是「灾祸专撵跌倒的人」的又一例证。

===Original===
On one of the miserable days that were now the rule, when Laura would have liked best to be a rabbit, hid deep in its burrow; as she was going upstairs one afternoon, she met Jacob, the man-of-all-work, coming down. He had a trunk on his shoulder. Throughout the day she had been aware of a subdued excitement among the boarders; they had stood about in groups, talking in low voices﻿—talking about her, she believed, from the glances that were thrown over shoulders at her as she passed. She made herself as small as she could; but when teatime came, and then supper, and Chinky had not appeared at either meal, curiosity got the better of her, and she tried to pump one of the younger girls.

Maria came up while she was speaking, and the child ran away; for the little ones aped their elders in making Laura taboo.

“What, liar? You want to stuff us you don’t know why she’s gone?” said Maria. “No, thank you, it’s not good enough. You can’t bamboozle us this time.”

“Sapphira up to her tricks again, is she?” threw in the inseparable Kate, who had caught the last words. “No, by dad, we don’t tell liars what they know already. So put that in your pipe and smoke it!”

===Chinese===
在如今已成了家常便饭的某一天愁苦日子里——这样的日子，劳拉最乐意变作一只兔子，深深缩进洞里——有天下午她上楼，迎面撞见校里那个什么活计都揽的男仆雅各（Jacob）下楼来，肩上扛着一只箱子。一整天，她都觉察到寄宿生中间压着一股按捺住的兴奋；她们一群群站着，交头接耳——她相信说的就是自己，因为她每回走过，都有目光越过肩膀朝她甩过来。她把自己缩得不能再小；可是茶点过了，晚饭也过了，中国眼两顿都没露面，好奇心到底压倒了她，她试着从一个年纪小的女生嘴里套话。

她正说着，玛丽亚走上前来，那小姑娘撒腿跑了；因为小家伙们也有样学样，把劳拉划成碰不得的禁忌。

「什么呀，撒谎精？你想糊弄我们，说你不知道她为什么走了？」玛丽亚道。「不，多谢了，这套不够瞧的。这回你可哄骗不动我们了。」

「撒非喇又在耍她的花招了，是吧？」寸步不离的凯特插进一句，她刚好听见最后那几个字。「不，好家伙，说谎的人自己心里早就有数的事，我们才犯不着奉告。这话你自己搁嘴里嚼摸去吧！」

===Original===
Only bit by bit did Laura dig out their meaning: then, the horrible truth lay bare. Chinky had been dismissed﻿—privately because she was a boarder﻿—from the school. Her crime was: she had taken half-a-sovereign from the purse of one of her roommates. When taxed with the theft, she wept that she had not taken it for herself, but to buy a ring for Laura Rambotham; and, with this admission on her lips, she passed out of their lives, leaving Laura, her confederate, behind. Yes, confederate; for, in the minds of most, liar and thief were synonymous.

Laura had not cared two straws for Chinky; she found what the latter had done, “mean and disgusting,” and said so, stormily; but of course was not believed. Usually too proud to defend herself, she here returned to the charge again and again; for the hint of connivance had touched her on the raw. But she strove in vain to prove her innocence: she could not get her enemies to grasp the abysmal difference between merely making up a story about people, and laying hands on others’ property; if she could do the one, she was capable of the other; and her companions remained convinced that, if she had not actually had her fingers in someone’s purse, she had, by a love of jewellery, incited Chinky to the theft. And so, after a time, Laura gave up the attempt and suffered in silence; and it *was* suffering; for her schoolfellows were cruel with that intolerance, that unimaginative dullness, which makes a woman’s cruelty so hard to bear. Laura had to accustom herself to hear every word she said doubted; to hear someone called to, before her face, to attest her statements; to see her roommates lock up their purses under her very nose.

===Chinese===
劳拉是一点一点才把她们的意思抠出来的：随后，可怕的真相当场摊露在光天化日之下。中国眼被学校打发走了——因为她是寄宿生，所以是私下了结的。她的罪名是：从同屋一个女生的钱袋里拿了半镑金币。被追问到偷窃时，她哭着说，拿钱不是为自己，是要给劳拉·拉姆博瑟姆买一枚戒指；她嘴上带着这句招供，从她们的生活里退场了，却把她的同谋劳拉留在了身后。是的，同谋；因为在大多数人心里，「撒谎的」跟「偷东西的」本是同义语。

劳拉对中国眼本就没上过一点心；她觉得后者干的事『卑鄙又恶心』，也怒气冲冲地这么说来着；可当然没有人信。她平日太骄傲，不屑于替自己辩护，这回却一而再、再而三地冲锋上阵；因为那句串通的暗示，正戳在她的痛处上。可她百般挣扎，也没法证明自己的清白：她没法叫她的对头们领会那道深不见底的鸿沟——编派人家的故事，跟把手伸到人家的财物上，中间隔着这一道；她既然做得出前一样，就做得出后一样；她的同伴们始终认定：就算她当真没把手指头伸进过谁的钱袋，也准是她贪爱首饰，才撺掇中国眼下的手。于是过了一阵，劳拉放弃了这番努力，默默忍着；这确实是受罪：因为她的同学们那种残忍，带着那种不容异己、那种缺乏想象的死硬，正是这些，让女人的残忍格外难捱。劳拉只得习惯于听自己说出的每一个字都被人怀疑；习惯于当着她的面唤过一个人来，给她的说法作证；习惯于看着同屋的女生们就在她眼皮底下，把各自的钱包一一上锁。

===Original===
However, only three weeks had still to run till the Christmas holidays. She drew twenty-one strokes on a sheet of paper, which she pinned to the wall above her bed; and each morning she ran her pencil through a fresh line. She was quite resolved to beg Mother not to send her back to school: if she said she was not getting proper food, that would be enough to put Mother up in arms.

The boxes were being fetched from the lumber-rooms and distributed among their owners, when a letter arrived from Mother saying that the two little boys had sandy blight, and that Laura would not be able to come home under two or three weeks, for fear of infection. These weeks she was to spend, in company with Pin, at a watering-place down the Bay, where one of her aunts had a cottage.

The news was welcome to Laura: she had shrunk from the thought of Mother’s searching eye. And at the cottage there would be none of her grown-up relatives to face; only an old housekeeper, who was looking after a party of boys.

===Chinese===
好在离圣诞节假期还剩下三个星期。她在一张纸上画了二十一道杠，把纸钉在床上方的墙上；每天早晨，用铅笔划掉新的一道。她已经铁了心，要恳求妈妈别再送她回学校：只要说自己在学校吃不上像样的饭食，就足够叫妈妈拍案而起了。

箱子正从堆破烂的储藏室里搬出来，一件件发还各自主人，这时妈妈的信到了，说两个小弟弟患了沙眼，怕传染，劳拉两三个星期内回不了家。这几个星期，她要同萍做伴，在海湾下游一处海滨浴场镇上过——她的一位姨妈在那里有一座小屋。

这消息正合劳拉的心意：她一直怕妈妈那双洞察一切的眼睛。而且小屋那边没有一个成年的亲戚要应付；只有一位看管一群男孩的老管家婆。

===Original===
Hence, when speech day was over, instead of setting out on an upcountry railway journey, Laura, under the escort of Miss Snodgrass, went on board one of the steamers that ploughed the Bay.

“I should say sea-air’ll do you good﻿—brighten you up a bit,” said the governess affably as they drove: she was in great good-humour at the prospect of losing sight for a time of the fifty-five. “You seem to be always in the dumps nowadays.”

Laura dutifully waved her handkerchief from the deck of the *Silver Star*; and the paddles began to churn. As Miss Snodgrass’s back retreated down the pier, and the breach between ship and land widened, she settled herself on her seat with a feeling of immense relief. At last﻿—at last she was off. The morning had been a sore trial to her: in all the noisy and effusive leave-taking, she was odd man out; no one had been sorry to part from her; no one had extracted a promise that she would write. Her sole valediction had been a minatory shaft from Maria: if she valued her skin, to learn to stop telling crams before she showed up there again. Now, she was free of them; she would not be humiliated afresh, would not need to stand eye to eye with anyone who knew of her disgrace, for weeks to come; perhaps never again, if Mother agreed. Her heart grew momentarily lighter. And the farther they left Melbourne behind them, the higher her spirits rose.

===Chinese===
于是，结业典礼一过，劳拉没有动身去坐回内地的火车，而是在斯诺德格拉斯小姐的护送下，登上了一艘犁开海湾水面的轮船。

「我看海边的空气对你有好处——能叫你精神精神。」驱车赶路的当儿，女教师和和气气地说：一想到有那么一阵子看不见那五十五个人，她的心情好得很。「你这些日子，好像老是蔫头蔫脑的。」

劳拉在「银星号」（*Silver Star*）的甲板上规规矩矩挥了挥手帕；明轮开始搅水。斯诺德格拉斯小姐的背影沿着码头越退越远，船与岸之间的豁口越拉越宽，她在座位上落了座，心头涌起一阵巨大的轻松。终于——终于她脱身了。这天上午对她是场难堪的考验：在满耳喧腾、热烈的话别声里，唯独她是个多出来的人；没有一个人为跟她分手感到难过；没有一个人非要逼她许下写信的诺言。给她送行的唯一临别赠言，是玛丽亚放来的一支恫吓的冷箭：她若还惜命，趁没再回去露面之前，先学会改掉扯瞎话的毛病。眼下，她把他们甩脱了；往后的几个星期，她不会再受新的羞辱，不必跟任何知道她丑事的人眼对眼站着；要是妈妈点了头，兴许永远都不必了。她的心一分一分地轻快起来。墨尔本被甩得越远，她的兴头就涨得越高。

===Original===
But then, too, was it possible, on this radiant December day, long to remain in what Miss Snodgrass had called “the dumps”?﻿—The sea was a blue-green mirror, on the surface of which they swam. The sky was a stretched sheet of blue, in which the sun hung a very ball of fire. But the steamer cooled the air as it moved; and none of the white-clad people who, under the stretched white awnings, thronged the deck, felt oppressed by the great heat. In the middle of the deck, a brass band played popular tunes.

At a pretty watering-place where they stopped, Laura rose and crossed to the opposite railing. A number of passengers went ashore, pushing and laughing, but almost as many more came on board, all dressed in white, and with eager, animated faces. Then the boat stood to sea again and sailed past high, grass-grown cliffs, from which a few old cannons, pointing their noses at you, watched over the safety of the Bay﻿—in the event, say, of the Japanese or the Russians entering the Heads past the pretty township, and the beflagged bathing-enclosures on the beach below. They neared the tall, granite lighthouse at the point, with the flagstaff at its side where incoming steamers were signalled; and as soon as they had rounded this corner they were in view of the Heads themselves. From the distant cliffs there ran out, on either side, brown reefs, which made the inrushing water dance and foam, and the entrance to the Bay narrow and dangerous: on one side, there projected the portion of a wreck which had lain there as long as Laura had been in the world. Then, having made a sharp turn to the left, the boat crossed to the opposite coast, and steamed past barrack-like buildings lying asleep in the fierce sunshine of the afternoon; and, in due course, it stopped at Laura’s destination.

===Chinese===
不过话说回来，在这十二月晴媚的一天里，难道还可能长久陷在斯诺德格拉斯小姐所谓的『蔫头蔫脑』里吗？——大海是一面蓝绿色的镜子，船就在镜面上游。天空是一匹绷开的蓝布，太阳悬在正中，活脱脱一团火球。但轮船一路行驶，把空气搅凉了；绷开的白凉篷底下，把甲板挤得满满当当的一身白衣的人们，谁也不觉得酷热难当。甲板正中，一支铜管乐队吹奏着流行曲子。

船在一个秀丽的海滨镇停靠，劳拉起身走到对面的栏杆边。一批乘客推推搡搡、笑哈哈地上了岸，可几乎又有同样多的人上了船，个个遍体白衣，一张张热切生动的脸。随后船重新站到海上，驶过高高的长满荒草的峭壁；壁上几尊老掉牙的大炮支起炮口对准你，守卫着海湾的安全——比如说，防备日本人或是俄国人打进海口，闯过那座漂亮小镇和镇下海滩上插满彩旗的浴场围栏。船渐渐挨近海岬上高高的花岗岩灯塔，塔边立着旗杆，进港的轮船在那儿打信号；一绕过这个拐角，海口本身便进入眼帘。从远处的峭壁两侧伸出褐色的礁石，叫涌进来的海水跳舞、翻沫，也叫海湾的入口又窄又险：一侧斜探出一截沉船的残骸，劳拉来到世上多少年，它就在那儿躺了多少年。随后，船猛地向左一拐，横渡到对岸，驶过一座座兵营似的大房子——它们在午后灼烈的阳光里酣睡着；不多时，船停在劳拉的目的地。

===Original===
Old Anne was waiting on the jetty, having hitched the horse to a post: she had driven in, in the “shandrydan,” to meet Laura. For the cottage was not on the front beach, with the hotels and boardinghouses, the fenced-in baths and great gentle slope of yellow sand: it stood in the bush, on the back beach, which gave to the open sea.

Laura took her seat beside the old woman in her linen sunbonnet, the body of the vehicle being packed full of groceries and other stores; and the drive began. Directly they were clear of the township the road as good as ceased, became a mere sandy track, running through a scrub of ti-trees. And what sand! White, dry, sliding sand, through which the horse shuffled and floundered, in which the wheels sank and stuck. Had one of the many hillocks to be taken, the two on the box-seat instinctively threw their weight forward; old Anne, who had a stripped wattle-bough for a whip, urged and cajoled; and more than once she handed Laura the reins and got down, to give the horse a pull. They had always to be ducking their heads, too, to let the low ti-tree branches sweep over their backs.

About a couple of miles out, the old woman alighted and slipped a rail; and having passed the only other house within cooee, they drove through a paddock, but at a walking-pace, because of the thousands of rabbit-burrows that perforated the ground. Another slip-rail lowered, they drew up at the foot of a steepish hill, beside a sandy little vegetable garden, a shed and a pump. The house was perched on the top of the hill, and directly they sighted it they also saw Pin flying down, her sunbonnet on her neck.

===Chinese===
老安妮（Anne）在栈桥上等着，马拴在一根桩子上：她是驾着那辆哐啷作响的旧马车（shandrydan）赶来接劳拉的。因为小屋不在前滩，那边有旅馆、有寄宿公寓，有圈起来的浴场和平缓舒展的一大片黄沙坡：它坐落在丛林里，在朝着外海的后滩上。

劳拉在戴亚麻遮阳软帽的老妇人身边落了座，车厢里塞满了杂货和别的补给；行程开始了。一驶出镇子，路便差不多等于没有了，只剩一条沙道，穿行在茶树（ti-tree）丛中。好一片沙！又白、又干、又流滑的沙，马在里头跌跌撞撞、深拔浅拽，车轮陷进去，动弹不得。逢上要翻那些数不清的沙丘，坐在车夫座上的两个人便本能地把重心朝前一压；老安妮拿一根剥光了叶子的金合欢枝当鞭子，又催又哄；还不止一回把缰绳递给劳拉，自己下车去，替马拽一把。她们还得一路不住地缩头，好叫低垂的茶树枝从脊背上扫过去。

出镇约莫两英里，老妇人下车抽开了一根活动栏木；路过了左近唯一一户「库伊」（cooee，林中呼人的长啸）喊得应的人家，她们驱车穿过一片围场，却只能一步一步蹭——因为地面被成千上万的兔子洞钻得千疮百孔。又放下另一根活动栏木，她们在一座略陡的山脚下停了车，旁边是一小方沙地菜园、一间棚屋、一台水泵。房子高踞在山顶上，她们一眼望见它，也一眼望见萍连飞带跑地冲下山来，遮阳帽甩在脖颈后头。

===Original===
“Laura, Laura! Oh, I *am* glad you’ve come. What a time you’ve been!”

“Hullo, Pin. Oh, I say, let me get out first.”

“And pull up your bonnet, honey. D’you want to be after gettin’ sunstruck?”

===Chinese===
「劳拉，劳拉！哎呀，你来了我真*是*高兴。你可去了这么久呀！」

「哈喽，萍。嗐，我说，先让我下车呀。」

「还有，把帽子系系好，宝贝儿。你想叫太阳晒中了暑哇？」

===Original===
Glad though Laura was to see her sister again, she did not manage to infuse a very hearty tone into her greeting; for her first glimpse of Pin had given her a disagreeable shock. It was astonishing, the change the past half-year had worked in the child; and as the two climbed the hill together, to the accompaniment of Pin’s bubbly talk, Laura stole look after look at her little sister, in the hope of growing used to what she saw. Pin had never been pretty, but now she was “downright hideous”﻿—as Laura phrased it to herself. Eleven years of age, she had at last begun to grow in earnest: her legs were as of old mere spindleshanks, but nearly twice as long; and her fat little body, perched above them, made one think of a shrivelled-up old man who has run all to paunch. Her face, too, had increased in shapelessness, the features being blurred in the fat mass; her blue eyes were more slit-like than before; and, to cap everything, her fine skin had absolutely no chance, so bespattered was it with freckles. And none of your pretty little sun-kisses; but large, black, irregular freckles that disfigured like moles. Laura felt quite distressed; it outraged her feelings that anyone belonging to her should be so ugly; and as Pin, in happy ignorance of her sister’s reflections, chattered on, Laura turned over in her mind what she ought to do. She would have to tell Pin about herself﻿—that was plain: she must break the news to her, in case others should do it, and more cruelly. It was one consolation to know that Pin was not sensitive about her looks; so long as you did not tease her about her legs, there was no limit to what you might say to her: the grieving was all for the onlooker. But not today: this was the first day; and there were pleasanter things to think of. And so, when they had had tea﻿—with condensed milk in it, for the cow had gone dry, and no milkman came out so far﻿—when tea was over﻿—and that was all that could be undertaken in the way of refreshment after the journey; washing your face and hands, for instance, was out of the question; every drop of water had to be carried up the hill from the pump, and old Anne purposely kept the ewers empty by day; if you *would* wash, you must wash in the sea﻿—as soon, then, as tea was over, the two sisters made for the beach.

===Chinese===
尽管再见到妹妹心里是高兴的，劳拉的招呼里却挤不出多少真心实意的热乎气；因为萍给她的第一眼，就叫她结结实实吃了一惊。半年工夫在这孩子身上造成的变化，真叫人咋舌；姐妹俩一路往山上爬，萍的话像开了锅似的咕嘟咕嘟直冒，劳拉则一遍又一遍偷眼打量小妹妹，指望看惯眼前这副模样。萍从来就算不得漂亮，可如今简直是『丑得没治了』——劳拉在心里这样措辞。她十一岁，总算开始正经长个儿了：两条腿照旧是两根细脚杆，却比先前长出了将近一倍；蹲在上头的那具胖乎乎的小身子，叫人想起一个干瘪缩皱、独独肚子滚圆的老头儿。她的脸也越发没了形状，五官在肉堆里糊成一团；蓝眼睛比先前更眯成了缝；到头来，她那身细皮嫩肉是彻底没救了——满脸满身溅满了雀斑。还不是那种秀气的小晒斑；是又大又黑、七歪八扭的雀斑，像痣一样毁人脸面。劳拉心里老大难受；自家骨肉竟出落得这样丑，她的体面仿佛都受了玷辱；萍呢，对姐姐的腹诽浑然不觉，兀自叽叽喳喳说个不住，劳拉一面在心里盘算自己该怎么办。她总得把自己的事对萍说破——这是明摆着的：她必须亲口捅破这层窗户纸，省得别人去捅，捅得更狠。可堪告慰的是，萍对自己的长相并不在意；只要你不拿她的两条腿打趣，随你怎么说都使得：难过全是替旁观的人难过的。可不能挑今天：今天是头一天；还有的是赏心乐事要想。于是，喝过茶——茶里兑的是炼乳，因为奶牛断了奶，送奶的又跑不到这么远——茶一喝完——旅途劳顿之后，能招待的也就这一顿了；比如说，洗脸洗手，想也不用想；每一滴水都得从山脚的水泵挑上山来，老安妮白天还故意叫水罐空着；你当真要洗，就只好下海去洗——茶点一完，姐妹俩便动身上海滩去了。

===Original===
The four-roomed, weatherboard cottage, to which at a later date a lean-to had been added, faced the bush: from the verandah there was a wide view of the surrounding country. Between the back of the house and the beach rose a huge sand-hill, sparsely grown with rushes and coarse grass. It took you some twenty minutes to toil over this, and boots and stockings were useless impedimenta; for the sand was once more of that loose and shifting kind in which you sank at times up to the knees, falling back one step for every two you climbed. But then, sand was the prevailing note of this free and easy life: it bestrewed verandah and floors; you carried it in your clothes; the beds were full of it; it even got into the food; and you were soon so accustomed to its presence that you missed the grit of it under foot, or the prickling on your skin, did old Anne happen to take a broom in her hand, or thoroughly remake the beds. When, however, on your way to the beach you had laboriously attained the summit of the great dune, the sight that met you almost took your breath away: as far as the eye could reach, the bluest of skies melting into the bluest of seas, which broke its foam-flecked edge against the flat, brown reefs that fringed the shore. Then, downhill﻿—with a trip and a flounder that sent the sand man-high﻿—and at last you were on what Laura and Pin thought the most wonderful beach in the world. What a variety of things was there! Whitest, purest sand, hot to the touch as a zinc roof in summer; rocky caves, and sandy caves hung with crumbly stalactites; at low tide, on the reef, lakes and ponds and rivers deep enough to make it unnecessary for you to go near the ever-angry surf at all; seaweeds that ran through the gamut of colours: brown and green, pearl-pink and coral-pink, to vivid scarlet and orange; shells, beginning with tiny grannies and cowries, and ending with the monsters in which the breakers had left their echo; the bones of cuttlefish, light as paper, and shaped like javelins. And, what was best of all, this beach belonged to them alone; they had not to share its treasures with strangers; except the inhabitants of the cottage, never a soul set foot upon it.

===Chinese===
这座四居室的木板小屋，后来又添盖了一间披屋，面朝丛林：从游廊上望出去，四下里的乡野一览无余。屋子背后同海滩之间，隆起一座巨大的沙山，稀稀落落长着芦草和粗硬的野草。翻过它得苦苦爬上二十来分钟，靴子和长袜纯属无用的累赘；因为这沙子又是那种松散流滑的性子，人时不时陷到膝盖，每爬两步就得倒退一步。不过话说回来，沙子本就是这种自由自在生活的主调：它铺满游廊和地板；你衣服里揣着它；床铺里塞满它；它甚至钻进吃食；用不了多久，你对它的存在便习以为常，倘若老安妮一时兴起抄起扫帚，或者把床铺彻底重铺一遍，你反倒要惦记脚底下少了那股咯吱劲儿、皮肤上少了那阵刺痒。然而，去海滩的路上，当你气喘吁吁爬上大沙丘的顶，迎面撞见的景致几乎叫你背过气去：目力所及，蓝到极处的天融进蓝到极处的海，海把缀满白沫的边沿，拍碎在镶着海岸的平坦褐礁上。接着下山——一个趔趄接一个趔趄，扬起一人高的沙子——你终于站到了劳拉和萍心目中全世界最奇妙的海滩上。那里的物事何其丰盛！最白最纯的沙子，摸上去跟夏天的锌皮屋顶一样烫手；石头洞，还有垂着酥脆钟乳石的沙洞；退潮时，礁石上处处是湖、塘和河，水足够深，你压根儿不必去沾那永远在动怒的拍岸浪；海藻的颜色跑遍了全音阶：棕色和绿色，珠粉和珊瑚粉，一路到鲜红和橙黄；贝壳，小起迷你小螺（grannies）和宝贝贝（cowries），大到大浪把回声留在里头的巨贝；还有乌贼骨，轻得像纸，形状像标枪。而顶顶好的是，这片海滩只属于她们两个；她们不必同外人分它的宝藏；除了小屋里住的这几口人，从来没有一个生灵踩上过它。

===Original===
The chief business of the morning was to bathe. If the girls were alone and the tide full, they threw off their clothes and ran into a sandy, shallow pool, where the water never came above their waists, and where it was safe to let the breakers dash over them. But if the tide were low, the boys bathed, too, and then Pin and Laura tied themselves up in old bathing-gowns that were too big for them, and all went in a body to the “Half-Moon Hole.” This pool, which was about twenty feet long and ten to fifteen deep, lay far out on the reef, and, at high tide, was hidden beneath surf and foam; at low water, on the other hand, it was like a glass mirror reflecting the sky, and so clear that you could see every weed that waved at the bottom. Having cast off your shoes, you applied your soles gingerly to the prickles of the rock; then plop!﻿—and in you went. Pin often needed a shove from behind, for nowhere, of course, could you get a footing; but Laura swam with the best. Some of the boys would dive to the bottom and bring up weeds and shells, but Laura and Pin kept on the surface of the water; for they had the imaginative dread common to children who know the sea well﻿—the dread of what may lurk beneath the thick, black horrors of seaweed.

===Chinese===
清早的头等大事是下海洗澡。若是姑娘们自己单独行动、又赶上满潮，她们脱光衣裳，跑进一个沙底的浅水潭，水从来漫不过腰，任凭碎浪当头砸下来也稳稳当当。可若是退了潮，男孩们也要洗，萍和劳拉便把自己五花大绑地裹进肥大不合身的旧式浴袍里，大伙儿成群结伙去「半月潭」。这潭约莫二十英尺长、十到十五英尺深，远远卧在礁石外端，涨潮时没在碎浪白沫底下；退了水，它却像一面映天的玻璃镜，清得潭底摇曳的每一根水草都数得见。脱了鞋，脚掌小心翼翼踩上礁石硌人的尖刺；扑通！——人就进去了。萍常常要在背后挨一掌，因为四下里自然没有一处立得住脚；劳拉却游得不输最棒的。有几个男孩会潜到潭底，捞上水草贝壳，劳拉和萍只肯浮在水面上；因为她们怀着熟悉大海的孩子们共有的那种想象出来的恐惧——怕那又厚又黑、阴惨惨的海藻底下，潜伏着什么东西。

===Original===
Then, after an hour or so in the water, home to dinner, hungry as swagmen, though the bill of fare never varied: it was always rabbit for dinner, crayfish for tea; for the butcher called only once a week, and meat could not be kept an hour without getting flyblown. The rabbits were skinned and in the stew-pot before they were cold; the crayfish died an instant death: one that drove the blood to Laura’s head, and made Pin run away and cry, with her fingers to her ears; for she believed the sizzling of the water, as the fish were dropped in, to be the shriek of the creatures in their death-agony.

Except in bathing, the girls saw little of the boys. Both were afraid of guns, so did not go out on the expeditions which supplied the dinner-table; and old Anne would not allow them to join the crayfishing excursions. For these took place by night, off the end of the reef, with nets and torches; and it sometimes happened, if the surf were heavy, that one of the fishers was washed off the rocks, and only hauled up again with considerable difficulty.

===Chinese===
然后，在水里泡上一个来钟头，便回家吃正餐，饿得像两个背蓝包袱的流浪汉——虽说菜单从不翻新：正餐顿顿是兔子，茶点顿顿是龙虾；因为卖肉的每星期只来一趟，肉搁不了一个钟头，就要招苍蝇下蛆。兔子还没凉透就剥了皮下进了炖锅；龙虾的死则是一瞬间的事：那种死法直冲得劳拉热血上头，萍则捂着耳朵跑开去哭；因为她认定，龙虾倒进锅里时水花那阵嘶响，便是这些小东西临死挣扎的惨叫。

除了洗澡，姑娘们跟男孩们少见面。她们俩都怕枪，所以不去参加那些为饭桌张罗吃食的出猎；老安妮也不许她们跟着去捕龙虾。因为那都是夜里干的营生，在礁石外端，张着网、打着火把；碰上浪头大的时候，有时会有哪个打鱼的一下子被卷下礁石，要好费一番周折才重新拽上来。

===Original===
Laura took her last peep at the outside world, every evening, in the brief span of time between sunset and dark. Running up to the top of one of the hills, and letting her eyes range over sky and sea, she would drink in the scents that were waking to life after the burning heat of the day: salt water, warmed sand and seaweeds, ti-scrub, sour-grass, and the sturdy berry-bushes, high as her knee, through which she had ploughed her way. That was one of the moments she liked best, that, and lying in bed at night listening to the roar of the surf, which went on and on like a cannonade, even though the hill lay between. It made her flesh crawl, too, in delightful fashion, did she picture to herself how alone she and Pin were, in their room: the boys slept in the lean-to on the other side of the kitchen; old Anne at the back. For miles round, no house broke the solitude of the bush; only a thin wooden partition separated her from possible bushrangers, from the vastness and desolation of the night, the eternal booming of the sea.

Such was the life into which Laura now threw herself heart and soul, forgetting, in the sheer joy of living, her recent tribulation.

===Chinese===
每天傍晚，在日落与天黑之间短短一段光阴里，劳拉要向外面的世界投去最后一瞥。她跑到一座山顶上，放任目光扫过天和海，痛饮那些经白昼灼烤之后苏醒过来的气息：盐水、晒暖的沙和海藻、茶树丛、酸草，还有齐她膝盖高的茁壮浆果丛——她是拨开它们一路蹚上来的。这是她顶欢喜的时刻之一；另一个，是夜里躺在床上，听拍岸浪的轰鸣，那声音像开炮似的连绵不断，尽管中间隔着一座山。而当她设想起自己和萍在房间里何等孤零零时，又会有一种酥麻快意的战栗：男孩们睡在厨房那一头的披屋里；老安妮睡在后边。方圆好几英里，没有一座房子打破丛林的荒寂；只有一层薄薄的木板壁，把她同可能摸来的丛林悍匪、同黑夜的浩大荒凉、同大海永无休止的咆哮隔开。

劳拉如今一头扎进这样的生活里，全心全意，凭着纯粹的生之欢悦，把新近的磨难抛到了脑后。

===Original===
But even the purest pleasures *will* pall; and after a time, when the bloom had worn off and the newness and her mind was more at leisure again, she made some disagreeable discoveries which ruffled her tranquillity.

It was Pin, poor, fat, little well-meaning Pin, who did the mischief.

===Chinese===
可就算是最纯粹的乐趣，也*终究*会腻；过了一些时候，光鲜磨掉了、新鲜劲儿过去了，她的心思又闲了下来，便有了几桩败兴的发现，搅乱了她的太平。

闯下这祸的，是萍——可怜的、胖乎乎的、一心好意的萍。

===Original===
Pin was not only changed in looks; her character had changed, too; and in so marked a way that before a week was out the sisters were at loggerheads. Each day made it plainer to Laura that Pin was developing a sturdy independence; she had ceased to look up to Laura as a prodigy of wisdom, and had begun to hold opinions of her own. She was, indeed, even disposed to be critical of her sister; and criticism from this quarter was more than Laura could brook: it was just as if a slave usurped his master’s rights. At first speechless with surprise, she ended by losing her temper; the more, because Pin was prone to be mulish, and could not be got to budge, either by derision or by scorn, from her espoused views. They were those of the school at which for the past half-year she had been a day-pupil, and seemed to her unassailable. Laura found them ridiculous, as she did much else about Pin at this time: her ugliness, her setting herself up as an authority: and she jeered unkindly whenever Pin came out with them. A still more ludicrous thing was that, despite her plainness, Pin actually had an admirer. True, she did not say so outright; perhaps she was not even aware of it; but Laura gathered from her talk that a boy at her school, a boy some three years older than herself, had given her a silk handkerchief and liked to help her with her sums. And to Laura this was the most knockdown blow of all.

===Chinese===
萍不光模样变了；性子也变了；而且变得这样扎眼，不出一个星期，姐妹俩就顶起牛来。每一天都叫劳拉看得更明白：萍正养成一股扎扎实实的独立劲儿；她不再把劳拉当智慧的奇才仰着看，开始有了自己的主见。她甚至摆出挑剔姐姐的架势；从这一头来的挑剔，劳拉可咽不下：那简直像奴才僭越了主子的权利。她先是惊得目瞪口呆，末了便按捺不住发了脾气；更何况萍天生一副骡子性子，讥也好、嘲也好，都休想叫她在认下的主见上挪动半分。那些主见，出自她当走读生上了半年的那所学校，在她看来固若金汤。劳拉只觉得它们荒唐，正如她这一阵觉得萍身上许多别的都荒唐一样：她的丑，她的自封权威：所以萍一搬出这些，她便刻薄地讥笑。更叫人笑掉大牙的是，萍虽生得寒碜，居然真有一位爱慕者。不错，她没有直说；兴许连自己都没觉出来；可劳拉从她的话缝里听出来，她学校里有个男生，比她大三岁，送过她一方丝手帕，还乐意帮她演算术题。在劳拉听来，这一记最具杀伤力。

===Original===
One day it came to an open quarrel between them.

They were lying on the beach after bathing, trying to protect their bare and blistered legs from the sandflies. Laura, flat on her back, had spread a towel over hers; Pin sat Turk-fashion with her legs beneath her and fought the flies with her hands. Having vainly endeavoured to draw from the reticent Laura some of those school-tales of which, in former holidays, she had been so prodigal, Pin was now chattering to her heart’s content, about the small doings of home. Laura listened to her with the impatient toleration of one who has seen the world: she really could not be expected to interest herself in such trifles; and she laughed in her sleeve at Pin’s simpleness. When, however, her little sister began to enlarge anew on some wonderful orders Mother had lately had, she could not refrain from saying crossly: “You’ve told me that a dozen times already. And you needn’t bawl it out for everyone to hear.”

===Chinese===
有一天，两人到底公开吵翻了。

那是洗完澡躺在海滩上，护着晒起泡的光腿躲蠓虫。劳拉仰面朝天躺着，往腿上搭了一条毛巾；萍土耳其式盘腿坐着，两条腿压在身下，光靠两只手驱赶蠓虫。萍白白地想从守口如瓶的劳拉那里掏出些学校的故事——往年放假，她讲这些故事是何等大手大脚——如今只好恣情恣意，唠叨家里那些鸡零狗碎。劳拉带着见过世面的人那种不耐烦的宽容听着：这样的芝麻绿豆，实在不能指望她提起兴趣；她又暗地里笑话萍的天真。可是，等小妹妹重新絮叨起妈妈新近接下的一批了不得的活计，她忍不住气冲冲地说：「这件事你已经讲了十几遍了。也不必扯着嗓子嚷嚷得人人都听见。」

===Original===
“Oh, Laura! there isn’t anyone anywhere near us﻿ ﻿… and even if there were﻿—why, I thought you’d be so pleased. Mother’s going to give you an extra shilling pocket-money, ’cause of it.”

“Of course I’m pleased. Don’t be so silly, Pin.”

“I’m not *always* silly, Laura,” protested Pin. “And I don’t believe you *are* glad, a bit. Old Anne was, though. She said: ‘Bless her dear heart!’ ”

“Old Anne? Well, I just wonder what next! It’s none of her dashed business.”
