“Oh, Laura!” began Pin, growing tearful both at words and tone. “Why, Laura, you’re not ashamed of it, are you?﻿—that mother does sewing?”﻿—and Pin opened her lobelia-blue eyes to their widest, showing what very big eyes they would be, were they not so often swollen with crying.

===Chinese===
「哦，劳拉！这左近根本一个人也没有嘛……就算有——咦，我还当你听了不知多高兴呢。妈妈要因为这个多给你一先令零花钱。」

「我当然高兴。别这么犯傻，萍。」

「我又不是*整天*犯傻，劳拉。」萍抗议道。「你呀，我才不信你有半点儿高兴。老安妮倒是真高兴。她说：『老天保佑她那颗亲爱的心！』」

「老安妮？嗬，真是新鲜事儿层出不穷！这根本不关她那该死的事。」

「哦，劳拉！」萍开了腔，话头话尾都叫她眼泪汪汪。「咦，劳拉，你不会为这个害臊吧，啊？——妈妈给人做针线活呀？」说着，萍把她那双半边莲色的蓝眼睛睁到了最大，叫人看得出，这双眼睛要不是常哭得发肿，该有多么大。

===Original===
“Of course not,” said Laura tartly. “But I’m blessed if I can see what it’s got to do with old Anne.”

“But she asked me﻿ ﻿… what mother was working at﻿—and if she’d got any new customers. She just loves mother.”

“Like her cheek!” snapped Laura. “Poking her ugly old nose into what doesn’t concern her. You should just have said you didn’t know.”

“But that would have been a story, Laura!” cried Pin, horrified, “I did know﻿—quite well.”

“Goodness gracious, Pin, you﻿—”

===Chinese===
「当然不害臊。」劳拉尖刻地说。「可我死活闹不明白，这跟老安妮有什么相干。」

「可她问过我……妈妈手头在做什么活计——还问有没有揽到新主顾。她就是疼妈妈嘛。」

「好大的脸皮！」劳拉呵斥道。「把她那张又老又丑的鼻子，伸到不关她的事里嗅。你就该干脆说你不知道。」

「可那样就是扯瞎话呀，劳拉！」萍吓了一跳，叫道。「我明明知道——知道得清清楚楚。」

「老天爷，萍，你——」

===Original===
“I’ve never told a story in my life,” said Pin hotly. “And I’m not going to either, for you or anyone. I think you ought to be ashamed of yourself.”

“Hold your silly tongue!”

“I shan’t, Laura. And I think you’re very wicked. You’re not a bit like what you used to be. And it’s all going to school that’s done it﻿—Mother says it is.”

“Oh, don’t be such a blooming ass!” and Laura, stung to the quick, retaliated by taunting Pin with the change that had come to pass in her appearance. To her surprise, she found Pin grown inordinately touchy about her looks: at Laura’s brutal statement of the truth she cried bitterly.

===Chinese===
「我这辈子还从来没说过一句瞎话。」萍涨红了脸说。「往后也不会，为了你，为了谁都不撒。我看你倒该自个儿害臊。」

「闭上你那张傻嘴！」

「我就不闭，劳拉。我看你坏得很。你一点儿也不像从前的你了。全是上学上成的这样——妈妈说的。」

「哦，别当这么头蠢驴！」劳拉被戳到心窝，反手就拿萍相貌上发生的变化回敬她。谁知她发现，萍对自己的长相已经变得出奇地碰不得：劳拉把实情残忍地一摆，她便伤心地大哭起来。

===Original===
“I’m not, no, I’m not! I haven’t got a full moon for a face! It’s no fatter than yours. Sarah said last time you were home how fat you were getting.”

“I’m sure I’m not,” said Laura, indignant in her turn.

“Yes, you are,” sobbed Pin. “But you only think other people are ugly, not yourself. I’ll tell mother what you’ve said as soon as ever I get home. And I’ll tell her, too, you want to make me tell stories. And that I’m sure you’ve done something naughty at school, ’cause you won’t ever talk about it. And how you’re always saying bad words like blooming and gosh and golly﻿—yes, I will!”

===Chinese===
「我不丑，不，我不丑！我的脸又不是满月！它才不比你的胖。上次你回家，萨拉还说你胖成什么样了呢。」

「我肯定不胖。」劳拉说，这回轮到她动了气。

「你就是胖。」萍抽抽搭搭地说。「可你光会嫌别人丑，不嫌你自己。我一到家，就把你说的话原原本本告诉妈妈。我还要告诉她，你想叫我说瞎话。还有，你在学校准是干了什么见不得人的事，因为你怎么也不肯提。还有你老是说那些难听的字眼，什么『该死的』『天哪』『乖乖』——对，我就是要告状！」

===Original===
“You were always a sneak and a telltale.”

“And you were always a greedy, selfish, deceitful thing.”

“You don’t know anything about me, you numbskull, you!”

“I don’t want to! I know you’re a bad, wicked girl.”

===Chinese===
「你从来就是个小告密的，就会打小报告。」

「你从来就是个又贪嘴、又自私、又会哄骗人的东西。」

「你对我什么都不知道，你这个榆木脑袋！」

「我才不要知道！我知道你是个又坏又恶毒的丫头。」

===Original===
After this exchange of home truths, they did not speak to each other for two days: Pin had a temper that smouldered, and could not easily forgive. So she stayed at old Anne’s side, helping to bake scones and leatherjackets; or trotted after the boys, who had dropped into the way of saying: “Come on, little Pin!” as they never said: “Come on, Laura!” and Laura retired in lonely dudgeon to the beach.

===Chinese===
这一场互抖家底的对骂过后，姐妹俩两天没再开口：萍是闷火的性子，轻易记仇不饶人。于是她守在老安妮身边，帮着烤司康饼和剥皮鱼（leatherjackets）；要不就跟在男孩们屁股后头小跑——男孩们叫顺了口：「来呀，小萍！」却从来不叫：「来呀，劳拉！」劳拉便悻悻然独自退到海滩上去。

===Original===
She took the estrangement so much to heart that she eased her feelings by abusing Pin in thought; Pin was a pigheaded little ignoramus, as timid as ever of setting one foot before the other. And the rest of them would be just the same﻿—old stick-in-the muds, unchanged by a hair, or, if they *had* changed, then changed for the worse. Laura had somehow never foreseen the day on which she would find herself out of tune with her home circle; with unthinking assurance she had expected that Pin, for instance, would always be eager to keep pace with her. Now, she saw that her little sister would probably never catch up to her again. Such progress as Pin might make﻿—if she were not already glued firm to her silly notions﻿—would be in quite another direction. For the quarrel had made one thing plain to Laura: with regard to her troubles, she need not look to Pin for sympathy: if Pin talked such gibberish at the hint of putting off an inquisitive old woman, what would she﻿—and not she alone﻿—what would they all say to the tissue of lies Laura had spun round Mr. Shepherd, a holy man, a clergyman, and a personal friend of Mother’s into the bargain? She could not blink the fact that, did it come to their ears, they would call her in earnest, what Pin had called her in her temper﻿—bad and wicked. Home was, alas! no longer the snug nest in which she was safe from the slings and shanghais of the world.

===Chinese===
这场反目让她伤了心，只好在心里痛骂萍出气：萍是个脑子不开窍的犟丫头，胆子还是跟从前一样小，先迈哪只脚都要掂量三遍。家里其余的人也准是一个样——一群老古板，一根头发丝儿也没变；就算*当真*变了，也是越变越糟。不知怎么，劳拉从来没料到会有这么一天：她竟跟家里的人合不上拍了；她想都不想地认定，就说萍吧，会永远急着追随她的脚步。如今她看清了，小妹妹多半永远也追不上她了。萍纵然还会有长进——倘若她还没黏死在她那些傻念头上——也是朝完全另一个方向去了。因为这场吵嘴让劳拉明白了一件事：说到自己的那些祸事，休想从萍这里讨到同情：不过是示意搪塞一个爱打听的老太婆，萍就说出这等浑话，那么她——而且不止她一个——他们所有的人，听了劳拉绕着谢泼德先生——一位圣洁的人、一位教士，外加还是妈妈的一位私交——编下的那一整套谎，又会说出什么来呢？她没法对这桩事实闭眼：这话一旦传进他们耳朵，他们就会正儿八经地叫她萍在气头上叫过的那两个词——又坏又恶毒。家啊，唉！再也不是那个护着她躲开世人飞石与弹弓的暖窝了。

===Original===
And then there was another thing: did she stay at home, she would have to relive herself into the thousand and one gimcrack concerns, which now, as set forth by Pin, so bored her: the colic Leppie had brought on by eating unripe fruit; the fact that another of Sarah’s teeth had dropped out without extraneous aid. It was all very well for a week or two, but, at the idea of shutting herself wholly up with such mopokes, of cutting herself off from her present vital interests, Laura hastily reconsidered her decision to leave school. No: badly as she had suffered at her companions’ hands, much as she dreaded returning, it was at school she belonged. All her heart was there: in the doings of her equals, the things that really mattered﻿—who would be promoted, who prefect, whose seat changed in the dining-hall. Besides, could one who had experienced the iron rule of Mr. Strachey, or Mrs. Gurley, ever be content to go back and just form one of a family of children? She not, at any rate!

===Chinese===
再有还有一桩：倘若待在家里，她就得把自己重新活进一千零一件花哨而无谓的琐事里——如今由萍一一转述起来、听得她腻烦透顶的那些事：莱皮贪吃没熟的果子闹起的肚子痛；萨拉又一颗牙没借外力、自己掉了下来。熬上一两个星期自然无妨，可一想到要同这样一群闷葫芦（mopokes）成天关死在一处，把自己同眼下切身的大事一刀两断，劳拉急急忙忙重新掂量起退学的决定。不：在同学们手里受的罪再重，回去的路再叫她胆寒，她终究是属于学校的。她整颗心都在那边：在她的同辈人的种种动静里，在那些真正要紧的事情里——谁升了级，谁当了级长，谁的座次在饭厅里挪动了。再说，一个尝过斯特雷奇先生或格利太太铁腕滋味的人，难道还能心满意足地回去，只做一群孩子里的一个吗？反正她不能！

===Original===
Thus she lay, all day long, her hands clasped under her neck, a small white speck on the great wave-lapped beach. She watched the surf break, watched the waves creep up and hide the reef, watched the gulls vanish in the sun-saturated blue overhead. Sometimes she rose to her elbow to follow a ship just inside the horizon; and it pleased her to think that this great boat was sailing off, with a load of lucky mortals, to some unknown, fairer world, while she, a poor Cinderella, had to stop behind﻿—even though she knew it was only the English mail going on to Sydney. Of Pin she preferred not to think; nor could she dwell with equanimity on her late misfortunes at school and the trials that awaited her on her reappearance; and since she *had* to think of something, she fell into the habit of making up might-have-been, of narrating to herself how things would have fallen out had her fictions been fact, her ascetic hero the impetuous lover she had made of him. In other words, lying prostrate on the sand, Laura went on with her story.

===Chinese===
就这样，她整日价躺着，双手交叠垫在颈后，是浪涛环抱的大海滩上一粒白色的小点。她看碎浪拍散，看海浪悄悄爬上来把礁石藏没，看海鸥一只只消失在头顶浸饱了阳光的蓝色里。有时她撑起胳膊肘，目送一艘刚冒出地平线的船；想到这艘大船正载着一船幸运的凡人，驶向某个未知的、更美好的世界，而她这个可怜的灰姑娘只好留下，她便觉得快意——尽管她心里有数，那不过是英国邮船接着开往悉尼去。萍，她宁可不去想；至于新近在学校里吃的那些苦头，还有再露面时等着她的那些关口，她也做不到心平气和地细细回味；既然*总得*想点什么，她便养成了编造种种「假如成真」的习惯，给自己讲述：假如她的虚构都是事实，事情会怎样收场；她那位苦行僧式的男主角，当真成了她笔下那个热烈的情人。换句话说，俯卧在沙滩上，劳拉把她的故事接着往下讲。

===Original===
When, towards the end of the third week, she and Pin were summoned to spend some days with Godmother, she had acquired such a gusto for this occupation, that she preferred to shirk reality, and let Pin pay the visit alone.

===Chinese===
到第三个星期快完的时候，她和萍被叫到教母家去住几天；可她那时干这桩营生已经上了瘾，宁可躲开现实，放萍一个人去走这趟亲。

## XV / 十五

===Original===
For days Laura avoided even thinking of this unlucky visit. Privately, she informed herself that Tilly’s wealthy relations were a “rude, stupid lot”; and, stuffing her fingers in her ears, memorised pages with a dispatch that deadened thought.

When, however, the first smart had passed and she was able to go back on what had happened, a soreness at her own failure was the abiding result: and this, though Tilly mercifully spared her the “dull as ditchwater,” that was Bob’s final verdict. But the fact that the invitation was not repeated told Laura enough.

Her hurt was not relieved by the knowledge that she had done nothing to deserve it. For she had never asked for Bob’s notice or admiration, had never thought of him but as a handsome cousin of Tilly’s who sat in a distant pew at St. Stephen’s-on-the-Hill; and the circumstance that, because he had singled her out approvingly, she was expected to worm herself into his favour, seemed to her of a monstrous injustice. But, all the same, had she possessed the power to captivate him, she would cheerfully have put her pride in her pocket. For, having once seen him close at hand, she knew how desirable he was. Having been the object of glances from those liquid eyes, of smiles from those blanched-almond teeth, she found it hard to dismiss them from her mind. How the other girls would have boasted of it, had they been chosen by such a one as Bob!﻿—they who, for the most part, were satisfied with blotchy-faced, red-handed youths, whose lean wrists dangled from their retreating sleeves. But then, too, they would have known how to keep him. Oh, those lucky other girls!
===Chinese===
接连好几天，劳拉连想都不肯去想这趟倒霉的做客。她私下里向自己宣告：蒂莉那家阔亲戚是『一伙粗鲁的蠢货』；随即把手指堵上耳朵，用一种足以麻瘪思绪的速度，一页接一页地死记硬背。

然而，等最初的刺痛过去，她能回头细想发生过的事了，留下的却是对自己失败的一股经久不散的隐痛：虽然蒂莉发了慈悲，没把鲍勃那句终审判决——『乏味得像沟里的死水』——学给她听。可是那邀请再没有第二回，这对劳拉已经说明了一切。

明知自己并没有做什么招致这份伤害的事，也无济于事。因为她从不曾乞求过鲍勃的青眼或倾慕，想起他来，也无非是蒂莉的那个漂亮表哥，坐在山上圣斯蒂芬教堂远远的长椅上；可是就因为他称许地挑中了她，众人便指望她自己钻营着去讨他的欢心——这在劳拉看来简直是骇人的不公。然而话虽如此，倘若她真有本事把他迷住，她也会心甘情愿地把自尊揣进口袋。因为自打近处见过他一面，她就知道他是多么叫人心动。既然做过那双水汪汪的眼睛顾盼的对象，领教过那一口杏仁般白亮的牙齿绽出的微笑，要再把它们从脑子里赶出去，就难了。换了别的姑娘，要是被鲍勃这样的人物挑中，会怎样地吹嘘啊！——那些姑娘，多半只能满足于满脸疙瘩、两手通红的小子，瘦伶伶的手腕从直往后缩的袖口里耷拉出来。不过话说回来，她们倒也知道怎么把他笼络住。哦，那些有福气的别的姑娘！

===Original===
“I say, Chinky, what do you do when a boy’s gone on you?”

She would have shrunk from putting an open question of this kind to her intimates; but Chinky could be trusted. For she garnered the few words Laura vouchsafed her, as gratefully as Lazarus his crumbs; and a mark of confidence, such as this, would sustain her for days.

But she had no information to give.

“Me?﻿ ﻿… why, nothing. Boys are dirty, horrid, conceited creatures.”
===Chinese===
「我说，中国眼，要是有小子迷上你了，你怎么办？」

这样的露骨问题，她是不好意思拿去问贴心朋友的；可中国眼信得过。因为劳拉赏她的那三言两语，她都像拉撒路拾掇碎屑那样感激不尽地收着；而这样一点信任的表示，也够她滋养好几天。

可是她拿不出什么高见。

「我？……嗐，什么办不办的。男孩子都是些又脏又讨厌、自以为了不起的东西。」

===Original===
In her heart Laura was at one with this judgment; but it was not to the point.

“Yes, but s’pose one was awfully sweet on you and you rather liked him?”

“Catch me! If one came bothering round me, I’d do this”﻿—and she set her ten outstretched fingers to her nose and waggled them.

And yet Chinky was rather pretty, in her way.
===Chinese===
这番评判，劳拉心底里原是赞同的；可它文不对题。

「是啦，可要是有一个特别特别地迷上了你，你也挺喜欢他呢？」

「我才不呢！要有小子到我眼前来纠缠，我就这样儿。」——说着，她把张开来的十根手指抵在鼻子上，一劲儿地晃。

话虽如此，中国眼自有她的一路姿色。

===Original===
Maria Morell, cautiously tapped, threw back her head and roared with laughter.

“Bless its little heart! Does it want to know?﻿—say, Laura, who’s your mash?”

“No one,” answered Laura stoutly. “I only asked. For I guess you *know*, Maria.”

“By gosh, you bet I do!” cried Maria, italicising the words in her vehemence. “Well, look here, Kiddy, if a chap’s sweet on me I let him be sweet, my dear, and that’s all﻿—till he’s run to barley-sugar. What I don’t let him savvy is, whether I care a twopenny damn for him. Soon as you do that, it’s all up. Just let him hang round, and throw sheep’s-eyes, till he’s as soft as a jellyfish, and when he’s right down ripe, roaring mad, go off and pretend to do a mash with someone else. That’s the way to glue him, chicken.”
===Chinese===
被小心翼翼地试探了一下的玛丽亚·莫雷尔，仰起头哈哈大笑。

「心疼它的小心肝儿哟！它想知道呀？——哎，劳拉，你的意中人是谁？」

「没有谁。」劳拉斩钉截铁地答。「我不过问问罢了。因为我猜你心里『有数』，玛丽亚。」

「好家伙，那还用说！」玛丽亚嚷道，那股激动的狠劲儿，仿佛给字字都排了斜体。「听我说，小家伙，要是有个小子迷上了我，我就由他迷去，亲爱的，就这么着——一直由他甜到熬成了大麦糖。我偏不让他弄明白的，是我到底拿他当不当一回事。你一让他弄明白了，就全完。只管由他在旁边转悠，由他抛媚眼，直等到他软得像只水母；等他熟透了、疯得嗷嗷叫了，你再走开去，装出跟别人相好的样子。这样才能把他粘住不放，小妞儿。」

===Original===
“But you don’t have anything of him that way,” objected Laura.

Maria laughed herself red in the face. “What’n earth more d’you want? Why, he’ll pester you with letters, world without end, and look as black as your shoe if you so much as wink at another boy. As for a kiss, if he gets a chance of one he’ll take it﻿—you can bet your bottom dollar on that.”

“But you never get to know him!”

“Oh, hang it, Laura, but you *are* rich! What d’you think one has a boy for, I’d like to know. To parlezvous about old Shepherd’s sermons? You loony, it’s only for getting lollies, and letters, and the whole dashed fun of the thing. If you go about too much with one, you soon have to fake an interest in his rotten old affairs. Or else just hold your tongue and let him blow. And that’s dull work. D’you think it ever comes up a fellow’s back to talk to you about your new Sunday hat! If it does, you can teach your grandmother to suck eggs.”
===Chinese===
「可那样的话，他什么也不归你呀。」劳拉提出异议。

玛丽亚笑得满脸通红。「你还想要天底下的什么呀？嗐，他的信会缠得你没完没了，你只要朝别的小子挤一下眼，他就拉下脸来，黑得跟你的鞋底似的。说到亲嘴儿——只要他捞着机会，准会亲——这一点你把压箱底的钱都押上去，也错不了。」

「可你永远也摸不透他！」

「哎呀得了，劳拉，你可真『逗』！我倒要请问，要个小子做什么用？跟他唧唧呱呱谈老谢泼德（Shepherd）的布道吗？你这小疯子，要他们不过是为了糖果呀、信呀，还有这件事里里外外的那股子乐子。要是你跟哪一个厮混得太多，很快就得装着对他那些破事儿感兴趣。再不然就闭住嘴，由他一个人吹去。那可就乏味透了。你以为一个小子会回过头来，跟你谈你新买的礼拜帽子吗！真有那样的事，你都可以教老奶奶嘬鸡蛋了。」

===Original===
But, despite this wisdom, Laura could not determine how Maria would have acted had she stood in her shoes.

And then, too, the elder girl had said nothing about another side of the question, had not touched on the sighs and simpers, the winged glances, and drooped, provocative lids﻿—all the thousand and one fooleries, in short, which Laura saw her and others employ. There was a regular machinery of invitation and encouragement to be set in motion: for, before it was safe to ignore a wooer and let him dangle, as Maria advised, you had first to make quite sure he wished to nibble your bait. And it was just in this elementary science that Laura broke down.

Looking round her, she saw mainly experts. To take the example nearest at hand: there was Monsieur Legros, the French master; well, Maria could twist him round her little finger. She only needed to pout her thick, red lips, or to give a coquettish twist to her plump figure, or to ogle him with her fine, bold, blue eyes, and the difficult questions in the lesson were sure to pass her by. Once she had even got ten extra marks added to an examination paper, in this easy fashion. Whereas, did she, Laura, try to imitate Maria, venture to pout or to smirk, it was ten to one she would be rebuked for impertinence. No, she got on best with the women-teachers, to whom red lips and a full bust meant nothing; while the most elderly masters could not be relied on to be wholly impartial, where a pair of magnificent eyes was concerned. Even Mr. Strachey, the unapproachable, had been known, on running full tilt into a pretty girl’s arms in an unlit passage, to be laughingly confused.
===Chinese===
可是，尽管有这一番高见，劳拉还是断不定：倘若处在她的地位，玛丽亚会怎么做。

再说，这位大姑娘对问题的另一面只字未提，根本没有触及那些叹息和假笑、长了翅膀的眼风、撩人地垂下的眼皮——总而言之，劳拉眼见她本人和别人使的一千零一样傻把戏，她一样也没讲。那是有一整套邀请和鼓励的机器要开动起来的：因为，照玛丽亚的主意，要安心地不理一个追求者、由他吊在那儿，你先得十拿九稳，确知他有心来咬你的饵。而恰恰在这门入门的学问上，劳拉败下阵来。

环顾四周，她看到的差不多全是行家。拿最近便的例子来说：法语教师勒格罗先生（Monsieur Legros）——哼，玛丽亚能把他绕着自己的小指头转。她只消嘟起那两片厚厚的红嘴唇，或是让丰满的身段风骚地一拧，或是用那双漂亮的、大胆的蓝眼睛朝他飞个眼波，课上的难题就准会绕过她去。有一回，她甚至靠这个轻省法子，在一份考卷上多得了十分。反过来，要是她劳拉学玛丽亚的样，斗胆嘟一嘟嘴、飞一个假笑，十有八九要挨一顿『放肆』的申斥。不，她跟女教师们处得最合适，红嘴唇和饱满的胸脯在她们那儿一钱不值；而那些上了年纪的男教师，一关系到一双出众的眼睛，也难保个个全然公正。连高不可攀的斯特雷奇先生也出过这样的事：大家知道，有一回他在一条没点灯的走廊里，一头撞进一个漂亮姑娘的怀抱，笑得手忙脚乱。

===Original===
Laura was not, of course, the sole outsider in these things; sprinkled through the College were various others, older, too, than she, who by reason of demureness of temperament, or immersion in their work, stood aloof. But they were lost in the majority, and, as it chanced, none of them belonged to Laura’s circle. Except Chinky﻿—and Chinky did not count. So, half-fascinated, half-repelled, Laura set to studying her friends with renewed zeal. She could not help admiring their proficiency in the art of pleasing, even though she felt a little abashed by the open pride they took in their growing charms. There was Bertha, for instance, Bertha who had one of the nicest minds of them all; and yet how frankly gratified she was, by the visible rounding of her arms and the curving of her bust. She spoke of it to Laura with a kind of awe; and her voice seemed to give hints of a coming mystery. Tilly, on the other hand, lived to reduce her waist-measure: she was always sucking at lemons, and she put up with the pains of indigestion as well as a red tip to her nose; for no success in school meant as much to Tilly as the fact that she had managed to compress herself a further quarter of an inch, no praise on the part of her teachers equalled the compliments this earned her from dressmaker and tailor. As for Inez, who had not only a pretty face but was graceful and slender-limbed as a greyhound, Inez no longer needed to worry over artificial charms, or to dwell self-consciously on her development; serious admirers were not lacking, and with one of these, a young man some eight years older than herself, she had had for the past three months a sort of understanding. For her, as for so many others, the time she had still to spend at school was as purgatory before paradise. To top all, one of the day-scholars in Laura’s class was actually engaged to be married; and in no boy-and-girl fashion, but to a doctor who lived and practised in Emerald Hill: he might sometimes be seen, from a peephole under the stairs, waiting to escort her home from school. This fiancée was looked up to by the class with tremendous reverence, as one set apart, oiled and anointed. You really could not treat her as a comrade﻿—her, who had reached the goal. For this *was* the goal; and the thoughts of all were fixed, with an intentness that varied only in degree, on the great consummation which, as planned in these young minds, should come to pass without fail directly the college-doors closed behind them. And here again Laura was a heretic. For she could not contemplate the future that was to be hers when she had finished her education, but with a feeling of awe: it was still so distant as to be one dense blue haze; it was so vast, that thinking of it took your breath away: there was room in it for the most wonderful miracles that had ever happened; it might contain anything﻿—from golden slippers to a Jacob’s ladder, by means of which you would scale the skies; and with these marvellous perhapses awaiting you, it was impossible to limit your hopes to one single event, which, though it saved you from derision, would put an end, forever, to all possible, exciting contingencies.
===Chinese===
当然，在这些事情上，劳拉并不是唯一的局外人；学院里星星点点还散布着另外几个这样的人，有的年纪也比她大，只因性子端凝，或者埋头功课，便置身事外。可她们淹没在大多数人里，而且碰巧，没有一个在劳拉的圈子里。中国眼除外——中国眼不算数。于是，劳拉半是着迷、半是发怵，重新燃起热情，着手研究起她的朋友们来。她们那身讨人欢心的本事，她不由得不佩服，尽管见她们公然为自己日渐长成的姿色得意洋洋，她又觉得有点儿难为情。比如伯莎——伯莎的头脑是她们所有人中间最灵秀的一个；可对于胳膊眼见得圆浑起来、胸脯弯出曲线，她又是多么坦白地心满意足。她带着一种敬畏对劳拉说起这件事；她的嗓音里仿佛隐隐透着一个即将降临的奥秘。蒂莉呢，活着就为了缩小腰围：她成天嘬着柠檬，消化不良的苦楚也忍了，鼻尖熬得发红也忍了；因为学校里的任何成就，在蒂莉心中的分量都及不上她又把腰身束细了四分之一英寸这件事，任何教师的夸奖，都抵不上这一成就替她从女裁缝和男裁缝嘴里换来的恭维。至于伊内兹——她不但有一张漂亮脸蛋，还像灵缇犬一样身姿轻盈、四肢修长——伊内兹已经不必再为人工的魅术操心，也不必忸忸怩怩地惦记自己的发育了；正经的倾慕者不乏其人，而且同其中一个比她大着八岁光景的青年，三个月来她已经有了某种默契。对她来说，也和对许多别的人一样，在学校还得熬过的那段时光，不过是天堂之前的炼狱。更了不得的是，劳拉班上一个走读生竟然已经订了婚；而且不是小孩子过家家，是许给了一位在绿宝石山（Emerald Hill）居住行医的医生：有时从楼梯底下的窥孔望出去，还能看见他等在那儿，预备护送她放学回家。全班怀着莫大的敬意仰望着这位未婚妻，视她为被分别出来的人，膏过圣油、受过祝圣的。你实在没法再把她当同伴看待——她，那个已经到达终点的人。因为这『就是』终点；所有人的心思都专注地钉在那上面，所差的只是专注的深浅：那场大团圆，照这些年轻头脑里的筹划，一等学院大门在他们身后哐当关上，就万无一失地要应验。而在这里，劳拉又是一个异端。因为一想到学业结束后等着她的那个未来，她便不能不心怀敬畏：它还那么遥远，远得只是浓重的一团蓝色雾霭；它那么浩大，想起来叫人屏住呼吸：里面装得下自古以来的最神奇的奇迹；它什么都可以容纳——从金拖鞋到一架雅各的天梯，顺着那梯子你就能登天；有这许多奇妙的『也许』在前面等着你，谁也不可能把希望拴死在单单一件事情上——那件事固然能使你免遭讪笑，却会永远终结掉一切可能的、叫人心跳的意外。

===Original===
These thoughts came and went. In the meantime, despite her apelike study of her companions, she remained where the other sex was concerned a disheartening failure. A further incident drove this home anew.

One Saturday afternoon, those boarders who had not been invited out were taken to see a cricket-match. They were a mere handful, eight or nine at most, and Miss Snodgrass alone was in charge. All her friends being away that day, Laura had to bring up the rear with the governess and one of the little girls. Though their walk led them through pleasant parks, she was glad when it was over; for she did not enjoy Miss Snodgrass’s company. She was no match for this crisply sarcastic governess, and had to be the whole time on her guard. For Miss Snodgrass was not only a great talker, but had also a very inquiring mind, and seemed always trying to ferret out just those things you did not care to tell﻿—such as the size of your home, or the social position you occupied in the township where you lived.

Arrived at the cricket ground, they climbed the Grand Stand and sat down in one of the back rows, to the rear of the other spectators. Before them sloped a steep bank of hats﻿—gaily-flowered and ribbon-banded hats﻿—of light and dark shoulders, of alert, boyish profiles and pale, pretty faces﻿—a representative gathering of young Australia, bathed in the brilliant March light.
===Chinese===
这些念头来了又去。与此同时，尽管她对同伴们做了猿猴似的观摩，在异性的问题上，她依然是个令人丧气的失败者。又出了一桩事，把这层道理重新敲进她心里。

一个星期六的下午，没被人邀请出去的寄宿生被带去看板球赛。总共不过一小撮，至多八九个，带队的只有斯诺德格拉斯小姐一个人。那天劳拉的朋友都不在，她只好同这位外聘女教师和一个小姑娘一起殿后。一路虽然穿过几座宜人的园林，她还是巴望早点走到头；因为斯诺德格拉斯小姐的陪伴实在无趣。她不是这位口角尖刻、字字带刺的外聘女教师的对手，只得时时刻刻提防着。因为斯诺德格拉斯小姐不但话多，还长着一副孜孜探问的头脑，仿佛总在设法掘出那些你正不愿说的事——比如你家房子有多大，你们在所住的镇子上是什么身份地位。

到了板球场，他们登上大看台，在后排拣了个位子坐下，处在其他观众的后面。面前一道陡坡下去，是密密层层一大片帽子——缀着艳丽花朵、扎着缎带的帽子——是深深浅浅的肩膀、机灵的男孩子气概的侧影和苍白秀丽的面孔——好一场年轻的澳大利亚的代表性聚会，沐浴在三月份明亮的阳光里。

===Original===
Laura’s seat was between her two companions, and it was here the malheur occurred. During an interval in the game, one of the girls asked the governess’s leave to speak to her cousin; and thereupon a shy lad was the target for twenty eyes. He was accompanied by a friend, who, in waiting, sat down just behind Laura. This boy was addressed by Miss Snodgrass; but he answered awkwardly, and after a pause, Laura felt herself nudged.

“You can speak to him, Laura,” whispered Miss Snodgrass. She evidently thought Laura waited only for permission, to burst in.

Laura had already fancied that the boy looked at her with interest. This was not improbable; for she had her best hat on, which made her eyes seem very dark﻿—“like sloes,” Chinky said, though neither of them had any clear idea what a sloe was.
===Chinese===
劳拉的座位在两位同伴中间，那桩「malheur」（法语『祸事』）就出在这儿。比赛中场歇着的当口，一个姑娘请求女教师允许她去跟表哥说话；于是一个腼腆的小伙子便成了二十只眼睛的靶子。他有个朋友陪着，那朋友等着的时候，就在劳拉身后坐下了。斯诺德格拉斯小姐同这男孩搭话；可他答得局促，稍过片刻，劳拉觉得自己被轻轻碰了一下。

「你可以跟他说话呀，劳拉。」斯诺德格拉斯小姐悄声说。她分明以为，劳拉只等一声许可，就要冲上去了。

劳拉早已暗自觉得，那男孩带着兴味望过她。这倒不是没有可能；因为她戴着自己最好的那顶帽子，衬得眼睛格外黑——「像两颗黑刺李」，中国眼说过，尽管她们俩谁也说不清黑刺李究竟是个什么东西。

===Original===
Still, a prompting to speech invariably tied her tongue. She half turned, and stole an uneasy peep at the lad. He might be a year older than herself; he had a frank, sunburnt face, blue eyes, and almost white flaxen hair. She took heart of grace.

“I s’pose you often come here?” she ventured at last.

“You bet!” said the boy; but kept his eyes where they were on the pitch.

“Cricket’s a lovely game﻿ ﻿… don’t you think so?”
===Chinese===
然而，一有人撺掇她开口，她的舌头就必定打结。她半转过身，心神不定地偷觑了那小伙子一眼。他大约比她大一岁；一张坦率的、晒黑了的脸，蓝眼睛，头发是近乎白色的亚麻色。她壮起了胆子。

「我想，你常到这儿来吧？」她终于大着胆子开了口。

「那还用说！」男孩说，眼睛却仍钉在球场上没动。

「板球是个多好的玩意儿……你不觉得吗？」

===Original===
Now he looked at her; but doubtfully, from the height of his fourteen male years; and did not reply.

“Do you play?”

This was a false move, she felt it at once. Her question seemed to offend him. “Should rather think I did!” he answered with a haughty air.

Weakly she hastened to retract her words. “Oh, I meant much﻿—if you played much?”
===Chinese===
这时他才看她；却带着疑虑，打着他十四岁男子汉年岁的高度看下来；没有答话。

「你打球吗？」

这是一步昏招，她立刻就觉出来了。她的问题像是冒犯了他。「我还能不打！」他带着一股傲气答道。

她软弱地赶紧往回找补。「哦，我是说多不多——常打吗？」

===Original===
“Comes to the same thing I guess,” said the boy﻿—he had not yet reached the age of obligatory politeness.

“It must be splendid”﻿—here she faltered﻿—“fun.”

But the boy’s thoughts had wandered: he was making signs to a friend down in the front of the Stand. Miss Snodgrass seemed to repress a smile.

Here, however, the little girl at Laura’s side chimed in. “I think cricket’s awful rot,” she announced, in a cheepy voice.
===Chinese===
「我看还不是一回事。」男孩说——他还没到非讲客气不可的年纪。

「那一定精彩极了」——说到这儿她迟疑了一下——「好玩极了。」

可男孩的心思已经游荡到别处去了：他正朝看台前排的一个朋友打手势。斯诺德格拉斯小姐像是忍住了一个微笑。

不过这时候，劳拉身边的小姑娘插了进来。「我看板球无聊透顶。」她用尖细的嗓音宣布。

===Original===
Now what was it, Laura asked herself, in these words, or in the tone in which they were said, that at once riveted the boy’s attention. For he laughed quite briskly as he asked: “What’s a kid like you know about it?”

“Jus’ as much as I want to. An’ my sister says so’s well.”

“Get along with you! Who’s your sister?”

“Ooh!﻿—wouldn’t you like to know? You’ve never seen her in Scots’ Church on Sundays I s’pose﻿—oh, no!”
===Chinese===
这些话里头，或者是说这些话的那副腔调里头，到底有什么东西，登时就把男孩的注意力铆住了——劳拉这样问自己。因为他立刻轻快地笑了，问：「你这么个小不点儿，懂个什么呀？」

「想懂多少懂多少。我姐姐也是这么说的。」

「去你的！你姐姐是谁呀？」

「哼！——你想知道吧？我看你准在苏格兰教堂（Scots’ Church）的礼拜日见过她——哦，没见过才怪呢！」

===Original===
“By jingo!﻿—I should say I have. An’ you, too. You’re the little sister of that daisy with the simply ripping hair.”

The little girl actually made a grimace at him, screwing up her nose. “Yes, you can be civil now, can’t you?”

“My aunt, but she’s a tip-topper﻿—your sister!”

“You go to Scots’ Church then, do you?” hazarded Laura, in an attempt to reenter the conversation.

“Think I could have seen her if I didn’t?” retorted the boy, in the tone of: “What a fool question!” He also seemed to have been on the point of adding: “Goose,” or “Sillybones.”
===Chinese===
「好家伙！——可不见过嘛。连你也见过。你就是那个头发漂亮得没治的小美人的妹妹呀。」

小姑娘竟朝他做了个鬼脸，把鼻子皱了起来。「哼，这会儿嘴倒甜了，是吧？」

「乖乖，她可真是个顶呱呱的人物——你姐姐！」

「那么你也上苏格兰教堂，是吗？」劳拉试着搭了一句，想重新挤进谈话。

「不去那儿我能见着她吗？」男孩顶了回来，那口气等于说：「多蠢的问题！」他似乎还差点就要补上一句『呆鹅』或者『傻骨头』。

===Original===
The little girl giggled. “She’s church”﻿—by which she meant episcopalian.

“Yes, but I don’t care a bit which I go to,” Laura hastened to explain, fearful lest she should be accounted a snob by this dissenter. The boy, however, was so faintly interested in her theological wobblings that, even as she spoke, he had risen from his seat; and the next moment without another word he went away. This time Miss Snodgrass laughed outright.

Laura stared, with blurred eyes, at the white-clad forms that began to dot the green again. Her lids smarted. She did not dare to put up her fingers to squeeze the gathering tears away, and just as she was wondering what she should do if one was inconsiderate enough to roll down her cheek, she heard a voice behind her.
===Chinese===
小姑娘咯咯笑了。「她是国教那边的」——她的意思是说，她是英国国教徒。

「是的，不过我上哪一座做礼拜都一点儿不在乎。」劳拉赶紧解释，生怕被这位不奉国教的小家伙看成势利眼。可是男孩对她在神学上的摇来摆去丝毫不感兴趣，不等她说完，他已经从座位上站了起来；下一刻，一句话没再说，他就走了。这一回，斯诺德格拉斯小姐索性笑出了声。

劳拉瞪着前方，眼睛已经模糊，望着一个个白衣身影重新在绿茵上点布开来。她的眼皮辣辣地疼。她不敢抬起手指去把涌上来的眼泪挤回去；正当她盘算着，万一有一颗不识好歹、当真滚下脸颊来可怎么办，忽听得背后有个声音。

===Original===
“I say, Laura﻿ ﻿… Laura!”﻿—and there was Chinky, in her best white hat.

“I’m sitting with my aunt just a few rows down; but I couldn’t make you look. Can I come in next to you for a minute?”

“If you like,” said Laura and, because she had to sniff a little, very coldly: Chinky had no doubt also been a witness of her failure.

The girl squeezed past and shared her seat. “I don’t take up much room.”
===Chinese===
「我说，劳拉……劳拉！」——中国眼来了，戴着顶最好的白帽子。

「我跟我姨妈坐在下面没几排；可我怎么也引不起你的注意。我能进来挨你坐一小会儿吗？」

「随你的便。」劳拉说，因为不得不轻轻吸了一下鼻子，话说得非常冷淡：中国眼无疑也目睹了她的失败。

那姑娘侧身挤了进来，同她合坐一个位子。「我不占多大地方的。」

===Original===
Laura feigned to be engrossed in the game. But presently she felt her bare wrist touched, and Chinky said in her ear: “What pretty hands you’ve got, Laura!”

She buried them in her dress, at this. She found it in the worst possible taste of Chinky to try to console her.

“Wouldn’t you like to wear a ring on one of them?”

“No, thanks,” said Laura, in the same repellent way.
===Chinese===
劳拉装出一副看比赛看得入神的样子。可是不多会儿，她觉出自己光裸的手腕被碰了一下，中国眼凑在她耳边说：「劳拉，你这双手多俊呀！」

听了这话，她把双手埋进了裙兜。中国眼竟想来安慰她，她觉得这真是俗气透顶。

「你不想在一只手上戴个戒指吗？」

「不，谢谢。」劳拉说，还是那副拒人千里的样子。

===Original===
“Truly? I’d love to give you one.”

“You? Where would *you* get it?”

“Would you wear it, if I did?”

“Let me see it first,” was Laura’s graceless reply, as she returned to her stony contemplation of the great sunlit expanse.

She was sure Miss Snodgrass, on getting home, would laugh with the other governesses over what had occurred﻿—if not with some of the girls. The story would leak out and come to Tilly’s ears; and Tilly would despise her more than she did already. So would all the rest. She was branded, as it was, for not having a single string to her bow. Now, it had become plain to her that she could never hope for one; for, when it came to holding a boy’s attention for five brief minutes, she could be put in the shade by a child of eight years old.
===Chinese===
「真的不想？我真爱送你一个呢。」

「你？『你』上哪儿弄去？」

「要是我真弄来了，你肯戴吗？」

「先拿来我看看。」劳拉没好气地回了一句，重新回到她那石头人似的凝望里，望着那大片阳光泼洒的开阔球场。

她拿得准，斯诺德格拉斯小姐一到家，就会同别的女教师笑谈这桩事——要不然，就是同哪几个姑娘。这件事会漏出去，传到蒂莉耳朵里；蒂莉会比现在更瞧不起她。其余所有人也一样。她如今本就顶着个烙印：弓上连一根『弦』都没有——没有一个追她的男孩子。到了这会儿，她已经看明白了，自己这一根也永远休想盼来；因为，论把一个小子的注意力抓住区区五分钟，她连个八岁的娃娃都赛不过，要被人比得黯然失色。

## XVI / 十六

===Original===

Since, however, it seemed that someone had to be loved if you were to be able to hold up your head with the rest, then it was easier, infinitely easier, to love the curate. With the curate, no personal contact was necessary﻿—and that was more than could be said even of the music-masters. In regard to them, pressures of the hand, as well as countless nothings, were expected and enacted, in the biweekly reports you rendered to those of your friends who followed the case. Whereas for the curate it was possible to simulate immense ardour, without needing either to humble your pride or call invention to your aid: the worship took place from afar. The curate was, moreover, no unworthy object; indeed he was quite attractive, in a lean, ascetic fashion, with his spiritual blue eyes, and the plain gold cross that dangled from his black watch-ribbon﻿—though, it must be admitted, when he preached, and grew greatly in earnest, his mouth had a way of opening as if it meant to swallow the church﻿—and Laura was by no means his sole admirer. Several of her friends had a fancy for him, especially as his wife, who was much older than he, was a thin, elderly lady with a tired face.

And now, by her own experience, Laura was led to the following discovery: that, if you imagine a thing with sufficient force, you can induce your imagining to become reality. By dint of pretending that it was so, she gradually worked herself up into an attack of love, which was genuine enough to make her redden when Mr. Shepherd was spoken of, and to enjoy being teased about him. And since, at any rate when in church, she was a sincerely religious little girl, and one to whom﻿—notwithstanding her protested indifference to forms of worship﻿—such emotional accessories as flowers, and music, and highly coloured vestments made a strong appeal, her feelings for Mr. Shepherd were soon mystically jumbled up with her piety: the eastward slant for the Creed, and the Salutation at the Sacred Name, seemed not alone homage due to the Deity, but also a kind of minor homage offered to and accepted by Mr. Shepherd; the school-pew being so near the chancel that it was not difficult to believe yourself the recipient of personal notice.

At home during the winter holidays, his name chanced to cross her lips. Straightway it occurred to Mother that he was the nephew of an old friend whom she had long lost sight of. Letters passed between Warrenega and Melbourne, and, shortly after her return to the College Laura learnt that she was to spend the coming monthly holiday at Mr. Shepherd’s house.

In the agitated frame of mind this threw her into, she did not know whether to be glad or sorry. Her feelings had, of late, got into such a rapt and pious muddle that it seemed a little like being asked out to meet God. On the other hand, she could not but see that the circumstance would raise her standing at school, immeasurably. And this it did. As soon as the first shock had passed she communicated the fact freely, and was shrewd enough not to relate how the invitation had come about, allowing it to be put down, as her friends were but too ready to do, to the effect produced on the minister by her silent adoration.

===Chinese===

话虽如此，既然看来非得有个人可爱，才能在同伴们中间抬得起头，那么，去爱副牧师（the curate）就容易多了——容易得没法比。爱副牧师，不必有任何亲身接触——这一点，就连音乐教师们也做不到。对音乐教师，握一握手啊，还有数不清的琐碎调笑啊，都在意料之中、照例上演，并且要一并写进你呈给那些追踪此案的朋友们的每两周一度的汇报里去。而副牧师呢，尽可以装出炽烈如火的爱慕，既不必折损骄傲，也用不着搬虚构来帮忙：这场礼拜是在远处进行的。何况副牧师本身也绝非不值一爱的对象；他确实颇有动人之处，是清瘦苦修的那一路：一双空灵的蓝眼睛，黑色表带上垂着一枚素金十字架——不过必须承认，他讲道讲得极其恳切的时候，那张嘴会越张越大，仿佛存心要把整座教堂吞下去——而且他也绝非只有劳拉一个爱慕者。她的好几个朋友都对他有意，尤其因为他那位比他大得多的太太，是个瘦瘦的、上了年纪的妇人，一脸倦容。

而这时，劳拉从自身的经验得出了下面这条发现：一件事，只要你想得足够用力，就能诱使你的想象变成现实。靠着一味假装果然如此，她渐渐把自己煽动出了一场爱情的发作，这场发作真切得很：谁一提起谢泼德先生（Mr. Shepherd），她就会红脸，别人拿他打趣，她也受用。何况，至少在教堂里的时候，她是个诚心信教的小姑娘，而且——尽管她口口声声对礼拜的种种形式无所谓——鲜花、音乐、色彩浓艳的法衣这一类煽情的点缀，对她都有莫大的吸引力，于是她对谢泼德先生的感情，很快便同她的虔诚神秘地搅在了一处：诵《信经》时朝东躬身、闻圣名时颔首致敬，这些仿佛不单是欠上帝的礼数，还是一种小小的敬礼，献给谢泼德先生、也由他领受了；学校的座席又紧挨着圣坛，要相信自己蒙受了人家个别垂顾，并不费事。

冬假在家，他的名字碰巧从她嘴边溜了出来。妈妈立刻想起，他是她一位久已断了音讯的老朋友的侄子。沃里内加和墨尔本之间通了几封信，返校后不久，劳拉便得知：即将到来的每月一度的假日，她要到谢泼德先生家去过。

这消息把她搅得心慌意乱，她不知道该欢喜还是该发愁。她的感情近来已经陷入一种狂热而虔敬的糊涂里，这一来倒有点像是受人邀请，出门去拜见上帝了。另一方面，她又不能不看到：这件事将把她在学校里的地位抬高，而且高得不可估量。事实也果真如此。最初那一阵震动一过，她便把这事儿大大方方地张扬出去，而且乖觉得不肯讲邀请是怎么来的，听任大家归因于她默默的倾慕在牧师身上产生的效力——她的朋友们本来巴不得这样归因。

===Original===

The Church girls were wild with envy. Laura was dragged up the garden with an arm thrust through each of hers. Mr. Shepherd’s holy calling and spiritual appearance stood him in small stead here; and the blackest interpretation was put on the matter of the visit.

“Nice things you’ll be up to, the pair of you﻿—oh, my aunt!” ejaculated Maria.

“I think it’s beastly risky her going at all,” filled in Kate Horner, gobbling a little; for her upper lip overhung the lower. “These saints are oftenest bad ’uns.”

“Yes, and with an Aunt Sally like that for a wife. Now look here, Kiddy, just you watch you’re not left alone with him in the dark.”

“And mind, you’ve got to tell us everything﻿—every blessed thing!”

===Chinese===

信英国国教的姑娘们羡慕得发了狂。劳拉被左右两边各伸出的一条胳膊挎住，一路拖上了花园。谢泼德先生的神圣职分和出尘仪表，到了这里全帮不上他的忙；对这次做客，大家作了最恶毒的解读。

「你们俩要干的好事——哎呀，我的老天！」玛丽亚脱口喊道。

「我看她这一去，简直是险得很，」凯特·霍纳补充道，说话有点咕哝含混，因为她的上唇包住了下唇。「这种圣徒，十有八九是坏东西。」

「可不是，再加上那么个木头婆子（Aunt Sally）似的老婆。听好了，小家伙，你千万当心，别在天黑的时候跟他单独待在一块儿。」

「还有，你可得什么都讲给我们听——一丝一毫都不许瞒！」

===Original===

Laura was called for, on Saturday morning, by the maiden sister of her divinity. Miss Isabella Shepherd was a fair, short, pleasant young woman, with a nervous, kindly smile, and a congenital inability to look you in the face when speaking to you; so that the impression she made was that of a perpetual friendliness, directed, however, not at you, but at the inanimate objects around you. Laura was so tickled by this peculiarity, which she spied the moment she entered the waiting-room, that at first she could take in nothing else. Afterwards, when the novelty had worn off, she subjected her companion to a closer scrutiny, and from the height of thirteen years had soon taxed her with being a frumpish old maid; the valiant but feeble efforts Miss Isabella made to entertain her, as they walked along, only strengthening her in this opinion.

Not very far from the College they entered a small, two-storied stone house, which but for an iron railing and a shrub or two gave right on the street.

“Will you come up to the study?” said Miss Isabella, smiling warmly, and ogling the doormat. “I’m sure Robby would like to see you at once.”

Robby? Her saint called Robby?﻿—Laura blushed.

But at the head of the stairs they were brought up short by Mrs. Shepherd, who, policeman-like, raised a warning hand.

===Chinese===

星期六早晨，来接劳拉的，是她那位心中神明的未嫁姐姐。伊莎贝拉·谢泼德小姐（Miss Isabella Shepherd）是个皮肤白皙、个子矮小、模样讨喜的年轻女人，带着又慌切又和气的笑，而且天生就不会在跟人说话时正眼看人；于是她给人的印象，是一种经久不歇的友好，只是这友好并非冲着你来，而是冲着你周身那些没有生命的东西去的。劳拉一进会客室就侦察出了这个特点，乐不可支，起初别的什么都不曾看进眼里。等到新鲜劲儿过去，她才把同伴细细打量一番，凭十三岁的高度，很快给她定了案：一个土里土气的老姑娘；一路走着，伊莎贝拉小姐为给她解闷所作的那些勇气可嘉而软弱无力的努力，只是叫她愈发这样认定。

离学院不远，她们走进一所两层的小石头房子；若不是隔着一道铁栏杆和一两丛灌木，这房子简直就直接临街。

「上楼到书房来坐坐好吗？」伊莎贝拉小姐说，笑容热切，眼睛却对着门前的擦鞋垫眉目传情。「我担保罗比（Robby）巴不得马上就见到你。」

罗比？她的圣徒居然叫罗比？——劳拉脸红了。

可是刚到楼梯顶，她们就被谢泼德太太（Mrs. Shepherd）拦住了，她像警察似的举起一只手来示警。

===Original===

“Hssh﻿ ﻿… ssh﻿ ﻿… sh!” she breathed, and simultaneously half-closed her eyes, as if imitating slumber. “Robby has just lain down for a few minutes. How are you, dear?”﻿—in a whisper. “I’m so pleased to see you.”

She looked even more faded than in church. But she was very kind, and in the bedroom insisted on getting out a clean towel for Laura.

“Now we’ll go down. It’s only lunch today, for Robby has a confirmation-class immediately afterwards, and doesn’t care to eat much.”

They descended to the dining-room, but though the meal was served, did not take their seats: they stood about, in a kind of anxious silence. This lasted for several minutes; then, heavy footsteps were heard trampling overhead: these persisted, but did not seem to advance, and at length there was a loud, impatient shout of: “Maisie!”

Both ladies were perceptibly flurried. “He can’t find something,” said Miss Isabella in a stage-whisper; while Mrs. Shepherd, taking the front of her dress in both hands, set out for the stairs with the short, clumsy jerks which, in a woman, pass for running.

A minute or two later the origin of the fluster came in, looking, it must be confessed, not much more amiable than his voice had been: he was extremely pale, too, his blue eyes had hollow rings round them, and there were tired wrinkles on his forehead. However, he offered Laura a friendly hand, which she took, with her soul in her eyes.

===Chinese===

「嘘……嘘……嘘！」她气声说，同时半阖上眼睛，仿佛扮演入睡。「罗比刚躺下歇几分钟。你好吗，亲爱的？」——用的是耳语。「见到你我真高兴。」

她看上去比在教堂里更显干瘦憔悴。不过她非常和气，还在卧室里执意要给劳拉取出一条干净毛巾。

「这就下楼去吧。今天只吃一顿午饭便饭，因为罗比饭后马上有个坚振班，不想多吃。」

她们下到饭厅，饭菜虽已摆好，人却没有入座：几个人站着，一片焦灼的沉默。这样过了几分钟；随后，楼上传来沉重的脚步声，咚咚踩着，却不见挪近；末了是一声又响又不耐烦的喊：「梅茜（Maisie）！」

两位女士都看得出慌了神。「他找不着什么东西了。」伊莎贝拉小姐用舞台耳语说；谢泼德太太则双手提起裙襟，迈着又短又笨的急碎步向楼梯进发——女人家这样，也就算是跑步了。

一两分钟后，这场慌乱的祸首走了进来——必须承认，他的神色并不比他的嗓音和善多少：他还苍白得很，蓝眼睛四周一圈黑晕，额头上刻着倦纹。不过他还是向劳拉友好地伸出手来，她握住了，一双眼睛把整个灵魂都捧了上去。

===Original===

“Well, and so this is the young lady fresh from the halls of learning, is it?” he asked, after a mumbled grace, as he carved a rather naked mutton-bone: the knife caught in the bone; he wrenched it free with an ill-natured tweak. “And what do they teach you at college, miss, eh?” he went on. “French?﻿ ﻿… Greek?﻿ ﻿… Latin? How goes it? *Infandum, Regina, jubes renovare dolorem*﻿—isn’t that the way of it? And then﻿ ﻿… let me see! It’s so long since I went to school, you know.”

“*Trojanas ut opes et lamentabile regnum eruerint Danai*,” said Laura, almost blind with pride and pleasure.

“Well, well, well!” he exclaimed, in what seemed tremendous surprise; but, even as she spoke, his thoughts were swept away; for he had taken up a mustard-pot and found it empty. “Yes, yes, here we are again! Not a scrap of mustard on the table.”﻿—His voice was angrily resigned.

“With *mutton*, Robby dear?” ventured Mrs. Shepherd, with the utmost humbleness.

“With mutton if I choose!” he retorted violently. “*Will* you, Maisie, be kind enough to allow me to know my own tastes best, and not dictate to me what I shall eat?”

But Mrs. Shepherd, murmuring: “Oh dear! it’s that dreadful girl,” had already made a timid spring at the bell.

===Chinese===

「哦——这么说，这就是刚从学问殿堂里出来的年轻小姐喽？」他含糊地咕哝完谢饭祷文后问道，手上正切一条相当光秃的羊腿骨：刀卡进了骨头；他没好气地一拧，把刀拧了下来。「学院里都教你们些什么呀，小姐，嗯？」他接着说。「法文？……希腊文？……拉丁文？学得怎么样啦？*Infandum, Regina, jubes renovare dolorem*（王后啊，你命我重诉说不尽的悲痛）——是不是这么说的？还有……让我想想！你知道，我离开学堂太久啦。」

「*Trojanas ut opes et lamentabile regnum eruerint Danai*（希腊人怎样倾覆了特洛伊的家业和那可哀的国度）。」劳拉说，几乎被骄傲和欢喜弄花了眼。

「哎呀，哎呀，哎呀！」他叫起来，似乎惊异无比；可是就在她说这话的当儿，他的心思已经被卷走了；原来他拿起芥末罐，发现是空的。「可不是嘛，又来了！桌上一星芥末都没有。」——他的声音愤怒里带着认命。

「配『羊肉』吗，亲爱的罗比？」谢泼德太太壮着胆子问，谦卑到了极处。

「我爱配就配！」他狠狠回嘴。「梅茜，『劳』您大驾，容我自己最清楚自己的口味，别来规定我该吃什么，行不行？」

可谢泼德太太早已一边嘀咕「哎呀！准是那个死丫头」，一边怯生生地一跃扑向了铃。

===Original===

“Poor Robby﻿ ﻿… so rushed again!” said Isabella in a reproachful tone.

“And while she’s here she may bring the water and the glasses as well,” snarled the master of the house, who had run a flaming eye over the table.

“Tch, tch, tch!” said Mrs. Shepherd, with so little spirit that Laura felt quite sorry for her.

“*Really*, Maisie!” said Miss Isabella. “And when the poor boy’s so rushed, too.”

This guerilla warfare continued throughout luncheon, and left Laura wondering why, considering the dearth of time, and the distress of the ladies at each fresh contretemps, they did not jump up and fetch the missing articles themselves﻿—as Mother would have done﻿—instead of each time ringing the bell and waiting for the appearance of the saucy, unwilling servant. As it turned out, however, their behaviour had a pedagogic basis. It seemed that they hoped, by constantly summoning the maid, to sharpen her memory. But Mrs. Shepherd was also implicated in the method; and this was the reason why Isabella﻿—as she afterwards explained to Laura﻿—never offered her a thimbleful of help.

“My sister-in-law is nothing of a manager,” she said. “But we still trust she will improve in time, if she always has her attention drawn to her forgetfulness﻿—at least Robby does; I’m afraid I have rather given her up. But Robby’s patience is angelic.” And Laura was of the same opinion, since the couple had been married for more than seven years.

===Chinese===

「可怜的罗比……又这么赶！」伊莎贝拉用嗔怪的口气说。

「她既然来了，干脆把凉水和杯子也一并带来吧。」这家里的主人咆哮道，他已用一只冒火的眼睛把桌子扫了一遍。

「啧，啧，啧！」谢泼德太太说，那声气有气无力得叫劳拉直替她难受。

「『真是的』，梅茜！」伊莎贝拉小姐说。「偏偏这可怜的孩子又忙成这样。」

这场游击战贯穿了整顿午饭，弄得劳拉直纳闷：时间这样紧，每出一桩新岔子女士们又那样惶急，她们为什么自己不跳起来把缺的东西取来——换了妈妈就会这样——而是每一回都打铃，干等那个没规矩、不情愿的女仆露面。不过后来才知道，她们的行为是有教育学根据的。她们似乎指望，靠不断地传唤女仆，磨利她的记性。但谢泼德太太本人也牵涉在这套办法之内；这便是为什么伊莎贝拉——她后来向劳拉解释过——从不肯伸手帮一丁点儿的忙。

「我这嫂子，一点儿不会当家。」她说。「不过我们仍然指望她会慢慢长进，只要她那健忘总有人给她点破——至少罗比还这么指望；我嘛，恐怕已经有点撒手不管了。罗比可是天使般的耐心。」劳拉对此深表同意，因为这一对夫妇结婚已经七年多了。

===Original===

The moment the meal, which lasted a quarter of an hour, was over, Mr. Shepherd clapped on his shovel-hat and started, with long strides, for his class, Mrs. Shepherd, who had not been quite ready, scuttling along a hundred yards behind him, with quick, fussy steps, and bonnet an awry.

Laura and Isabella stood at the gate.

“I ought really to have gone, too,” said Isabella, and smiled at the gutter. “But as you are here, Robby said I had better stay at home today. Now what would you like to do?”

This opened up a dazzling prospect, with the whole of Melbourne before one. But Laura was too polite to pretend anything but indifference.

“Well, perhaps you wouldn’t mind staying in then? I want so much to copy out Robby’s sermon. I always do it, you know, for he can’t read his own writing. But he won’t expect it today and he’ll be so pleased.”

It was a cool, quiet little house, with the slightly unused smell in the rooms that betokens a lack of children. Laura did not dislike the quiet, and sat contentedly in the front parlour till evening fell. Not, however, that she was really within hundreds of miles of Melbourne; for the wonderful book that she held on her knee was called *King Solomon’s Mines*, and her eyes never rose from the pages.

===Chinese===

这顿历时一刻钟的饭一完，谢泼德先生立刻把铲形帽（shovel-hat）往头上一扣，迈开大步赶他的班去了；还没拾掇利索的谢泼德太太，迈着又急又乱的碎步，帽子歪在一边，在他身后一百码开外一路小跑追赶。

劳拉和伊莎贝拉站在大门口。

「按说我也真该去的。」伊莎贝拉说，朝街边的阴沟笑了笑。「不过既然你在这儿，罗比就说我今天最好待在家里。现在，你想做点儿什么呀？」

这一下展开了一片耀眼的前景，整个墨尔本都任人挑选。可是劳拉太讲礼貌了，除了装出满不在乎，旁的什么也不会装。

「那么，你大概不介意就待在家里吧？我太想把罗比的讲道稿誊清出来了。你知道，一向都是我誊的，因为他认不出自己的笔迹。不过他今天想不到会有这一着，准会高兴得很。」

这是一所清凉、安静的小房子，各个房间里有一股淡淡的久不使用的气味，昭示着家里缺少孩子。劳拉并不讨厌这份安静，心满意足地坐在前客厅里，一直坐到暮色四合。不过，要说她当真身在墨尔本，那可差着几百英里呢；因为她摊在膝头的那本奇妙的书，名叫《所罗门王的宝藏》（King Solomon's Mines），她的眼睛再没有从书页上抬起来过。

===Original===

Supper, when it came, was as scrappy and as hurried as lunch had been: a class of workingmen was momently expected, and Robby had just time to gulp down a cup of tea. Nor could he converse; for he was obliged to spare his throat.

Afterwards the three of them sat listening to the loud talking overhead. This came down distinctly through the thin ceiling, and Mr. Shepherd’s voice﻿—it went on and on﻿—sounded, at such close quarters, both harsh and rasping. Mrs. Shepherd was mending a stole; Isabella stooped over the sermon, which she was writing like copperplate. Laura sat in a corner with her hands before her: she had finished her book, but her eyes were still visionary. When any of the three spoke, it was in a low tone.

Towards nine o’clock Mrs. Shepherd fetched a little saucepan, filled it with milk, and set it on the hob; and after this she hovered undecidedly between door and fireplace, like a distracted moth.

“Now do try to get it right tonight, Maisie,” admonished Isabella; and, turning her face, if not her glance, to Laura, she explained: “It must boil, but not have a scrap of skin on it, or Robby won’t look at it.”

Presently the workingmen were heard pounding down the stairs, and thereupon Maisie vanished from the room.

The next day Laura attended morning and evening service at St. Stephen’s-on-the-Hill, and in the afternoon made one of Isabella’s class at Sunday school.

===Chinese===

晚饭到时端上来，也和午饭一样零碎、一样匆忙：一班工人随时就要到了，罗比只来得及把一杯茶灌下肚。他也没法子交谈；因为他还得省着嗓子。

饭后，她们三个坐着，听楼上高声的讲话。声音透过薄薄的天花板清晰地落下来，谢泼德先生的嗓音——它讲啊讲啊——隔得这么近听来，又沙哑又刺耳。谢泼德太太在缝补一条圣带；伊莎贝拉俯身在讲道稿上，一笔一画誊得像铜版字。劳拉坐在角落里，双手摆在身前：书看完了，眼睛里还带着恍惚的憧憬。三人中不管谁开口，都是低声细气。

将近九点，谢泼德太太取来一只小奶锅，倒满牛奶，坐上炉架；此后她便举棋不定地在门口与壁炉之间飘来飘去，活像一只心慌意乱的飞蛾。

「今晚可千万弄妥帖了，梅茜。」伊莎贝拉叮嘱道；随即把脸——就算不是目光——转向劳拉，解释说：「必须煮开，又不能结一星奶皮，不然罗比瞧都不会瞧一眼的。」

不多时，听得工人们咚咚咚跑下楼来，梅茜随即从屋里消失了。

第二天，劳拉在山上圣斯蒂芬教堂做了早祷和晚祷，下午又编进伊莎贝拉的班里，上了一课主日学校。

===Original===

That morning she had wakened, in what seemed to be the middle of the night, to find Isabella dressing by the light of a single candle.

“Don’t you get up,” said the latter. “We’re all going to early service, and I just want to make Robby some bread and milk beforehand. He would rather communicate fasting, but he has to have something, for he doesn’t get home till dinnertime.”

When midday came, Robby was very fractious. The mutton-bone﻿—no cooking was done﻿—was harder than ever to carve with decency; and poor Mrs. Shepherd, for sheer fidgetiness, could hardly swallow a bite.

But at nine o’clock that evening, when the labours of the day were behind him, he was persuaded to lie down on the sofa and drink a glass of port. At his head sat Mrs. Shepherd, holding the wine and some biscuits; at his feet Isabella, stroking his soles. The stimulant revived him; he grew quite mellow, and presently, taking his wife’s hand, he held it in his﻿—and Laura felt sure that all his querulousness was forgiven him for the sake of this moment. Then, finding a willing listener in the black-eyed little girl who sat before him, he began to talk, to relate his travels, giving, in particular, a vivid account of some months he had once spent in Japan. Laura, who liked nothing better than travelling at second hand﻿—since any other way was out of the question﻿—Laura spent a delightful hour, and said so.

===Chinese===

那天清早，她在恍如半夜的时辰里醒来，发现伊莎贝拉正就着一支蜡烛的光穿衣裳。

「你别起来，」后者说。「我们全都要去早祷，我只是想先给罗比弄点儿牛奶泡面包。他本宁愿空着肚子领圣餐，可总得垫点儿东西，因为他要到午饭时分才回得了家。」

到了晌午，罗比烦躁得很。羊腿骨——家里不动烟火——比先前更难切得体面了；可怜的谢泼德太太，光是一个劲儿地干着急，几乎一口都咽不下去。

可是那天晚上九点钟，一天的劳碌都落在了身后，他被劝得躺在沙发上，喝了一杯波特酒。谢泼德太太坐在他头边，捧着酒和几块饼干；伊莎贝拉坐在他脚边，抚摩他的脚底。这剂兴奋剂让他还了魂；他变得相当温厚了，不多时，他握起太太的手，攥在自己手心里——劳拉觉得可以断定，他那一肚子的怨气，全凭这一刻得到了宽恕。随后，他发现面前这个黑眼睛的小姑娘是个现成肯听的听众，便打开话匣子，讲起他的游历来，尤其有声有色地描绘了他有一年在日本盘桓过的几个月。劳拉呢，顶喜欢这种借人家的眼睛去旅行——别的走法她想也不用想——劳拉过了一个钟头快活的日子，而且还照实说了。

===Original===

“Yes, Robby quite surpassed himself tonight, I thought,” said Isabella as she let down her hair. “I never heard anyone who could talk as well as he does when he likes. Can you keep a secret, Laura? We are sure, Maisie and I, that Robby will be a Bishop some day. And he means to be, himself. But don’t say a word about it; he won’t have it mentioned out of the house. And meanwhile he’s working as hard as he can, and we’re saving every penny, to let him take his next degree.”

“I do hope you’ll come again,” she said the following morning, as they walked back to the College. “I don’t mind telling you now, I felt quite nervous when Robby said we were to ask you. I’ve had no experience of little girls. But you haven’t been the least trouble﻿—not a bit. And I’m sure it was good for Robby having something young about the house. So mind you write and tell us when you have another holiday”﻿—and Isabella’s smile beamed out once more, none the less kindly because it was caught, on its way to Laura, by the gate they were passing through.

Laura, whose mind was set on a good, satisfying slab of cake, promised to do this, although her feelings had suffered so great a change that she was not sure whether she would keep her word. She was pulled two ways: on the one side was the remembrance of Mr. Shepherd hacking cantankerously at the bare mutton-bone; on the other, the cherry-blossom and the mousmés of Japan.

===Chinese===

「是啊，我看罗比今晚真是超水平发挥了。」伊莎贝拉一面解开头发一面说。「他高兴起来的时候讲得那样好，我从没听过谁赶得上他。你能保守秘密吗，劳拉？梅茜和我都拿准了，罗比总有一天要做主教。他自己也存心要做。可你一个字也别露；他不许在这宅子外头提这件事。眼下呢，他在拼命用功，我们也在一个便士一个便士地省，好让他去拿他的下一个学位。」

「我真盼着你再来，」第二天早晨回学院的路上，她说。「现在可以不瞒你了，罗比说我们要请你的时候，我心里还怪紧张的。小姑娘我从来没打过交道。可你一点儿没让人费心——半点儿也没有。而且我敢说，家里有个年轻些的，对罗比有好处。所以记着，下回再有假日，一定写信告诉我们。」——伊莎贝拉的微笑又一次焕发出来，依旧那么和气，只是在飞向劳拉的路上，被她们正走过的大门半途接了过去。

劳拉呢，心里正惦着一块好好的、解馋的大蛋糕，便应承了下来，尽管她的感情已经起了偌大的变化，连她自己都拿不准会不会守约。她被两头拉扯着：一头，是谢泼德先生冲那条光秃秃的羊腿骨恶狠狠乱剁的回忆；另一头，是日本的樱花和日本小姑娘（mousmés）。

## XVII / 十七



===Original===

> Ohnmacht zur Lüge ist lange noch nicht Liebe zur Wahrheit﻿ ﻿… Wer nicht lügen kann, weiss nicht, was Wahrheit ist.
> — Nietzsche

===Chinese===

> 无力说谎，远远还算不上热爱真理……不会说谎的人，就不知道什么是真理。
> ——尼采

===Original===

A pantomime of knowing smiles and interrogatory grimaces greeted her, when, having brushed the cake-crumbs from her mouth, she joined her class. For the twinkling of an eye Laura hesitated, being unprepared. Then, however, as little able as a comic actor to resist pandering to the taste of the public, she yielded to this hunger for spicy happenings, and did what was expected of her: clapped her hands, one over the other, to her breast, and cast her eyes heavenwards. Curiosity and anticipation reached a high pitch; while Laura, by tragically shaking her head, gave it to be understood that no signs could transmit what she had been through, since seeing her friends last.

In the thick of this message she was, unluckily, caught by Dr. Pughson, who, after dealing her one of his butcherly gibes, bade her to the blackboard, to grapple with the Seventh Proposition.

The remainder of the forenoon was a tussle with lessons not glanced at since Friday night. Besides, Laura seldom forestalled events by thinking over them, choosing rather to trust for inspiration to the spur of the moment.

===Chinese===

她把嘴角的蛋糕屑掸干净，回到班上时，迎接她的是一场由会意的微笑和探询的怪脸合演的哑剧。一眨眼的工夫，劳拉迟疑了一下，因为她毫无准备。然而，她到底跟滑稽戏子一样，抵挡不住迎合大众口味的诱惑，便顺从了这场对香艳逸事的如饥似渴，做了大家指望她做的事：双手一只压一只地按在胸前，眼珠向天上一翻。好奇与渴盼升到了沸点；劳拉则悲悲切切地摇着头，示意任何手势都传达不出自上次同朋友们分手以来她所经历的一切。

不巧，这番表情做到一半，她就被普夫森博士逮了个正着；博士赏了她一句他那种屠夫式的挖苦，便打发她到黑板跟前，去跟第七命题搏斗。

上午余下的工夫，是一场缠斗——同打星期五晚上起就没瞟过一眼的功课缠斗。再说，劳拉很少抢在事情前头预先思量，她宁可把灵感托付给临场的一激。

===Original===

Morning school at an end, she was laid hands on and hurried off to a retired corner of the garden. Here, four friends squatted round, determined to extract her adventures from her﻿—to the last pip.

Laura was in a pretty pickle. Did she tell the plain truth, state the pedestrian facts﻿—and this she would have been capable of doing with some address; for she had looked through her hosts with a perspicacity uncommon in a girl of her age; had once again put to good use those “sharp, unkind eyes” which Mother deplored. She had seen an overworked, underfed man, who nagged like any woman, and made slaves of two weak, adoring ladies; and she very well knew that, as often as her thoughts in future alighted on Mr. Robby, she would think of him pinching and screwing, with a hawk-like eye on a shadowy bishopric. Of her warm feelings for him, genuine or imaginary, not a speck remained. The first touch of reality had sunk them below her ken, just as a drop of cold water sinks the floating grounds in a coffeepot﻿ ﻿… But did she confess this, confess also that, save for a handful of monosyllables, her only exchange of words with him had been a line of Virgil; and, still more humbling, that she had liked his wife and sister better than himself: did this come to light, she would forfeit every sou of the prestige the visit had lent and yet promised to lend her. And, now that the possible moment for parting with this borrowed support had come, she recognised how greatly she had built on it.

These thoughts whizzed through her mind, as she darted a look at the four predatory faces that hemmed her in. Tilly’s was one of them: the lightly mocking smile sat on it that Laura had come to know so well, since her maladroit handling of Bob. She would kill that smile﻿—and if she had to die for it herself.

Still, she must be cautious, wary in picking her steps. Especially as she had not the ghost of an idea how to begin.

===Chinese===

上午的课一收场，她就被人下了手，急急匆匆架到花园里一个僻静的角落。四个朋友围蹲成一圈，铁了心要把她的奇遇从她嘴里榨出来——榨到最后一颗果核。

劳拉的处境很是为难。倘若她端出干巴巴的实情，摆出那些平淡无奇的事实——这她本来是能办得挺利索的；因为她早已把这一家主人看穿，那份眼力，在她这般年纪的女孩身上实属罕见；妈妈为之痛心的那双『尖刻、不厚道的眼睛』，她又好好地派了一回用场。她看见的是一个操劳过度、吃不饱饭的男人，唠叨起来赛过随便哪个女人，还把两个软弱而崇拜他的女人当奴隶使；她心里也十分明白，往后她的念头每落到罗比先生身上一次，她就会想起他怎样一文一文地抠、一分一分地攒，鹰隼似的眼睛盯住一个影影绰绰的主教之位。她对他那一片热乎乎的情意——无论是真起的还是假想的——已经一星半点也不剩了。现实的初次一碰，便把它们沉到了她的视界之下，正像一滴冷水把咖啡壶里漂浮的渣滓沉下去一样……可她若是把这些招认出来，还得连带招认：除开寥寥几个单音节词，她与他之间唯一的一次言语往来，就是一行维吉尔（Virgil）的诗；更叫她下不来台的是，他的太太和妹妹，她喜欢得比他本人还多些：这些一旦见了光，这趟拜访已经借给她、并且还指望再借给她的那份威望，她就得一个铜板不剩地输个精光。而如今，同这根借来的支柱分手的时机当真到了，她才认清，自己在这上头倚仗得有多深。

这些念头在她脑子里嗖嗖掠过，同时她飞快地扫了一眼把她围在当中的那四张掠食者似的脸。蒂莉的是其中之一：那上面挂着一丝轻嘲的笑——自从她在鲍勃跟前失手以来，劳拉对这笑早已熟透了。她要把那个笑容杀掉——哪怕得自己拿命去换。

不过，她还是得谨慎，拣着脚步下脚。何况该怎么开头，她连一点影儿也没有。

===Original===

Meanwhile cries of impatience buzzed round her.

“She doesn’t want to tell.”

“Mean brute!”

“Shouldn’t wonder if it’s too dashed shady.”

“Didn’t I *say* he was a bad ’un?”

“I bet you there’s nothing to tell,” said Tilly cockily, and turned up her nose.

===Chinese===

这工夫，不耐烦的叫嚷在她四下里嗡嗡直响。

「她不想讲。」

「小气畜生！」

「只怕那事儿见不得人得很，我才不奇怪呢。」

「我不是*就说过*嘛，他不是个好东西！」

「我敢打赌，根本就没什么好讲的，」蒂莉气昂昂地说，鼻子翘上了天。

===Original===

“Yes, there is,” flung out Laura, at once put on the defensive, and as she spoke she coloured.

“Look at her! Look how red she’s got!”

“And after she promised﻿—the sneak!”

“I’m not a sneak. I *am* going to tell. But you’re all in such a blooming hurry.”

“Oh, fire away, slowcoach!”

===Chinese===

「有，当然有，」劳拉冲口顶了回去，一下子被逼上了守势，开口间脸就红了。

「瞧她！瞧她脸红成什么样儿了！」

「还答应过要讲的呢——滑头鬼！」

「我才不是滑头鬼。我*是*要讲的。可你们一个个急得跟什么似的。」

「嗐，快开腔吧，磨蹭鬼！」

===Original===

“Well, girls,” began Laura gamely, breathing a little hard. “But, mind, you must never utter a word of what I’m going to tell you. It’s a dead secret, and *if* you let on﻿—”

“S’ help me God!”

“Ananias and Sapphira!”

“Oh, *do* hurry up.”

“Well﻿ ﻿… well, he’s just the most﻿—oh, I don’t know how to say it, girls﻿—the *most*﻿—”

===Chinese===

「好吧，姑娘们，」劳拉壮起胆子开了腔，呼吸微微有点急。「可你们记住，我接下来讲的话，你们一个字也不许往外吐。这是天大的秘密，*要是*你们走漏了风声——」

「上帝作证！」

「亚拿尼亚和撒非喇！」

「噢，*千万*快点儿吧。」

「嗯……嗯，他简直就是顶——噢，我都不知道怎么说，姑娘们——*顶*——」

===Original===

“Just scrumptious, I suppose, eh?”

“Just positively scrumptious, and﻿ ﻿…”

“And what’d he do?”

“And what about his old sketch of a wife?”

“Her? Oh”﻿—and Laura squeezed herself desperately for the details that *would* not come﻿—“oh, why she’s just a perfect old﻿ ﻿… old cat. And twenty years older than him.”

===Chinese===

「就是『妙得不得了』呗，对不对？」

「就是实实在在、彻头彻尾的『妙得不得了』，而且……」

「那他都干了些什么呀？」

「那他那位老婆呢，那个干瘦得只剩个影儿的老东西？」

「她？噢」——劳拉拼命绞着自己的脑汁，那些细节偏偏*不肯*冒出来——「噢，她呀，就是个十足的老……老恶猫。还比他大二十岁呢。」

===Original===

“What on earth did he marry her for?”

“Guess he’s pretty sick of being tied to an old gin like that?”

“I should say! Perfectly *miserable*. He can’t think now why he let himself be induced to marry her. He just despises her.”

“Well, why in the name of all that’s holy did he take her?”

Laura cast a mysterious glance round, and lowered her voice. “Well, you see, she had *lots* of money and he had none. He was ever so poor. And she paid for him to be a clergyman.”

===Chinese===

「他到底图什么要娶她？」

「我猜呀，跟这么个老土著婆子拴在一块儿，他早腻烦透了吧？」

「可不是嘛！简直*惨*透了。他如今都想不明白，自己当年怎么会由着人劝，娶了她。他简直就是打心眼里瞧不起她。」

「那到底，看在一切神圣事物的份上，他为什么把她娶了？」

劳拉神秘地朝四下里扫了一眼，压低了嗓音。「是这样的，你们瞧，她有*大把大把*的钱，他一个子儿也没有。他穷得不得了。还是她掏钱供他当上牧师的。」

===Original===

“Go on! As poor as all that?”

“As poor as a church-mouse. But, oh,” she hastened to add, at the visible cooling-off of the four faces, “he comes of a *most* distinguished family. His father was a lord or a baronet or something like that, but he married a beautiful girl who hadn’t a penny against his father’s will and so he cut him out of his will.”

“I say!”

“Oh, never mind the father.”

“Yes. Well, now he feels under an awful obligation to her, and all that sort of thing, you know.”

===Chinese===

「不会吧！穷到那份儿上？」

「穷得跟教堂里的老鼠一个样。不过，噢，」眼见四张脸明显地冷了下来，她赶忙添上一句，「他可是出身*顶*显贵的人家。他父亲是位勋爵还是从男爵来着，反正那一类；只是他违逆父意，娶了一个身无分文的漂亮姑娘，他父亲便把他从遗嘱里除了名。」

「哎呀！」

「嗐，别管他父亲了。」

「是啊。他呢，如今觉得欠了她天大的人情，还有诸如此类的，你们懂的。」

===Original===

“And she drives it home, I bet. She looks a nipper.”

“Is always throwing it in his face.”

“What a ghoul!”

“He’d do just *anything* to get rid of her, but﻿—Girls, it’s a dead secret; you must swear you won’t tell.”

===Chinese===

「我敢打赌，她还天天拿这个往他心窝里戳。瞧她那样儿，就是个掐人精。」

「成天把这个摔在他脸上。」

「真是个妖婆！」

「为了甩脱她，他什么都*干*得出来，可是——姑娘们，这是天大的秘密；你们得发誓不讲出去。」

===Original===

Gestures of assurance were showered on her.

“Well, he’s to be a Bishop some day. It’s promised him.”

“Holy Moses!”

“And I suppose he can’t divorce her, because of that?”

“No, of course not. He’ll have to drag her with him like millstone round his neck.”

===Chinese===

保证的手势雨点般浇到她身上。

「是这样的：他将来是要当主教的。人家已经应许给他了。」

「我的摩西爷！」

「这么说，就为这个，他才不能跟她离婚？」

「不能，当然不能。他只好把她当磨盘挂在脖子上，走到哪儿拖到哪儿。」

===Original===

“And he’d twigged right enough you were gone on him?”

Laura’s coy smile hinted many things. “I should say so. Since the very first day in church. He said﻿—but I don’t like to tell you what he said.”

“You must!”

“No. You’ll only call me conceited.”

“No fear, Kiddy. Out with it!”

===Chinese===

「那他早就看得分明，你迷他迷得不行咯？」

劳拉含羞的一笑里藏着千般暗示。「可不是嘛。打头一回到教堂里便是如此。他说过——可我不愿意告诉你们他说了什么。」

「你必须讲！」

「不嘛。你们只会说我自高自大。」

「怕什么，小家伙。倒出来吧！」

===Original===

“Well, then, he said he saw me as soon as he got in the pulpit, and he wondered ever so much who the girl was with the eyes like sloes, and the skin like﻿ ﻿… like cream.”

“Snakes-alive-oh! He went it strong.”

“And how often were you alone with him?”

“Yes, and if he had met me before he was married﻿—but no, I can’t tell any more.”

“Oh, don’t be such an ass!”

“No, I can’t. Well, I’ll whisper it then﻿ ﻿… but only to Maria,” and leaning over Laura put her lips to Maria’s ear.

===Chinese===

「那好吧，他说他一上讲坛就瞧见了我，还纳闷得不得了，那个眼睛黑亮得像野李子、皮肤像……像奶油的姑娘是谁。」

「嗬哟，活见蛇了！他这话可说得够肉麻。」

「那你跟他单独在一块儿有多少回？」

「是啊，他要是结婚前就遇见了我——可是不行，不能再讲了。」

「嗐，别这么犯傻！」

「不行，讲不得。那这样，我咬着耳朵讲……只讲给玛丽亚一个人。」说着，劳拉俯下身去，把嘴唇贴到玛丽亚耳朵上。

===Original===

The reason for this by-stroke she could not have told: the detail she imparted did not differ substantially from those that had gone before. But by now she was at the end of her tether.

Here, fortunately for Laura, the dinner-bell rang, and the girls had to take to their heels in order to get their books put away before grace. Throughout the meal, from their scattered seats, they exchanged looks of understanding, and their cheeks were pink.

In the afternoon, Laura was again called on to prove her mettle. Her companion on the daily walk was Kate Horner. Kate had been one of the four, and did not lose this chance of beating up fresh particulars.

===Chinese===

这一记旁敲侧击为的什么，她自己都说不上来：她透露的细节，同先前那些并无实质的两样。只是到这时，她已是山穷水尽。

到这儿，托老天爷的福，午饭铃响了，姑娘们只得撒腿飞奔，好赶在谢恩祷告之前把书归置停当。一顿饭的工夫，她们从各自散坐的位子上交换着心照不宣的眼色，一个个脸颊绯红。

下午，劳拉又一次被叫上去显一显身手。每日例行散步时与她做伴的是凯特·霍纳。凯特正是方才四人中的一个，自然不肯放过这个再搜罗些新鲜细节的机会。

===Original===

After those first few awkward moments, however, which had come well-nigh being a fiasco, Laura had no more trouble with her story. Indeed, the plunge once taken, it was astounding how easy it became to make up things about the Shepherds; the difficulty was, to know where to stop. Fictitious details crowded thick and fast upon her﻿—a regular hotchpotch; she had only to stretch out her hand and seize what she needed. It was simpler than the five-times multiplication-table, and did not need to be learnt. But all the same she was not idle: she polished away at her flimflams, bringing them nearer and nearer probability, never, thanks to her sound memory, contradicting herself or making a slip, and always able to begin again from the beginning.

Such initial scepticism as may have lurked in her hearers was soon got the better of. For, crass realists though these young colonials were, and bluntly as they faced facts, they were none the less just as hungry for romance as the most insatiable novel-reader. Romance in any guise was hailed by them, and swallowed uncritically, though it was no more permitted to interfere with the practical conduct of their lives than it is in the case of just that novel-reader, who puts untruth and unreality from him, when he lays his book aside. Another and weightier reason was, their slower brains could not conceive the possibility of such extraordinarily detailed lying as that to which Laura now subjected them. Its very elaboration stood for its truth.

===Chinese===

不过，熬过开头那几阵险些砸锅的尴尬时刻之后，劳拉讲她的故事就再无难处了。真的，头一跳一旦跳了下去，编派谢泼德一家的事情竟容易得叫人吃惊；难的是知道在哪里收手。虚构的细节又急又密地朝她蜂拥而来——十足一个大杂烩；她只消伸出手去，抓住用得着的就行。这比五的乘法口诀还简单，还用不着背。可尽管如此她也没闲着：她一个劲儿打磨自己的鬼话，叫它们一寸一寸贴近可信；多亏记性好，她从不自相矛盾，也不露马脚，而且随时都能从头再讲一遍。

听众心里纵然还潜藏着一星将信将疑，也很快便被降服了。因为这些年轻的殖民地姑娘虽是彻头彻尾的现实主义者，面对事实又是那样直硬，可她们对传奇的饥渴，丝毫不亚于最贪得无厌的小说读者。传奇无论披着哪件外衣，她们一概欢呼迎接，囫囵吞下，不加辨析——只是它也同样不被允许干预她们生活的实际经营，恰如那位小说读者，书一放下，便把虚妄与不真实推到一旁。另一个分量更重的理由是：她们脑子转得慢，根本设想不出世上竟有劳拉此刻加诸她们这般细密的谎。谎编得越繁复，便越担保它的真实。

===Original===

And the days passed, and Laura had the happiest ideas. A strange thing about them was that they came to her quite unsought, dropping on her like Aladdin’s oranges on his turban. All she had to do was to fit them into their niche in her fabrication.

At first, her tale had been chiefly concerned with the internal rift in Mr. Shepherd’s home-life, and only in a minor degree with herself. But her public savoured the love-story most, and hence, consulting its taste, as it is the tale-maker’s bounden duty to do, Laura was obliged to develop this side of her narrative at the expense of the other. And the more the girls heard, the more they wished to hear. She had early turned Miss Isabella into a staunch ally of her own, in the dissension she had introduced into the curate’s household; and one day she arrived at a hasty kiss, stolen in the vestry after evening service, while Mr. Shepherd was taking off his surplice. The puzzle had been, to get herself into the vestry; but, once there, she saw what followed as if it had actually happened. She saw Mr. Shepherd’s arm slipped with diffident alacrity round her waist, and her own virtuous recoil; saw Maisie and Isabella waiting, sheep-like, in their pew, till it should please the couple to emerge; saw the form of the verger moving about the darkening church, as he put the lights out, one by one.

===Chinese===

日子一天天过去，劳拉有的是最痛快的点子。这些点子的一桩妙处是：它们全然不请自来，像阿拉丁（Aladdin）的橘子落上他的头巾一样落到她头上。她要做的，只是把它们安进她那座谎话大厦里各自的壁龛。

起初，她的故事主要讲谢泼德先生家庭生活的内部裂痕，讲她自己的份量还只占个小头。可她的听众最爱咀嚼的是爱情故事，于是，迎合听众口味——这本是编故事人的本分——劳拉便只得发展叙事的这一面，牺牲那一面。姑娘们听得越多，想听的也就越多。她早早就把伊莎贝拉小姐写成自己的铁杆同盟，拉进她挑进副牧师家中的那场失和，站到她这一边；到后来某一天，她终于讲到了一个匆促的吻，是晚祷后在法衣室里偷的——趁谢泼德先生解白法衣的当口。难题原本在于怎样把她自己弄进法衣室；可一旦进去了，后来的情形她便看得历历如真。她看见谢泼德先生的一条胳膊带着羞怯的急切滑上她的腰肢，又看见她自己贞洁的一缩；看见梅茜和伊莎贝拉像两只羊似的等在她们的家庭席位上，恭候这一对儿尽兴出来；看见教堂司事的身影在渐暗下来的教堂里走动，把灯一盏一盏熄掉。

===Original===

But the success this incident brought her turned Laura’s head, making her so foolhardy in her inventions that Maria, who for all her boldness of speech was at heart a prude like the rest, grew uneasy.

“You’re not to go to that house again, Kiddy. If you do, I’ll peach to old Gurley.”

Laura ran upstairs to dress for tea, taking two steps at a time. On the top landing, beside the great clothes-baskets, she collided with Chinky, who was coming primly down.

===Chinese===

但这件事给她带来的成功冲昏了劳拉的头脑，令她的编造愈发胆大妄为，以致玛丽亚——她嘴上尽管放肆，骨子里跟旁人一样是个道学姑娘——也渐渐不安起来。

「那家人家里你可不许再去了，小家伙。你要再去，我就到老格利太太跟前告你的状。」

劳拉跑上楼去换衣裳预备用茶点，楼梯两级一跨。在顶层楼梯口、大洗衣筐旁边，她跟正一本正经走下来的中国眼撞了个满怀。

===Original===

“O ki, John!” she greeted her, being in a vast good-humour. “What do you look so black for?”

“Dunno. Why do you never walk with me nowadays, Laura? I say, you know about that ring? You haven’t forgotten?”

“Course not. When am I to get it? It never turns up.” Her eyes glittered as she asked, for she foresaw a further link in her chain. “Soon, now?”

Chinky nodded mysteriously. “Pretty soon. And you promise faithfully never to take it off?”

“But it must be a *nice* one﻿ ﻿… with a red stone in it. And listen, Chink, no one must ever know it was you who gave it me.”

“All right, I swear. You’re a darling to say you’ll wear it,” and putting her arm round Laura’s shoulders, Chinky gave her a hearty kiss.

===Chinese===

「哦开，约翰！」她打招呼道，正自兴头十足。「你拉着张脸做什么呀？」

「不为什么。如今你怎么从来不跟我一块儿走了，劳拉？我说，戒指那事儿你知道吧？没忘吧？」

「当然没忘。可我什么时候能到手里呀？总也不见个影儿。」问这话时她眼睛闪闪发亮，因为她预见到她的锁链上又要添出新的一环。「就快了吗？」

中国眼神秘地点点头。「就快了。那你郑重答应我，永远不摘下来？」

「可那必须是一枚*漂亮*的……上头要镶颗红石头。还有，听着，中国眼，谁也永远不许知道是你给我的。」

「好，我发誓。你肯说愿意戴它，真是个小亲亲。」说着，中国眼伸出胳膊搂住劳拉的肩膀，热热地亲了她一下。

===Original===

This was more than Laura had bargained for;﻿—she freed herself, ungraciously. “Oh, don’t!﻿—now mind, a red stone, and for the third finger of the left hand.”

“Yes. And Laura, I’ve thought of something to put inside. *Semper eadem*﻿ ﻿… do you like that, Laura?”

“It’ll do. Look out, there’s old Day!” and leaving Chinky standing, she ran down the corridor to her room.

===Chinese===

这份热络可超出了劳拉原先的盘算；——她不客气地挣脱了身子。「嗐，别这样！——记着，红石头，要戴左手无名指。」

「好。还有，劳拉，我想好了要刻在里头的话。*Semper eadem*（拉丁文：始终如一）……你喜欢吗，劳拉？」

「行。留神，老戴来了！」她把中国眼撂在原地，顺着走廊一路跑回自己屋里去了。

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===

> Der Verbrecher ist haüfig genug seiner Tat nicht gewachsen.
> — Nietzsche

===Chinese===

> 罪犯往往担当不起自己的罪行。
> ——尼采

===Original===

For a month or more, Laura fed like a honeybee on the sweets of success. And throve﻿—even to the blindest eye. What had hitherto been lacking was now hers: the admiration and applause of her circle. And never was a child so spurred and uplifted by praise as Laura. Without it, her nature tended to be wary and unproductive; and those in touch with her, had they wished to make the most of her, would no more have stinted with the necessary incentive, that one stints a delicate rose tree in aids to growth. Laura could swallow praise in large doses, without becoming over-sure. Under the present stimulus she sat top in a couple of classes, grew slightly ruddier in face, and much less shrinking in manner.

“Call her back at once and make her shut that door,” cried Miss Day thickly, from behind one of the long, dining-hall tables, on which were ranged stacks and piles of clean linen. She had been on early duty since six o’clock.

The pupil-teacher in attendance stepped obediently into the passage; and Laura returned.

===Chinese===

有一个月光景，劳拉像只蜜蜂似的，饱餐着成功的甜头。人也发旺了——连最瞎的眼睛都看得出来。她一向欠缺的东西如今到手了：她那个圈子里的倾慕与喝彩。天底下从来没有一个孩子，像劳拉这样被夸奖激励着、托举着。没有夸奖，她的天性便趋于设防、趋于无所出产；与她相处的人，倘若想让她把本事尽数使出来，决不会吝惜那份必不可少的激励，正如谁也不会舍不得给一株娇贵的玫瑰添扶助。劳拉能大剂大剂地吞下赞美，而不至于自负过头。眼下的这剂兴奋，让她在一两门功课里坐上了头名，脸色红润了些，举止也不那么缩手缩脚了。

「马上把她叫回来，叫她把那扇门关上！」戴小姐隔着饭厅一张长桌声音含混地喊道，桌上码着一摞摞、一堆堆洗好的衬单布巾。她从六点钟起就值早班了。

当值的见习教师（pupil-teacher）乖乖走进过道去；劳拉回来了。

===Original===

“Doors are made to be shut, Laura Rambotham, I’d have you remember that!” fumed Miss Day in the same indistinct voice: she was in the grip of a heavy cold, which had not been improved by the draughts of the hall.

“I’m sorry, Miss Day. I thought I had. I was a little late.”

“That’s your own lookout,” barked the governess. “Oh, there you are at last, Miss Snodgrass. I’d begun to think you weren’t going to appear at all this morning. It’s close on a quarter past seven.”

“Sorry,” said Miss Snodgrass laconically. “My watch must be losing. Well, I suppose I can begin by marking Laura Rambotham down late. What on earth are you standing there holding the door for?”

“Miss Day knows﻿—I don’t,” sauced Laura, and made her escape.

===Chinese===

「门造出来就是给人关的，劳拉·拉姆博瑟姆，这一点你给我记住！」戴小姐用同样含混的嗓音冒火道：她正被一场重伤风缠着，饭厅里的过堂风又没让伤风见好。

「对不起，戴小姐。我还当自己关上了。我稍微迟了一会儿。」

「那是你自己的事。」女教师厉声喝道。「哦，你总算来了，斯诺德格拉斯小姐。我都当你今早索性不打算露面了。这都快七点一刻啦。」

「抱歉。」斯诺德格拉斯小姐言简意赅地说。「我的表准是慢了。好吧，我想我不妨拿劳拉·拉姆博瑟姆开个头，先记她一笔迟到。你到底站在那儿抓着门做什么？」

「戴小姐知道——我可不知道。」劳拉没大没小地回了一句，一溜烟脱身跑了。

===Original===

She did not let Miss Snodgrass’s bad mark disturb her. No sooner had she begun her practising than she fell to work again on the theme that occupied all her leisure moments, and was threatening to assume the bulk of an early Victorian novel. But she now built at her top-heavy edifice for her own enjoyment; and the usual fate of the robust liar had overtaken her: she was beginning to believe in her own lies. Still she never ventured to relax her critical alertness, her careful surveillance of detail. For, just a day or two before, she had seen a quick flare-up of incredulity light Tilly’s face, and oddly enough this had happened when she tried her audience with a fact, a simple little fact, an incident that had really occurred. She had killed the doubt, instantly, by smothering it with a fiction; but she could not forget that it had existed. It has very perplexing; for otherwise her hearers did not shy at a mortal thing; she could drive them where and how she chose.

At the present moment she was planning a great coup: nothing more or less than a frustrated attempt on her virtue. It was almost ready to be submitted to them﻿—for she had read *Pamela* with heartfelt interest during the holidays﻿—and only a few connecting links were missing, with which to complete her own case.

Then, without the slightest warning, the blow fell.

===Chinese===

她没让斯诺德格拉斯小姐记下的这一笔扰乱自己。琴刚练上手，她就又埋头干起那个占尽她闲暇时刻的主题——它眼看要胀成一部维多利亚时代早期小说的体量了。不过如今她营造她那座头重脚轻的大厦，是为了自己受用；撒谎撒得皮实的家伙命中注定的下场，也落到她头上了：她开始相信起自己的谎来。但她始终不敢松懈那分批判的警觉、那分对细节的仔细督查。因为就在一两天前，她亲眼看见一星疑火的苗头倏地窜上蒂莉的脸，而蹊跷的是，这偏偏发生在她拿一件事实、一件简简单单的小事实、一桩真正发生过的小事去试探听众的时候。她当即用一个虚构，把那点疑心当场闷死了；可它存在过，这她忘不掉。这事真叫人挠头；因为除此之外，她的听众对再要命的东西也不曾打个怯；她想把他们往哪儿赶、怎么赶，就能往哪儿赶。

眼下她正筹划着一桩大手笔：不多不少，正是一次被人挫败的、对她贞操的强暴。它差不多已经成形，可以呈交听众了——假期里她曾怀着满心的兴味读过《帕梅拉》（Pamela）——只欠几个衔接的环节，好把她这桩案子办齐全。

随后，没有半点先兆，打击落了下来。

===Original===

It was a Sunday afternoon; the half-hour that preceded Sunday school. Laura, in company with several others, was in the garden, getting her Bible chapter by heart, when Maria called her.

“Laura! Come here. I want to tell you something.”

Laura approached, her lips in busy motion. “What’s up?”

“I say, chicken, your nose is going to be put out of joint.”

“Mine? What do you mean?” queried Laura, and had a faint sense of impending disaster.

===Chinese===

那是个星期天下午；主日学校开课前的半个钟头。劳拉正同另外几个姑娘在园子里背她该背的《圣经》章节，玛丽亚喊她了。

「劳拉！过来。我有话要跟你说。」

劳拉走过去，嘴唇还在一开一合地忙着。「什么事？」

「我说，小鸡仔，你的鼻子要给人挤歪啦。」

「我的鼻子？」劳拉问，「你这是什么意思？」心里隐隐觉出一场大祸正在逼近。

===Original===

“What I say. M. Pidwall’s asked to the you-know-who’s next Saturday.”

“No, she’s not!” cried Laura vehemently, and clapped her Bible to.

“S’help me God, she is,” asserted Maria. “Look out, don’t set the place on fire.”

“How do you know?﻿ ﻿… who told you?”

“M. P. herself﻿—Gosh, but you are a jealous little cub. Oh, go on, Kiddy, don’t take it like that. I guess he won’t give you away.”﻿—For Laura was as pale as a moment before she had been scarlet.

===Chinese===

「就是我说的意思。皮德沃尔受邀去那个你晓得是谁的家，就下个星期六。」

「不，她才不去呢！」劳拉激烈地喊道，啪地把《圣经》合上了。

「老天作证，她就是要去。」玛丽亚一口咬定。「留神，别把这地方点着了。」

「你怎么知道的？……谁告诉你的？」

「M. P. 亲口说的——天哪，你这家伙，可真是个爱吃醋的小崽子。嗐，别这样嘛，小家伙，别往心里去。我琢磨着，他总不会出卖你的。」——因为劳拉的脸一阵白，跟一分钟前的通红恰成对照。

===Original===

Alleging a violent headache, she mounted to her room, and sat down on her bed. She felt stunned, and it took her some time to recover her wits. Sitting on the extreme edge of the bedstead, she stared at the objects in the room without seeing them. “M. P.’s going there on Saturday﻿ ﻿… M. P.’s going there on Saturday,” she repeated stupidly, and, with her hands pressed on her hips, rocked herself to and fro, after the fashion of an older woman in pain.

The fact was too appalling to be faced; her mind postponed it. Instead, she saw the fifty-five at Sunday school﻿—where they were at this minute﻿—drawn up in a line round the walls of the dining-hall. She saw them rise to wail out the hymn; saw Mr. Strachey on his chair in the middle of the floor, perpetually nimming with his left leg. And, as she pictured the familiar scene to herself, she shivered with a sudden sense of isolation: behind each well-known face lurked a possible enemy.

===Chinese===

她推说头剧烈地疼，上楼回到自己屋里，在床上坐下。她只觉得懵了，好一阵子才缓过神来。她坐在床架的最外沿上，瞪着屋里的种种物件，却视而不见。「M. P. 星期六要去那儿……M. P. 星期六要去那儿。」她呆头呆脑地念叨着，两手按在腰胯上，身子一前一后地摇，活像个犯了痛病、上了年纪的女人。

这事实太骇人，当面承受不了；她的心思把它往后推。她想的是：那五十五个人此刻正在主日学校里，贴着饭厅四壁站成一圈。她看见她们起身哀声唱圣歌；看见斯特雷奇先生坐在场子中央的椅子上，左腿没个完地小偷小摸似的挪动。她把这熟悉的场面在心里描画着，忽然打了个寒噤，生出一种孤绝之感：每一张熟脸背后，都潜伏着一个可能的敌人。

===Original===

If it had only not been M. P.!﻿—that was the first thought that crystallised. Anyone else!﻿ ﻿… from any of the rest she might have hoped for some mercy. But Mary Pidwall was one of those people﻿—there were plenty such﻿—before whom a nature like Laura’s was inclined, at the best of times, to shrink away, keenly aware of its own paltriness and ineffectualness. Mary was rectitude in person: and it cannot be denied that, to Laura, this was synonymous with hard, narrow, ungracious. Not quite a prig, though: there was fun in Mary, and life in her; but it was neither fun nor vivacity of a kind that Laura could feel at ease with. Such capers as the elder girl cut were only skin-deep; they were on the surface of her character, had no real roots in her: just as the pieces of music she played on the piano were accidents of the moment, without deeper significance. To Mary, life was already serious, full of duties. She knew just what she wanted, too, where she wanted to go and how to get there; her plans were cut and dried. She was clever, very industrious, the head of several of her classes. Nor was she ever in conflict with the authorities: she moved among the rules of the school as safely as an egg-dancer among his eggs. For the simple reasons that temptations seemed to pass her by. There was, besides, a kind of manly exactness in her habit of thinking and speaking; and it was this trait her companions tried to symbolise, in calling her by the initial letters of her name.

She and Laura, though classmates, had never drawn together. It is true, Mary was sixteen, and, at that time of life, a couple of years dig a wide breach. But there was also another reason. Once, in the innocence of her heart, Laura had let the cat out of the bag that an uncle of hers lived in the upcountry township to which Mary belonged.

===Chinese===

唯独不要是 M. P.！——这是头一个凝成形的念头。谁都行！……换了其余随便哪一个，她都还指望讨到几分怜悯。可玛丽·皮德沃尔（Mary Pidwall）偏偏是那样一种人——这种人世上多的是——在这样的人面前，劳拉这样的天性，就算在最顺当的日子里，也总归要畏缩退避，痛切地觉出自己的卑琐与不中用。玛丽就是正直的化身：无可否认，在劳拉眼里，这跟生硬、狭隘、不近人情是同义词。倒也不完全是个道学姑娘：玛丽身上有趣味，也有生气；只是那趣味与活泼，都不是劳拉能安然相处的那一种。这位年长姑娘耍出的种种顽皮把戏，只浮在皮面上；摆在她性情的表层，在她身上并没有扎下真正的根：正像她在钢琴上弹的那些曲子，全是一时兴到的偶然，没有更深的意义。对玛丽来说，生活已经是一本正经的事，处处是本分。她想要什么，自己也一清二楚：想去哪里，怎样去到；她的计划早裁好晒干、一成不变了。她聪明，非常勤勉，好几门功课都是头名。她也从不跟校方起冲突：她在学校的种种规章之间走动，安稳得像走蛋艺人走在他的鸡蛋中间。原因很简单：种种诱惑似乎都跟她擦身而过。此外，她思考和说话的习惯里，还有一种男子汉式的严整精确；同伴们连称呼都取她姓名的首字母 M. P.，正是想点出她这分特质。

她跟劳拉虽是同班，却从没亲近过。不错，玛丽十六岁了，在人生那个年纪，两年的差距便掘出一道宽宽的鸿沟。可另外还有一层缘故。有一回，劳拉一时心地天真，说漏了嘴，露出她有位叔叔，就住在玛丽所属的那座内地小镇上。

===Original===

The girl had eyed her coldly, incredulously. “What? That dreadful man your uncle?” she had exclaimed: she herself was the daughter of a church dignitary. “I should say I did know him﻿—by reputation at least. And it’s quite enough, thank you.”

Now Laura had understood that Uncle Tom﻿—he needed but a pair of gold earrings to pose as the model for a Spanish Grandee﻿—that Uncle Tom *was* odd, in this way: he sometimes took more to drink than was good for him; but she had never suspected him of being “dreadful,” or a byword in Wantabadgery. Colouring to the roots of her hair, she murmured something about him of course not being recognised by the rest of the family; but M. P., she was sure, had never looked on her with the same eyes again.

Such was the rigid young moralist into whose hands her fate was given.

===Chinese===

当时那位姑娘冷冷地、将信将疑地打量她。「什么？那个可怕的男人是你叔叔？」她嚷了出来：她自己可是位教会要人的千金。「我当然认识他——至少名声总是听过的。光这个也就够受的了，多谢关照。」

劳拉那时也明白，汤姆叔（Uncle Tom）——他只差一对金耳环，就能站出来给一位西班牙大公充当模特儿了——汤姆叔确实古怪，古怪在这里：他有时贪起杯来，喝得过了于己有益的分寸；可她从没疑心过他是「可怕」的，或是旺塔巴德里（Wantabadgery）一地的话柄。她红到了发根，含含糊糊嘀咕了几句，说家里其余的人当然不认他这门亲；可她拿得准，打那以后，M. P. 再没用同样的眼光看过她。

她的命运，就这样交到了这位古板年轻的卫道士手里。

===Original===

She sat and meditated these things, in spiritless fashion. She would have to confess to her fabrications﻿—that was plain. M. P.’s precise mind would bring back a precise account of how matters stood in the Shepherd household: not by an iota would the truth be swerved from. Why, oh why, had she not foreseen this possibility? What evil spirit had prompted her and led her on?﻿—But, before her brain could contemplate the awful necessity of rising and branding herself as a liar, it sought desperately for a means of escape. For a wink, she even nursed the idea of dragging in a sham man, under the pretence that Mr. Shepherd had been but a blind, used by her to screen someone else. But this yarn, twist it as she might, would not pass muster. Against it was the mass of her accumulated detail.

She sat there, devising scheme after scheme. Not one of them would do.

===Chinese===

她蔫蔫地坐着，把这些事翻来覆去地想。她只好把自己编造的那些东西招认出来——这是明摆着的。M. P. 那颗精确的头脑，会把谢泼德家的实情一分不差地带回来：真相一丝一毫也歪不了。为什么，哦为什么，她没预见到这个可能？是什么恶鬼撺掇了她、又牵着她一路走到这儿？——可是，她脑子还没来得及逼视那件可怕的事——站起来，亲手给自己烙上撒谎者的烙印——就拼死拼活找起脱身的路子来。有一眨眼的工夫，她甚至抱着这样的主意：拖出一个假想的男人，佯称谢泼德先生不过是个幌子，是她拿来遮掩另外什么人的。可这套话，随她怎么拧，也过不了关。跟它作对的，是她日积月累堆下的那一大堆细节。

她坐在那里，计策一条接一条地想。没有一条行得通。

===Original===

When, at teatime, she rose to wash her face before going downstairs, the sole point on which she had come to clearness was, that just seven days lay between her and detection. Yet after all, she reminded herself, seven days made a week, and a week was a good long time. Perhaps something would happen between now and Saturday. M. P. might have an accident and break her leg, and not be able to go. Or thin, poorly-fed Mr. Shepherd fall ill from overwork. Oh, how she would rejoice to hear of it!

And, if the worst came to the worst and she *had* to tell, at least it should not be today. Today was Sunday; and people’s thoughts were frightfully at liberty. Tomorrow they would be engaged again; and, by tomorrow, she herself would have grown more accustomed to the idea. Besides, how foolish to have been in too great a hurry, should something come to pass that rendered confession needless.
